## INTRODUCTION

### Position and role of the National Development Strategy 2020

The  National  Development  Strategy  2020  (NDS)  is  a  part  of  the  new  national  development management  system,  the  foundation  of  which  are  determined  by  the  amended  law  of  6 December 2006 on the principles of development policy (Polish Journal of Laws [Dz.U.] of 2009 No  84,  item  712,  as  amended)  and  by  the  document  on  Poland's  Development  Management System, adopted by the Council of Ministers on 27 April 2009.

Due  to  the  need  to  adjust  the  National  Development  Strategy  2007-2015,  adopted  on  29 November  2006,  to  the  new  socio-economic  conditions  and  to  the  internal  and  external challenges, as well as to the requirements of the introduced development policy management system,  it  was  decided  to  update  it  and  to  extend  its  time  horizon  until  2020.  The  efforts  to update  the  strategy  were  correlated  with  the  work  on  other  strategic  documents  under development,  such  as  the  Long-term  National  Development  Strategy  and  the  Integrated Strategies.

The main strategic documents of the new system, on the basis of which the development policy is implemented, include: Long-Term National Development Strategy - LTNDS (Poland 2030 - Third Wave  of  Modernity)  which  defines  major  trends  and  challenges  as  well  as  the  concept  of development of the country in the long-term, Medium-Term National Development Strategy MTNDS (National  Development  Strategy  2020)  which  is  the  most  important  document  in  the medium term, setting out strategic objectives for the development of the country until 2020, and key  in  determining  the  development  activities,  including  those  that  can  potentially  be  funded under the UE financial perspective 2014-2020 and under 9 Integrated Strategies whose aim is to assist  in  achieving  the  development  objectives:  Strategy  for  Innovation  and  Efficiency  of  the Economy,  Human  Capital  Development  Strategy,  Transport  Development  Strategy,  Energy Security  and  the  Environment,  Efficient  State,  Social  Capital  Development  Strategy,  National Strategy of Regional Development  2010-2020.  Regions,  cities,  rural areas , Strategy for Development  of  the  National  Security  System,  Strategy  for  Sustainable  Development  of  Rural Areas, Agriculture and Fisheries. LTNDS, MTNDS and the 9 Integrated Strategies are joined by a coherent hierarchy of objectives and directions for intervention. The Integrated Strategies depart from  a  narrow  sectoral  approach,  instead  they  focus  on  the  integration  of  areas  and  on  the permeability of various  phenomena and processes. The National Strategy of Regional Development, which indicates the extent of the territorial impact of interventions implemented under various public policies, and therefore also under the remaining Integrated Strategies, has a particular  role  in  the  entire  system.  It  sets  out  the  key  regional  development  challenges and outlines  the  development  objectives  in  various  areas,  taking  into  account  the  functions  such objectives perform as well as the existing potentials and barriers.

The  concept  of  spatial  development  of  the  country  (National  Spatial  Development  Concept 2030) 1 represents  the  spatial  framework  for  the  development  policy  in  Poland,  including  the implementation  of  various  development  strategies.  It  is  the  main  strategic  document  for  the creation of spatial order in Poland, which also addresses the issues related to the development, where the space is treated as a plane of reference for development activities.

> 1 National Spatial Development Concept 2030, adopted by the Council of Ministers on 13 December 2011

The work on national strategic documents coincided with the elaboration of the development objectives for the EU in its primary document - EU strategy for smart, sustainable and inclusive growth -  Europe  2020  (Europe  2020  strategy),  in  particular  with  drafting  the  National  Reform Programme  (NRP)  for  the  implementation  of  Europe  2020.  The  National  Reform  Programme, which  is  an  important  part  of  the  national  system  of  strategic  documents,  determines  the manner of implementation of the actions set out in these policy documents so that they fit in the priorities of the common actions across the EU. The annual update of NRP, provided under the so called European Semester, makes it possible to flexibly respond to the changing conditions of the implementation of priorities set out in the MTNDS.

On the one hand, national documents transfer the European challenges into national ground, and on the other, they enable active participation in the formulation of strategic objectives of the EU through the identification of national potentials and barriers. The development objectives and priorities indicated in the national documents are, to a significant extent, a part of the European objectives, and are consistent with them.

The diagnostic basis for drafting the national development strategies is the report 'Poland 2030. Development Challenges' 2 , presenting the challenges and recommendations in the key developmental areas of the functioning of the state.

MTNDS, the main strategy for development in the medium term, while accounting for the key challenges included in LTNDS, points the strategic objectives of the country, undertaking of which is  crucial in the perspective of the next few years to strengthen the processes of development (along with an estimate of the necessary funds).

MTDS  presents  the  development  scenario  resulting,  among  others,  from  an  analysis  of  the barriers and risks as well as of the existing potentials, and of the ability to finance the operations designed.

The National Development Strategy 2020 is based on a sustainable development scenario. The success in realizing all the objectives set for in this Strategy will depend on many factors, both internal  and  external,  which  may  affect  the  availability  of  funds  for  its  implementation.  The development  of  the  situation  in  the  global  economy,  especially  in  the  euro  area,  shall  be  of particular importance. The experience of financial and economic crisis, which broke out in 2008 and affected mainly the developed countries, including the EU countries, has shown that due to the openness of the economies of individual countries and to their growing interdependence, the scope  and  scale  of  impact  of  emerging  phenomena  and  crisis  processes  on  the  economies  of individual countries is significant and may require verification of ambitious development plans. In the  case  of  Poland,  what  will  have  the  most  significant  impact  on  the  implementation  of  the National Development Strategy 2020 is the situation in individual EU countries and the stability of the euro area.

> 2 Poland 2011 Report. Economy, Society, Region, adopted by the Council of Ministers on 2 November 2011.

6

[[END OF PAGE 6]]

[[START OF PAGE 7]]

The amount of EU funds which Poland may expect to obtain under the 2014-2020 EU financial perspective  is  yet  unknown.  The  EU  funds  may  be  an  important  source  of  funding  for development activities, but will still require involvement of the national public funds.

In the coming years, it will be crucial to reconcile the need to balance the public finances and to increase  savings,  while  pursuing  the  development  based  on  removing  major  barriers  in  its expansion, but also the growth increasingly based on education, digitization and innovation.

It  is  particularly  important  to  introduce  systemic,  competence  and  institutional  changes, promoting the release of development potentials, reserves and resources.

MTNDS sets outs the strategic areas where the main activities shall focus, and it defines the kinds of interventions necessary in the medium-term  perspective  in order to accelerate the development  processes.  The  MTNDS  is  thus  a  reference  to  a  new  generation  of  strategic documents  prepared  in  Poland  for  the  programming  of  EU  funds  for  2014-2020.  This  is  even more important since the programming system was significantly modified in the programming document proposed in the model of legal regulations for the EU cohesion policy for the years 2014-2020;  the  model  contains  no  document  such  as  the  National  Development  Plan  or  the National Strategic Reference Framework. So it is the MTNDS that creates the essential framework for the structural intervention to be undertaken in Poland within the various European policies in the next programming period 2014-2020. MTNDS reinforces the importance of cohesion policy as an instrument of national objectives, while maintaining the solidarity of Europe.

The  medium-term strategy  identifies  actions  to  be  taken,  involving  the  removal  of  barriers  to development, of specific "bottlenecks", including weakness of the Polish economy revealed by the  economic  crisis,  while  focusing  on  the  socio-economic  and  spatial  potentials  which  may stimulate the growth if properly reinforced.

The MTNDS makes the choice and it focuses on factors enabling the achievement of strategic objectives. At the same time, it shows consequence of developmental processes in various areas and their  interdependencies.  The  use  of  these  relationships  and  of  the  existing  feedbacks  will enhance  the  effects  of  actions  in  the  multiannual  perspective.  On  the  other  hand,  a  delay  in implementation of activities in one area may prevent the realization of the objectives set up in other related strategic areas. Therefore, public intervention should make use of and strengthen the feedback loops as a source of added value, consisting in impact on many interrelated areas in the long run.

The MTNDS accounts for the results of assessment of development activities taken so far and for recommendations for development policy, contained in the document Poland 2011 Economy Society - Regions, drafted by the Ministry of Regional Development. 3

The  MTNDS  is  not  only  directed  to  public  administration.  It  integrates  around  the  strategic objectives all of the public entities as well as economic and social circles involved in development and  which  may  support  it,  both  at  central  and  regional  levels.  MTNDS  indicates  the  reforms necessary  to  limit  or  eliminate  the  barriers  to  socio-economic  development,  the  indicative timetable for their implementation and the way of funding the projected activities (it includes a long-term framework plan of public expenditure on development activities).

> 3 Poland 2011 Report. Economy, Society, Region, adopted by the Council of Ministers on 2 November 2011

7

[[END OF PAGE 7]]

[[START OF PAGE 8]]

The Strategy draws attention to the territorial dimension of actions taken and to the need to strengthen and use more effectively the regional potentials. Therefore, it sees the importance of local  government  and  of  other  entities  in  enhancing  the  development  of  regions  and  of  the country.

Until 2020, the strategic emphasis will be placed primarily on strengthening the potentials which would ensure the future long-term development, and not only on the direct funds allocation in the areas of major deficits. Thus, the MTNDS does not develop all the areas relevant to the very fact of the functioning of the state, but it focuses mainly on those areas where actions should be taken to strengthen and accelerate the development process (including the necessary structural changes) over the next ten years.

The MTNDS is the base for the 9 Integrated Strategies which should contribute to the realization of  the  objectives  set  out  in  the  MTNDS,  develop  the  actions  designed  therein,  and  detail  the reforms indicated in MTNDS. The role of Integrated Strategies shall be to specify the directions of the activity and to provide the instruments for implementing the strategic tasks determined for the state.

### The conditions and development trends for the period until 2020

The  development  of  the  country  is  a  complex  process,  affected  by  numerous  internal  and external  factors.  Formulation  of  the  development  policy  requires  an  in-depth  analysis  and  the willingness  and  courage  to  actively  and  effectively  minimize  the  negative  impact  of  adverse trends.

The international conditions are subject to constant dynamic transformations, including in the dimension of global economy, which is one of the biggest challenges for long- and medium-term development of Poland, as well as for its current interests, commitments and actions. Periodic tensions  and  disturbances,  both  in  the  countries  playing  the  leading  role  in  the  world  and  in smaller  scale  economies,  that  shape  global  and  European  economic  situation,  are  a  particular determinant in this context. The scope and strength of their impact on Poland is significant and may be higher, given the increasing  openness of  the  Polish economy. The world  crisis  related phenomena and processes in particular restrict the conditions and limit the dynamics of Polish exports,  which  in  turn  affects  the  growth  of  the  country's  economy.  At  the  same  time,  the temporary financial problems substantially restrict the inflow of foreign capital to Poland and the growth funding opportunities.

It should be noted that Polish membership in the EU - together with the stability of the internal economy  structures,  including  the  strength  and  vitality  of  Polish  companies  -  contributes  to strengthening the resilience to these shocks and crises in foreign markets. Anticipating, however, the economic growth, both - appropriate strategic options and prudential rules in economic and financial policy - should be taken into account.

A  condition  necessary  for  the  country  to  develop,  and  thus  the  direction  of  state  activity,  is strengthening the position and role in the international community, including national security. Poland has already reached a high level of international security, but the phenomena that may pose a threat to this status (natural disasters, cyber attacks, terrorism, and regional outbreaks of tension)  cannot  be  ruled  out.  Therefore,  it  is  necessary  to  constantly  develop  the  national security  system,  allowing  Poland  to  respond  effectively  to  the  contemporary  challenges  and potential threats to its security and other vital interests.

8

[[END OF PAGE 8]]

[[START OF PAGE 9]]

Polish international position is on the one hand closely related to the development potential of the  country,  and  on  the  other,  it  determines  the  possibilities  of  implementing  the  national development strategy, including the specific interests and projects.

The period until 2020 is going to be a period of intense exploration of development models for socio-economic and political systems, of models that would take into account the experience of the economic crisis and the global challenges related to the need of expanding the innovation potential of the economy and of providing a stable basis for growth. It will be necessary to raise the awareness of the smart, sustainable and inclusive growth stimulation, in accordance with the Europe 2020 strategy. At the same time, the meaning of the non-state actors with global reach (for example network online communities, multinational corporations) would increase.

The growing economic and political importance of developing countries shall change the world balance  of  power,  which  in  turn  will  affect  the  global  system  of  market  regulations  and  of management of hazards and risks arising from these global challenges. The most important risks include: financial - associated with the instability of financial markets, environmental - associated with  environmental  degradation  and  consequent  adverse  natural  phenomena, energetic -  the very small supply of energy (global) and the impact it may have on the economic development of countries, food - related to the instability of markets and to the growing demand for agricultural products, technical -  associated  with  the  adverse  effects  of  civilization  leading  to  technical disasters, epidemiological - related to the possibility of a pandemic of an unknown aetiology.

The  growing  global  demand  for  products,  services  and  capital  goods  in  the  context  of  these threats  and  risks  will  stimulate  the  search  for  new  technologies  and  ways  of  increasing  the efficiency of production activity, and will simultaneously lead to the creation of new consumption patterns  and  lifestyles.  Consequently,  the  main  mechanism  driving  the  restructuring  and development of global economy will be the widely understood innovation, both technological and  organizational  or  social.  Management  theorists  postulate  the  transition  from  business models of enterprises based on the principle of profit maximization into models based on the principle  of  the  divided  value,  for  which  the  source  of  value  and  development  is  to  balance competition with cooperation, find synergies and optimally use the distributed potentials, which becomes possible through the use of new communication techniques.

Polish  economy  is  an  integral  part  of  the  global  economic  system,  therefore  one  of  the  key factors influencing the economic development of the country is the global economic situation. The  dynamics  of  Polish  economy  and  its  dependence  on  the  cycles  and  trends  of  the  world system result from the direct economic ties, from the conditions on world markets, and from the consequences  of  participation  in  the  EU  common  market  and  in  multilateral  free  trade agreements. As an entity in international trade relations, Poland is also a link in the global supply chain, and therefore is not only subject to the principles of cooperation in international trade, but also has an influence on the shape of those principles.

The  accession  to  the  European  Communities  has  a  significant  impact  on  the  image  of contemporary Poland. The consequences of integration are visible in virtually all areas of social and economic life. There is no doubt that membership in the European Union will continue to be a factor significantly influencing the pace and direction of development of the country.

The  competitiveness  of  Polish  economy  is  still  low.  Its  backwardness  in  relation  to  the  most economically advanced countries results mainly from its structure, based largely on traditional manufacturing and services, as well as from low levels of capital accumulation and of the use of human capital. The current economic growth relies heavily on the use of the 'underdevelopment benefit' (economic benefit resulting from the omission of certain stages of development). Polish economic potential  could  not  be  fully  exploited  due  to  the  relatively  low  rates  of  savings  and investment, the low employment rate and the insufficient productivity growth rate.

9

[[END OF PAGE 9]]

[[START OF PAGE 10]]

Meanwhile, the participation of Poland in international economic relations results in a situation where to act effectively in a competitive environment one must constantly develop potential and build advantages. Competition forces the entire national economy and the entities that create it to improve the management efficiency. The existing competitive advantages, based primarily on low labour costs, lose their significance. It is necessary to develop new strengths. As a result, the companies  using  older,  less  efficient  business  models  and  production  technologies  are  in  the need for extensive modernization and should base their action on an overall innovation, that is on  finding  and  implementing  organizational,  process,  technology,  products  and  marketing innovation.

Along  with  adapting  new  technologies,  a  progressive  reorientation  of  Polish  exports  towards products of a higher technological level shall take place. The competitiveness of Polish export will primarily  be  created  in  industries  using  medium  and  high  technology.  Competitive  advantage should be increasingly based on high innovation, knowledge and creativity, on effects of scale and, increasingly, on cooperative relationships. In cooperation within the cluster structures, also small- and medium-sized enterprises can create products able to compete in the global market regional, national and EU special commodities that may successfully compete with the traditional areas of  comparative  advantages  of  countries, including the BRIC countries.  Favourable transformations  in  the  structure  of  trade  will  be  accompanied  by  continued  importance  of exports in agri-food sector, based largely on food produced using integrated, organic, traditional and regional methods and local raw materials.

Business and professional openness, willingness to cooperate and to enter in joint investment projects,  namely  the  characteristics  conditioned  by  the  quality  of  social  capital,  promote economic development. The essence of social capital is its ability to network the resources, to integrate the activities and to develop voluntary cooperation in various spheres of life; for that reason social capital is one of the basic factors determining the productivity and innovation of the country. Due to the specific historical conditions, the social capital that is being developed in Poland usually takes the form of closed social networks, operating in isolation from institutions and from each other, often aimed at survival and adaptation rather than at development and innovation. The condition to convert the patterns of survival and adaptation to the pattern of development is to increase the level of social capital, based on open social networks, including the cooperation and communication chains of various communities, citizens and institutions.

The increasing consumer  demand  of  the  society shall increasingly affect  the  economic development. The growing wealth and higher material aspirations of the Poles will translate into a desire to achieve the standard of living comparable to that in the "old Union". The pursuit of cultural  models  and  lifestyles  of  the  affluent  societies  will  be  reflected  in  the  structure  of demand.  As  a  result,  the  production  of  consumer  goods,  trade  and  services  shall  develop. Therefore, the activities for effective implementation of the principles of sustainable production and  consumption,  particularly  shaping  the  pro-environmental  attitudes  of  consumers  and promoting a healthy lifestyle, shall be important. The economic importance of small and medium enterprises, capable of more flexible operation on the market and able to present individualized, unique offers, shall also increase.

Chances  and  opportunities  for  modernization  and  development  of  long-term  competitive position of the country depend not only on the enterprises adaptability, but also on many other key factors of an internal nature: demographic trends, quality of human capital, access to basic energy  resources,  environmental  situation,  prospects  for  development  of  science,  engineering and technology, territorial cohesion and the quality of spatial order, condition of infrastructure, funding  possibilities  for  socio-economic  development,  determination  of  the  authorities  and partner  cooperation  in  reforming  the  country,  culture  promoting  values  and  social  practices enhancing development.

10

[[END OF PAGE 10]]

[[START OF PAGE 11]]

The  demographic  situation  shapes  the  important  determinants  of  economic  development: potential consumer demand and potential supply of labour. The labour market will undergo a further  transformation,  not  only  because  of  the  aging  of  the  Polish  population  and  the deteriorating labour force structure. Demographic processes will be accompanied by migratory pressures resulting from the globalization trends and from the opening, after the Polish accession to the European Union, of many attractive foreign labour markets, and by increasing domestic demand for jobs after the economic upturn. Consequently, legislative changes will be required, aimed at lengthening the working life and reducing the system capacity for early retirement. The problems resulting  from  too  large  share  of  workers  in  low-productive  sectors  of  the  economy (including agriculture), as well as from the low economic activity of the Poles and from the low employment rate, especially of women, will require solutions. The aging of the Polish population is also a major challenge for the health care system - it will be necessary to match the supply of health services to the demand for them, changing due to the demographic and epidemiological factors, and to introduce system solutions for the pharmaceutical care of elderly people.

The conditions for access to non-renewable resources, including energy, shall be an important factor  of  the  future  competitive  position  of  the  country.  In  the  perspective  of  ten  years,  the energy carriers such as: coal, petroleum and natural gas will keep their primary role in energy balance of the country. Consequently, the development of the economy and its competitiveness will depend on the access to markets for these materials and their degree of monopolization. The potential  opportunity  lies  in  hydrocarbon  resources  from  unconventional  deposits,  which  may substantially  alter  the  energy  balance  of  the  country.  At  the  same  time,  due  to  technical  and technological  progress, the  opportunities  for  obtaining  raw  materials  from  renewable  sources and for using nuclear energy will increase.

Increasing demographic pressure and economic development affect the global ecosystem on an unprecedented scale. The problem of maintaining a healthy environment, able to reproduce its resources  and  biodiversity,  has  grown  into  a  key  political,  economic  and  social  problem, becoming a domain of increasing interest of national, regional and local authorities. Key issues arising from the civilization pressure on the environment concern the water (protection against floods, drought and water shortage, and ensuring access to clean water) and waste management (waste treatment hierarchy maintenance, usage of the best available techniques and technologies and product life cycle analysis), maintaining the biodiversity (nature and landscape protection),  and  air  protection.  Appropriate  protection  and  response  to  the  effects  of  climate changes, especially the excessive warming of the atmosphere - the so called greenhouse effect, and the changes resulting from floods, droughts and adverse weather events of high intensity have become a matter of special importance. Also the changes taking place in the protection and sustainable use of biodiversity will be considered. Creating a flexible climate monitoring systems for rapid response and organization of appropriate safeguards to reduce the scale of the damage and devastation becomes a task of paramount importance. A transition is necessary towards a green, low carbon economy, with a view to create new sources of economic growth based on the development of environmental technologies, and conducive to the creation of green jobs. This will require a set of flexible and complementary instruments (economic, legislative, informational),  and  strengthening  of  cooperation  with  the  private  sector,  in  particular  with industry organizations.

Scientific  and  technological  developments  and  their  practical  use  in  the  economy  increasingly determine the competitive position of the country and of enterprises. Innovation and technological race enhance competition and lead the entrepreneurs to adopting various business strategies involving - apart from self-development and implementation of innovation - creation of  strategic  alliances  of  companies,  their  mergers  and  various  forms  of  amalgamation.  The relationship between  technological change  and economic  growth  is bilateral in nature. Technological changes stimulate economic growth, which -in turn -encourages the development of technology. Technological progress is the most important factor of productivity growth, and it will remain so in the coming decades. As a result, the technological progress and the  associated  increase  in  productivity  will  contribute  to  reductions  in  mature  sectors  of  the economy, but at the same time the new technologies will stimulate the development of new markets  and  the  demand  for  labour  in  new  branches  of  production  and  services.  Nontechnological innovations, for example in organization, procedures and product marketing, also play an important role in improving innovation and competitiveness of the economy. In contrast to the technological innovations that require substantial financial resources for research, testing and  implementation,  the  organizational  or  procedural  innovations  are  characterized  by  lower cost consumption. Moreover, such solutions may lead to fast gain of a competitive advantage for the businesses that implement them. Non-technological innovation is often being developed in a processes  of  cooperation  between  entrepreneurs  and  their  customers,  leading  to  stronger consumer loyalty to a product or service.

11

[[END OF PAGE 11]]

[[START OF PAGE 12]]

The key vector of the development of technologies of strategic importance in the world will be the intensification of the  consequences  of  the  digital  revolution  and  the  resulting  new opportunities to create economic value (for example processing the large data sets - the so called Big  Data as  a  source  of  new  business  models),  which  means  new  challenges  for  Polish entrepreneurs - yet poorly prepared for the wave of change.

An important factor in the development of the system shall be the benefits - for the economy, society and the country - arising from an increased and more efficient access to ICT resources and from  opportunities  of  cyberspace,  while  ensuring  a  high  level  of  security  for  all  the  users. Conducting an educational campaign in this regard in the media, as well as implementation of appropriate training in companies and of training programs for public administration will largely contribute to the development of social capital, particularly by increasing the digital literacy of society and the activity of the citizens.

The  attempts  to  meet  the  growing  demands  of  society  undergoing  modernization  will  be  a challenge and a development stimulus for local government units. In order to be development poles, cities need to become more friendly places to live in, work, conduct business activity, and enjoy  cultural  goods,  leisure  and  entertainment.  This  will  require  an  active  investment  policy, carried out mainly at the local level, as well as the release of market potentials, able to meet the growing needs and aspirations of the population.

The basic condition for achieving the national development objectives is to restore and preserve the spatial order to meet  the needs of social, economic, environmental, cultural and compositional  aesthetic.  Structured  and  integrated  space  facilitates  the  functioning  of  society and  of  the  economy  by  creating  conditions  for  the  efficient  development  processes,  and, ultimately, it improves the quality of life. The lack of organization in the areas of development entails growth of outlays (private and public) for their maintenance, and generates an increase of investment and business costs.

Differentiation  in  terms  of  territorial  development  exists  in  Poland  in  various  breakdowns  between urban and rural, metropolitan and peripheral regions, and between historically shaped districts or voivodeships. The  current level of development  significantly  determines  the possibilities  of  improvement  in  the  coming  years  and  it  requires  the  mobilization  of  stronger diffusion processes.

The availability of transport and infrastructure is an important factor in the development process. Underdevelopment and inconsistencies in this area, inadequate infrastructure and its mismatch to  the  needs  of  socio-economic  and  spatial  development  require  continuous  modernization efforts. Gradual improvement of the transport network in Poland as well as the connections with other  European  countries  and  with  the  world  shall  -  on  the  one  hand  -  dynamize  the development of the country and of individual regions, but - on the other hand - it shall require substantial public funding.

12

[[END OF PAGE 12]]

[[START OF PAGE 13]]

Funding for the socio-economic development on a global scale in the long-term is always rooted in  savings.  However,  in  terms  of  liberalization  and  internationalization  of  the  capital  turnover, funding for the social and economic development may come from domestic or foreign savings international credits and loans, foreign direct and portfolio investment, as well as various forms of foreign aid (for example EU funds).

European  funds  will  continue  to  be  a  strong  stimulus  of  modernization  in  the  perspective  of 2020. Their proper use will contribute to building a solid foundation for development. Investing in human capital and ensuring high quality education, regardless of the place of residence and of the economic status, will be factors affecting the release of activity of the population and of the dynamics of economy. Infrastructure investments will help to reduce civilisation delays; they will also  affect  the  spread  of  development  processes  and  the  use  of  economic  development potentials. They will also be a factor important for stimulating demand.

At  the  same  time,  the  current  decade  is  the  time  to  gradually  increase  the  share  of  national development expenditure. This process should force the acceleration of structural changes in the expenditure of Polish public finance sector. There is an urgent need to achieve and then maintain the medium-term budgetary objective, namely the structural deficit of 1% of GDP. The central government has the dominant share in the deficit, while the relatively greatest deterioration has taken  place  among  local  government  and  social  security  funds,  which  requires  a  corrective action.

Reducing the structural deficit of the public finance sector became an important challenge, and the actions taken to reduce public expenditure shall contribute to the reduction of the structural deficit.  This  will  be  possible,  among  others,  by  postponing  the  effective  retirement  age,  by reducing the specific professional entitlements, by eliminating the factors that discourage labour force participation and continued employment, by changing the way of financing the inefficient or  declining  sectors.  In  parallel,  actions  should  be  taken  to  increase  budget  revenues  by extending the uniform tax rules to cover all segments of society, by rationalizing the tax system and improving the efficiency of tax collection.

Changes should be introduced to the system of financing public policies. It is necessary to link the expenditure  to  its  effects  and  to  introduce  a  system  to  assess  the  effectiveness  of  public expenditure.  Expenditure  should  be  concentrated  on  selected  areas  -  important  for  the development of the country. This should be accompanied by identifying measures related to the implementation  of  integrated  development  strategies  in  the  country.  It  will  be  essential  to actually link planning of the budget to strategic planning, based on multiannual State Financial Plan and on performance budget.

The  release  of  the  development  potential  requires  rebuilding  the  state  structures.  Complex activities are necessary to remove legal, institutional and organizational factors being an obstacle to national development and to the achievement of life goals by the citizens.

Also the consistent continuation of the reforms started in the past is advisable. The privatization process  shall  be  completed,  which  will  enhance  the  well-established  private  ownership  in  the economy.  The  freedom  of  establishment  shall  be  improved  by  deviating  from  the  current excessive  and  complicated  administrative  procedures.  It  is  necessary  for  the  public-private partnerships to function on a scale far larger than before.

There  is  a  need  for  a  change  in  the  philosophy  and  for  a  strong  shift  from  administering development to managing development. The key issue is an appropriate - realistic  and  at  the same time ambitious - determination of development objectives and designing instruments for their implementation, and then taking consistent actions leading to their realization.

13

[[END OF PAGE 13]]

[[START OF PAGE 14]]

In addition to ensuring the adequate levels of funding for the development policy - coming from EU assistance funds, state budget and budgets of local governments - a crucial factor determining the  development  of  the  country  is  the  functioning  of  professional  management  in  the  public sector. Its quality as a whole and at different levels determines - to a large extent - the ability to appropriately respond the national or regional strategic challenges emerging in the contemporary world.

The efficiency of the system is seriously affected by the people creating it. It is essential for the personnel  responsible  for  designing  and  management  of  development  processes  to  have  the relevant expertise. Their selection in both central and local government should take place on the basis  of  their  knowledge  and  skills.  The  management  system  in  the  public  sector  should  also include cooperation with partners from science, economy, and social organizations, and it should skilfully engage them in the implementation of the development strategy.

Managing  development  requires  not  only  the  public  authority  to  be  determined,  but  also  to involve  the  civil  society  actors  and  institutions  as  partners,  and  public  institutions  to  become open  to  ideas  and  solutions  coming  from  the  citizens.  Social  development  does  not  merely involve developing the system of social benefits and services. It is a much wider phenomenon, affecting many  processes  that stimulate communication  and  organization  of individuals, communities  and  institutions  around  the  common  objectives.  It  includes  the  development  of institutions and social behaviour, with a view to create a civic democracy, in which the economic freedom is based on civil liberties - and the other way round - the welfare of society is built on entrepreneurship,  cooperation,  economic  freedoms  and  civil  liberties,  and  it  promotes  the strengthening of mutual trust. The quality of social capital - trust among people and institutions, the  level  of  civic  participation,  cooperation  and  communication  skills,  cultural  and  creative potential - translate into the living conditions of citizens and their permanent welfare, as well as into sustainable development of the country.

In such a model of democracy the citizens are actively involved in building a common state and they take responsibility for their own fate as well as for the fate of the community and of future generations. Therefore, it is essential to create such conditions for the citizens that would enable them to create development processes rather than participate  in  them  passively.  An  effective civic  dialogue, in which citizens actively create development processes, must be based, among others, on expanding the scope of public consultation and enabling citizens to participate in the shaping of public policies at every stage of their development. A real civilization change shall not be possible without transforming the dominating attitude of the citizens form "passive recipient" to "active participant".

It is also important to develop and promote social dialogue at all three levels: national, regional and the level of enterprises, to develop optimal solutions in the area of public policy creation, for the  increase  of  competitiveness  and  innovativeness  of  the  Polish  economy,  allowing  for  a consensus  in  the  area  of  labor  relations,  and  to  guarantee  a  wider  public  acceptance  of  the adopted solutions.

Commissioning  the  performance  of  public  tasks  to  NGOs  plays  an  important  role  in  the implementation  of  the  civil  society  partnerships  with  institutions,  based  on  the  principle  of subsidiarity.

In launching and promoting social forces dynamizing the development and in creating a system based on empowerment of the society (social subjectivity) through the development processes, one should not forget the risk of social exclusion. The problem is compounded by the fact that the currently used instruments of social policy have proved not fully effective. It is necessary to support innovative instruments reducing the effects of social exclusion, to create social added value, including social economy. The level of social cohesion, particularly the existing diversity of income and development opportunities of different groups, largely determines the development objectives in areas such as quality of life and poverty reduction.

14

[[END OF PAGE 14]]

[[START OF PAGE 15]]

A  factor  of  growing  importance  for  modern  development  is  culture,  meaning  the  values  and norms  of behaviour accepted by society, as well as the accepted codes that enable communication  and  practices  that  condition  cooperation.  It  is  these  factors  that  largely determine the willingness to take risks as well as creative and innovative activities, and they have a  significant  influence  on  the  ability  to  interact  and  to  create  social  capital.  In  today's  rapidly changing society, culture is the main source for creating personal and group identity, and giving the  meaning  to  individual  and  collective  actions.  As  a  result,  culture  is  becoming  increasingly important  as  a  development  factor,  while  at  the  same  time  it  undergoes  profound  changes caused  by  civilizational  transformations.  As  a  result,  culture  becomes  a  space  for  innovation, where new forms of subjectivity, participation, consumption and of creation of values are sought and tested. Those have a direct (in the form of cultural industries and creative industries) and indirect impact on the socio-economic development.
