[[END OF PAGE 15]]

[[START OF PAGE 16]]

### Vision of Poland 2020

#### In 2020, Poland shall be: an active society, competitive economy and efficient state

The next decade is crucial for the economic development of Poland. It is going to be the decade of  balancing  public  finances  and  increasing  savings,  while  continuing  development  based  on eliminating  the  biggest  development  barriers  (for  example  catching  up  in  infrastructure  and reducing the disparities between regions). At the same time, however, it should be a decade of growth increasingly based on education, on digital impetus and on innovation.

As  a  result,  Poland  in  2020  will  be  able  to  better  use  its  economic,  social  and  institutional potentials in order to develop the country and improve local living conditions. The phase of using simple  reserves  resulting  from  the  transformation  and  liquidation  of  the  post-transformation development  gaps  by  means  of  direct  interventions,  to  some  extent  financed  from  external sources, shall end. The economy increasingly moves towards the mature development paradigm, built on the pillars of innovation, of sustainable territorial development and efficiency.

After a period of slow development resulting from the global crisis, a sustainable development scenario will be implemented. The emphasis placed on the construction of efficient and effective state  interacts  with  the  simultaneously  undertaken  reforms  in  public  finance,  social  security, health, education, science, culture, economy, justice and democracy. The development impulses are  coming  from  the  macroeconomic  solutions  applied  and  -  to  a  lesser  extent  -  from  direct microeconomic interventions. These processes correspond to the external environment in which Europe and the world return to the path of growth and integration.

The  Polish  economy  is  transforming  into  an  increasingly  knowledge  based  economy,  while maintaining a strong manufacturing and industrial base as a source of productivity growth and competitiveness improvement. Such a development model gains acceptance of a majority of the population  which  participates,  in  a  subjective  way,  in  developing  and  implementing  the modernization processes. Providing access to knowledge is an important impulse increasing the professional activation, including of those excluded, reducing the risk of structural unemployment and the phenomenon of extreme poverty.

Economic  growth  dynamics  will  be  higher  than  at  our  neighbours.  It  is  assumed  that  in  2020 Poland  will  reach  74-79%  of  average  EU  GDP  per  capita.  The  implementation  of  key  MTNDS development  goals  in  infrastructure,  in  human  resources  development  and  in  promoting entrepreneurship, accompanied by successful implementation of the National Finance Recovery Programme, will allow Poland to achieve more rapid growth in 2020 perspective. 4 In the horizon of  2020, to  use  this  potential  will  require  greater  labour  supply,  among  others  by  starting  the process of postponing the retirement age and by further social security system reforms. After an in-depth  analysis  of  costs  and  benefits  of  the  current  tax  system,  appropriate  changes  will  be introduced to encourage companies and households to invest and build the foundation for the innovation of the Polish economy. Also firm anchoring of inflation and public finances allowing the Polish accession to the euro area are elements of this long-term Polish growth potential Use of labour resources will be increased to a level guaranteeing that the needs of development are  met,  which  would  be  possible,  for  example,  by  increasing  the  professional  activity  of  the elderly  and  of  women.  Economic  activity  and  adaptability  shall  reach  a  level  competitive  with other economies at a similar or higher level of development.

> 4 Maintaining  the  current  development  potential  of  Poland  (without  carrying  out  the  reforms  set  in MTNDS) would allow meeting 70% of the average GDP per capita in the EU-27 around 2020. The pace of real convergence in the status quo scenario (so maintaining the existing development potential of Poland) in 2011-2020 would be significantly lower than in the period 2001-2010. In the years 2001-2010 the GDP in Poland - in comparison to the EU-27- increased from 48% to 62%, which is about 14 percent point. Without reforms this growth would amount to only 8 percent points in the next decade. The assumptions - Institute for Structural Research (ISR).

16

[[END OF PAGE 16]]

[[START OF PAGE 17]]

The availability of transport and infrastructure will improve significantly and will no longer pose a problem  of  civilizational  delay  and  development  barrier.  A  greater  emphasis  shall  be  put  on sustainable transport and on development of more environment friendly means of transport.

The energy demands of developing economy and society are met, and the sources are diversified in compliance with the environmental requirements. We are getting ready to for nuclear power implementation.

Increasing socio-economic and territorial cohesion shall enhance the inner strength and national security. The economic development level achieved shall improve the efficiency of the economy and the level and quality of life.

Poland has reduced the gap with the top European countries in the scale and the way of creation and use of intellectual capital. Intensification of territorial development processes and balancing development have  helped  the  problematic  areas  to  define  and  mobilise  their  own  potentials, leading to better integration of the whole country.

By  creating  conditions  for  better  use  of  opportunities  by  individuals  and  groups  at  risk  of exclusion,  the  scope  of  this  phenomenon  was  reduced.  Citizens  as  well  as  social  groups  and business communities perceive the state as more friendly and efficient than it was in 2010. 5 The degree of social trust keeps increasing.

The objectives set out in the Europe 2020 strategy are implemented in a harmonious way, but taking into account the specific situation of Poland as well as the level of development and the priorities of national strategies. Poland will aim at achieving the objectives set by the European Union, while focusing on its objectives to increase competitiveness of the economy, to improve institutional efficiency and effectiveness, and to ensure social and territorial cohesion.

As a result, the country, the widest circles of society as well as individual citizens, will successfully achieve their goals and will satisfy their material and socio-political needs, as well as their cultural and civilizational aspirations, including participation in the life of the international community. Poland shall be a well-organized country, one that has well established conditions for sustainable development and offers a wide range of possibilities and opportunities for individual development.  This  will  increase  the  sense  of  justice  and  social  solidarity  as  well  as  the  public confidence.

In implementing the vision of Poland 2020, the following key areas of development shall play a crucial role:

#### Institutional  transformations  strengthening  the  efficient  state
 -higher  quality of public institutions, the active role of social capital.

The  modernization  of  the  state  in  the  period  until  2020  will  be  based  on  pursuing  the  public interest in a harmonious manner, in accordance with the principle that the interest of each of Polish citizen should be respected. This will be supported by good governance of the country, in line with modern standards - management through objectives defined in a rational and realistic manner, involving different actors, integrating various public policies, and taking into account the specific  circumstances  and  needs  of  different  territories,  based  on  the  principle  of  open government. The vision of Poland as an efficient state includes system solutions, procedures and institutions  enabling  an  effective  and  user-friendly  implementation  of  laws,  and  satisfying  the needs  and  aspirations  of  citizens.  It  shall  also  involve  legal  and  institutional  infrastructure fostering  the  creation  of  competitive  advantages  for  the  country  and  for  economic  and  social actors operating in it.

> 5 In 2010, the level of trust in public administration amounted to 42% (CBOS).

17

[[END OF PAGE 17]]

[[START OF PAGE 18]]

The implementation of this vision requires modernization efforts as well as political, constitutional and institutional reforms. Modern mechanisms of acting by public administration shall be introduced into the mainstream of Polish institutional development. A new model shall be  developed  to  include  social,  economic  and  scientific  groups  into  the  stream  of  social  and economic  changes  in  order  for them  to  become  active  partners  for the planning  and implementation  of  development  activities.  At  the  same  time,  the  role  of  local  government  in policy development and in shaping the public space will increase, which is important for all the areas of development.

Effective and open public institutions will foster the growth of confidence, whereas the public debate, involving citizens in the process of creating development policy, as well as the increased access  to  knowledge  and  to  public  information  will  promote  understanding  of  the  socioeconomic transformations undertaken and will increase their public acceptance. Concern for the public  interest,  the  high  quality  of  public  debate  and  of  public  space,  the  increasing  civic participation - individual and institutionalized - and the increased role of creative and intellectual potential shall foster the building of civil society.

Development processes will be supported by a gradual release of public funds. Actions for public finance  balancing  and  for  systemic  transformations  enabling  departure  from  the  existing structure  of  public  expenditure  towards  an  increased  spending  on  research,  development  and education and a decreased relative share of fixed expenditure will begin to bring positive effects. The increase in the efficiency of public funds will contribute to a better use of off-budget sources for funding development. In many cases, the effects of changes introduced in the first phase of NDS will be visible in the long-term perspective only.

#### Effective  socio-economic  development  and  the  high  competitiveness  of  the  economy
 -economic growth resulting in Polish GDP per capita at the level of 74-79% of that in the EU by 2020; knowledge-based economy, modern infrastructure, human capital -the leading competitive advantages.

In  2020,  Poland  will  become  a  country  with  a  modern  economy,  characterized  by  high productivity.  The  main  barriers  to  growth,  such  as  low  employment,  and  the  lack  of  balance between  labour  demand  and  supply  shall  be  eliminated;  transport,  energy  and  IT  will  evolve sufficiently to meet the development needs.

Effective structural changes shall be an important factor in improving the competitiveness of the economy. Although the industry will be dominated by medium-tech sector, the sectors of hightech and of services with the highest added value will grow fastest. The effective mechanisms introduced to promote innovation as well as the increase of public investment on research and development will stimulate involvement of private entities in this area. Competitive advantages will also be built, based on the specialization of regions, conducive to above-average concentration  of  entities  operating  in  particular  sectors,  which  will  result  in  the  formation  of these  regions  in  cluster  structures,  characterized  by  a  greater  propensity  of  the  participating entities for innovation, internationalization and growth of social capital.

A  competitive  economy  is  an  economy  possessing  adequate  and  effectively  used  energy resources  allowing  for dynamic  growth.  By  2020,  most  of  the  activities  related to the diversification  of  energy  sources  and  carriers  will  enter  a  crucial  phase  of  implementation. Increased  energy  efficiency  of  the  economy  and  increased  use  of  renewable  sources  will contribute  to  reducing  CO2  emissions  and  meeting  the  commitments  under  the  Climate  and Energy Package. The condition for the implementation of the country development objectives - in addition to the energy access - is also a man-friendly environment, being a basis of his existence and  aimed  at  satisfying  his  numerous  needs.  Innovative  solutions  will  be  introduced  for  the rational use of resources, while reducing the impact of human activities on the environment.

18

[[END OF PAGE 18]]

[[START OF PAGE 19]]

The growing competitive advantage of the country will increasingly result from the development of  human  capital,  created  by  people  ever  better  educated,  more  active,  and  endowed  with abilities  enabling  better  navigation  on  the  labour  market,  as  well  as  from  the  development  of social  capital,  strengthening  the  skills  and  attitudes  conducive  to  cooperation, communication, creativity, openness, flexibility and ability to use knowledge and experience. This will be fostered by  reforms  of  education  system,  including  higher  education,  and  involving  both  organizational and financial rules as well as the introduction of modern education models, which will contribute to learning the key skills and attitudes such as: language skills, practical knowledge, team work skills, ability to use modern technology and creative thinking. Changes will also be introduced in the  approach  to  the  learning  process  by  disseminating  solutions  for  lifelong  learning.  The professional  activity  of  Poles  will  contribute  to  the  creation  of  favourable  conditions  for combining work and family functions; actions will be taken for activation of the unemployed, for reduction of the incentives for early exit  from  the  labour  market,  and  for  provision of  help  in work continuation thanks to the spread of a healthy lifestyle. The development of human capital will also affect the growth of social infrastructure, including infrastructure of culture. The modern cultural institutions, art schools and institutions operating in historic buildings create the space for the development of key competencies and are an important factor in the development of the country.

Greater  stability  of  the  economy  will  contribute  to  balancing  the  public  finances  by  the rationalization of public expenditure and by improving its structure in the direction of an increase in  the  share  of  expenditure  for  development.  At  the  same  time,  the  stability  of  business conditions will  also  allow  maintaining  low  inflation and  public  deficit  levels,  and  balancing  the current account balance.

#### Socio-economic  and  territorial  cohesion
 -  better  territorial  cohesion,  availability  of  public services,  broad  range  of  opportunities  for  individual  development,  smart  and  effective  social integration.

Improving the competitiveness of Polish economy and providing a stable base for a long-term development of the country is only possible if all areas, individuals and social groups will have the opportunity to participate in and profit from the benefits generated by those transformations.

In the period until 2020, a higher level of cohesion shall be achieved, both in social and territorial dimension, mainly thanks to public intervention aimed at making use of development potentials of all regions, and to the efforts undertaken within the framework of social policies in the area of prevention, rescue and activation as regards such social issues as poverty and social exclusion. As a  consequence,  the  diversity  of  the  measured  core  indicators  relating  to  the  most  important factors in regional development shall be reduced. This does not mean an automatic reduction of diversity  in  the  level  of  GDP  per  capita,  or  the  ability  to  create  better  jobs.  Levelling  the differences in this scope will be a result of long-term processes of structural transformations.

An effective  but  also  sustainable  transport system  is  a  necessary  condition  to  fully  exploit  the potential  inherent  in  the  economy.  In  2020,  the  existing  high  quality  transport  links  (including connections between provincial cities and Warsaw, transport links around major urban centres, and  ensuring  better  accessibility  of  rural  areas  which  conditions  the  availability  of  labour markets,  and  also  cross-border  connections)  will  strengthen  the  process  of  balancing  the territorial  development  of  the  country,  and  also  promote  its  social,  economic  and  spatial cohesion.

19

[[END OF PAGE 19]]

[[START OF PAGE 20]]

By  2020,  the  access  of  citizens  to  basic  public  services  shall  improve.  This  will  reduce  the peripheriality  of  border  areas  by  increasing  their  accessibility  in  domestic  and  international relations. Restructuring processes will lead to increased competitiveness and to diversification of economic structures in rural areas through modernization and innovation in the agri-food sector and through development of non-agricultural economic functions.

Also  the  processes  of  marginalization  will  be  halted  in  the  areas  losing  their  current  socioeconomic  functions,  while  their  capacity  to  develop  will  be  enhanced  through  revitalization processes.  New  functions,  recovery  and  diversification  of  business  will  take  place  in  the  most deprived neighbourhoods of cities, as a result of consistent revitalization activities undertaken.

Measures will be taken aimed directly at strengthening social cohesion, at increasing the activity of people socially excluded or at risk of social exclusion, and at improving the efficiency of social benefits.  Social  enterprises  operating  in  the  system  of  social  economy  shall  be  used  for  this purpose, not only strengthening social cohesion, but also allowing for the implementation of a range  of  public  services,  helping  to  create  social  capital  and  being  a  binder  for  the  local community, and functioning on the principle of solidarity (non-profit).

Increasing social cohesion will enhance the competitiveness of the economy and will significantly improvement the labour market. The approach to social cohesion will be redirected primarily to the efficient use of potentials of people with disabilities and dysfunctions which hampering their own  use  of  these  potentials.  Thus,  the  instruments  of  social  policy  will  be  designed  first  and foremost to eliminate or minimize the causes of exclusion and, if it turns out impossible, they will aim not to disturb the economic and social integration - in the greatest scope possible. Increasing social cohesion will also contribute to improving the health of Poles, achieved through actions aimed at adapting the health care system to the demographic trends.

### Strategic objective and intervention areas

The main objective of medium-term strategy is to strengthen and make use of the economic,  social  and  institutional  capabilities  in  order  to  ensure  rapid  and sustainable development of the country, and to improve the quality of life.

MTNDS indicates how the objectives of the Europe 2020 strategy will be achieved, taking into account  the  specificity  of  Poland  and  conditions  that  contribute  to  the  established  national development objectives.

MTNDS determines the key indicators reflecting the progress towards the objectives set in the selected strategic areas, and it indicates the paths leading to the levels designated, which are the guidelines for the directions of interventions, activities and indicators detailed in nine Integrated Strategies.

The choice of the three strategic areas (Efficient and effective state, Competitive economy, Social and territorial cohesion) as well as the specific objectives and directions of priority interventions in  these  areas  is  a  response  to  the  key  challenges  of  the  coming  decade,  and  it  will  help  to intensify the development process and to avoid the development drift.

Efficient state, which processes the available public funds effectively and makes efficient use of the  energy  of  self-organizing  civil  society  is  a  precondition  for  achieving  the  development objectives agreed upon. Efficient state is: transparent, friendly, supportive and participative. The efficiency  of  the  state  depends  on  the  effectiveness  of  its  institutions  and  on  its  capacity  to launch  and  carry  out  necessary  reforms,  in  cooperation  with  its  citizens  and  with  public acceptance; it also depends the management capacity at the time when resources are limited. The development management system is a complex one, and the ability to identify challenges and strategic issues as well as to respond to them effectively is largely dependent on the quality of this system at each level of state organization.

20

[[END OF PAGE 20]]

[[START OF PAGE 21]]

It  is  important  to  complete  the  process  of  transition  from  administering  development  to managing  it  (by  introducing  all  the  components  of  the  system  of  management  of  the development of the country), which would increase the efficiency of public institutions and foster the optimal achievement of objectives.

The manner of state organization is an important component of its institutional efficiency, both of its chief organs, central government and the subordinate institutions, and of entities operating at  different  territorial  levels  of  the  country.  The  efficiency  of  the  administration  of  justice  is  a major  aspect  of  the  efficiency  of  the  state.  The  state  is  the  guardian  of  liberty,  but  it  also performs specified intervention functions under various public policies. The state, therefore, is selectively  active  in  areas  that  require  its  support  to  benefit  the  citizens.  It  means  that  public institutions of the modern state should be open to the needs of citizens and enterprises, which does not mean paternalistic care aiming to meet all the needs of public services, but rather the proper implementation of the subsidiarity principle. According to this principle, institutions of the state  support  the  citizens  in  satisfying  their  needs  and  aspirations  not  only  through  providing direct  public  services,  but  also  through  stimulating  the  development  of  social  capital  (for example, through public-social partnerships). The ability of public institutions to do so will largely shape  the  public  opinion  about  the  efficiency  of  the  state,  and  it  will  have  an  impact  on  the acceptance for the implemented reforms. At the same time, it will prepare the ground for active involvement in the creation of development processes.

The efficiency of the state requires sound legislation, regulations conducive to the development of entrepreneurship, as well as macroeconomic stability and conditions fostering the growth of state revenues, with the participation of socio-economic partners.

Gaining  funds  for  development  is  an  important  task.  In  the  first  years  of  the  strategy implementation (that is in the years 2012-2015), state activities should not be limited solely to mitigating the effects of economic slowdown caused by the global crisis, but should also create the  basis  for  building  new  competitive  advantages  of  the  Polish  economy.  Also  removing  the existing development barriers, the 'bottle necks' that hinder the increase of competitiveness of Polish economy, still remains an important task, in particular, decreasing the high public sector deficit,  increasing  funds  for  development  activities  and  streamlining  the  system  of  financing public policies and of local government institutions.

The available, limited budgetary resources should be concentrated and focused on specific areas, most important for the development of economy competitiveness. At the same time, a gradual reallocation  of  public  expenditure  should  take  place,  towards  increasing  expenditure  on development activities.

Efficient state provides security for its citizens in all its aspects, it reacts in crisis situations and eliminates situations that cause or may cause risks to health and life of citizens. For this purpose, it is necessary to build an integrated national security system, linking security with development of  the  country,  and  involving  all  state  structures.  The  tasks  to  be  taken  for  safety  of  citizens should  also  include  measures  to  ensure  food  security,  which  affects  the  quality  of  life  and  of public health, as well as development opportunities. Building strong social capital, on the other hand, will contribute to strengthening the development potential of the country as well as the legitimacy and social acceptance for the changes conducted.

Increasing the competitiveness of the economy is a key task, conditioning the development of the country and the Polish position on the world market. Efficient functioning of the economy, based on sound principles, its efficiency and modernity affect the prosperity of the citizens, as well as the ability of the state to perform its functions and to ensure safety. The modern system of  global  links  creates  huge  opportunities  for  development,  but  also  exposes  the  economy, including  the  Polish  one,  to  the  competitive  pressure  of  unprecedented  scale.  The  traditional competitive  advantages  diminish  in  importance.  Hence  it  is  extremely  urgent  to  develop  new advantages, based on knowledge, on intellectual and social capital, as well as on the results of digitization.

21

[[END OF PAGE 21]]

[[START OF PAGE 22]]

Over the  next  decade,  the  education  system  and  the  conditions  for  shaping  positive  attitudes towards  lifelong  learning    -  including  informal  learning  -  shall  be  radically  improved.  Young people  shall  receive  the  state-of-the-art  knowledge  to  enable  them  to  function  in  modern civilization, to develop their curiosity, creativity, willingness to seek original solutions, the ability to  cooperate  and  the  competencies  necessary  for  self-development  in  the  future.  Higher education  system  will  aim  at  functioning  in  accordance  with  the  third  generation  university model  (connecting  the  educative  and  research  role  with  implementing  innovation  and  with business). Links of higher education system with the industry shall be strengthened. The number of graduates in technical and natural sciences shall increase. The intellectual capital, which is a solid foundation for a modern and competitive economy, shall be reinforced.

At the same time efforts will be made to increase innovation in the economy. Expenditure on R&D shall be increased and the efficiency of its use shall be improved. Conditions will also be created  to  stimulate  the  funds  of  private  sector  for  this  objective.  The  commercialization  of research  results  shall  be  improved.  The  development  of  advanced  technologies  shall  be supported and the number of innovative companies shall grow. Total employment as well as the employment in sectors with high added value shall increase. As a result, the number of patents, innovation and new technologies in Polish economy shall increase.

The existing economic potential should be used effectively. The specialization of regions based on their resources and development opportunities has to be supported.

The conditions of business functioning shall be subject to special attention. This issue requires a broad spectrum of activities - from the creation of a favourable regulatory environment - free from unnecessary and severe administrative burdens for businesses, through facilitated financing,  to  adequate  support  for  building  of  a  modern  and  functional  infrastructure.  The development  and  improvement  of  the  accessibility  to  transport  and  telecommunications infrastructure is essential.

Increasing the productivity and efficiency of work will have a significant impact on the growth of competitiveness of the economy. The factor of an increasing strategic importance for achieving this goal would be the appropriate use by the economy of the effects of digitization and of the opportunities created by the evolution of the information society into a network digital society. It will be of major importance to reduce energy consumption and to ensuring stable energy supply, adequate to the needs of the society, and at competitive prices.

In order to improve social and territorial cohesion conditions will be created for the spread of the developmental processes in geographical terms - in various areas with weak potentials, as well as in  the  form  of  inclusion  in  the  development  process  of  those  who  have  been  just  passive recipients of change. Development processes are usually accompanied by stratification, resulting from a combination of factors such as: backwardness of infrastructure, outdated socio-economic structure,  low  productivity  and  innovation,  as  well  as  weakness  of  human  and  social  capital. Compensatory  measures  taken  do  not  always  bring  the  desired  effect.  Therefore,  emphasis should be placed above all on the activities highlighting and supporting the diverse potentials, both of individuals and of social groups, as well as of individual territories on the basis of the generational and territorial solidarity rule (consisting of equal opportunities and competitiveness). In this context it is important, on the one hand, to reduce the "development gap" through actions aimed at eliminating barriers to opportunities for development and, on the other hand, to strengthen the development potential. Higher social and spatial cohesion makes it possible  for  pro-growth  factors  to  impact  the  various  areas  of  socio-economic  life.  In  the territorial arrangement, such cohesion manifests itself through stimulating the development of competitiveness and innovation growth centres, such as regional centres, on the one hand, and on  the  other  hand  -  through  strengthening  the  development  potential  of  areas  with  lower macroeconomic indicators. The impact of centres of growth on these areas and the spread of developmental processes will support changes in the economic and social sphere. Taking care of natural resources, including the maintaining of full functionality of ecosystems, is an important element of territorial cohesion. This  applies  both to  the  improvement of  infrastructure and  to taking  account  of  the  needs  of  sustainable  environmental  management  in  development processes.

22

[[END OF PAGE 22]]

[[START OF PAGE 23]]

### Development policy principles

MTNDS is based on an integrated approach to planning and to interventions under various public policies.  This  integrated  approach  allows  the  use  of  endogenous  potential  of  territories,  their resources  and  knowledge.  This  allows  for  implementing  interventions  in  response  to  global development challenges, such interventions being adapted to the local conditions. The integrated approach facilitates combining various sectoral and domain interventions, with a view to achieve the highest possible level of synergy (horizontal integration). It also promotes a stronger liaison and coordination  of activities  undertaken  by  actors  at different levels of governance  (vertical integration).  Such  integrated  approach  also  combines  economic,  social  and  territorial  aspects. Properly conducted development policy, concentrated thematically and territorially, shall contribute to a better use of all public resources, which is becoming increasingly important in the context  of  limited  national  funds  and  efficient  use  of  EU  funds,  including  under  the  financial perspective 2014-2020.

The  assumption  underlying  the  MTNDS  tasks  identified  for  the  state  is  that  public  intervention should be focused on those areas and projects that bring the greatest added value in achieving specified goals and developmental effects. The thematic concentration of public intervention in the areas  key  to  the  development  in  the  coming  years  and  in  the  resulting  projects,  with  necessary resources to achieve the expected impact, should strengthen the existing development potential and its best possible use. Such an approach enhances the development impulses and allows them to spread to other areas.

The concentration of public funds on the implementation of a limited number of priorities, essential for  ensuring  economic  growth  and  competitiveness  of  the  economy,  will  prevent  them  from spreading,  and  thus  being  used  ineffectively.  The  scope  of  the  state's  involvement  should  be differentiated  -  interventions  will  be  undertaken  in  those  areas  where  the  market  action  is insufficient, and in the scope necessary to achieve the planned objectives and results. In areas where market forces are sufficient, the public intervention will be limited to creating regulatory, institutional and organizational conditions, facilitating the undertaking of certain activities by non-state actors.

The importance of public-private partnership will grow. Various financial engineering instruments (guarantees,  loans,  preferential  loans  and  equity  instruments,  such  as  seed  funds  and  venture capital)  will  be  used  in  a  larger  scope,  thus  increasing  the  efficiency  of  invested  funds  and facilitating the involvement of, for example, NGOs in the execution of public tasks commissioned by the public administration. Thanks to the repayable assistance, a large part of funds can be re-used, allowing support for more entities than in the case of non-repayable assistance in the form of single cash  transfers.  A  necessary  condition  for  the  use  of  financial  engineering  instruments  is  to encourage  private  capital  to  become  more  involved  in  financing  the  planned  projects.  The advantages  of  extending  the  scope  of  financial  engineering  instruments  include:  an  increased involvement of financial institutions, the ability to attract additional resources, and the distribution of risks incurred during the investment by all stakeholders.

23

[[END OF PAGE 23]]

[[START OF PAGE 24]]

The effectiveness of thematic concentration will depend largely on directing public intervention taking into account the specificity of the territory for which such intervention is targeted. It is also necessary to use a territorial approach to development, which consists, on the one hand - in the use of knowledge and experience of local and regional development actors, and on the other hand - in making use of analyses of the challenges and potentials for development, as well as of functional  linkages  between  different  areas  in  planning  and  implementation  of  development activities.  The  result  of  a  territorial  approach  should  consist  in  designing  a  set  of  public interventions under several policies which would be tailored to the needs of a given territory, involving various actors at the national, regional and local level. Territorial approach promotes consistent  funding  of  the  development  objectives  in  relation  to  space,  and  it  increases  the efficiency  of  public  intervention  in  accordance  to  the  principle  of  subsidiarity,  and  multi-level management  of  development  processes.  Better  targeting  of  public  resources  strengthens  the development processes specific for a given area / territory.

Many reforms need to be carried out in order to avoid the development drift. It should, however, be taken into account that the public funds are limited in relation to the tasks set by the MTNDS. In order to increase the efficiency of funds distribution, the principle of conditionality in the granting of  public  funding  shall  be  followed.  The  volume  of  public  funds  allocated  to  various  tasks  will depend,  among  others,  on  the  type  of  intervention,  on  the  entities  performing  the  task  (for example,  using the public-private partnerships mechanism,  extensive  involvement  of local communities, for example in the form of local initiatives, as defined by law), as well as on expected outcomes, etc. The principle of conditionality will also account for the territorial conditions.

Such a conditional allocation of public funds should bring tangible benefits, particularly given the limited  financial  resources  and  the  savings  measures  undertaken.  The  focus  on  results  will facilitate the achievement of MTNDS development goals rather than dispersing resources, and as such  will  force  better  targeting  of  the  planned  public  interventions.  The  instruments  of conditionality can successfully support efforts to prepare the ground for structural transformation  and  public  investment  (for  example  adequate  administrative  capacity  and  a favourable regulatory environment, application of useful principles and standards, set out among others  in  the  EU  Directives);  they  can  also  mobilize  public  institutions  and  private  partners  to improve their efficiency and effectiveness.

The development goals set will be achieved thanks to several interventions under various public policies,  carried  out  by  different  entities  (ministries  and  institutions).  This  will  require  good coordination of interventions, at the stage of programming and planning as well as the stage of implementation, in order to obtain the synergies necessary to achieve the objectives of MTNDS. This  means  moving  away  from  the  sectoral  to  a  horizontal  approach.  The  implementation mechanisms in all development strategies and programs, including the rules for identifying and funding projects, need to be subordinated to the above principle.

An  essential  condition  for  high  efficiency  of  public  intervention  is  to  base  public  policies  on evidence  ( evidence-based  policies ),  that  is  to  make  decisions,  at  both  strategic  and  operational levels, on the basis of analysis. This will be supported by strengthening and widening the level of evaluation in order to examine the whole system of policy development systematically, regardless of the source of funding for intervention. An adequate analytical capacity of the units responsible for programming and implementation of development activities and for ongoing cooperation with external  experts  shall  be  an  important  support  for  the evidence  based  policies .  It  will  also  be important to stimulate the development of new analytical methods resulting from the progress of knowledge and of measurement techniques (such as new sources of data and information resulting from use of digitization), as well as the development of knowledge on identifying, analysing and meeting the needs.

24

[[END OF PAGE 24]]

[[START OF PAGE 25]]

### Legal framework, applicable national and EU documents

#### National documents

The  system  of  national  strategic  documents  is  set  out  in  the  Act  of  6  December  2006  on  the principles  of  development  policy  (Polish  Journal  of  Laws  [Dz.U.]  of  2009  No  84,  item  712,  as amended). This Act determines, among others, the medium-term national development strategy, including  its  time  horizon,  its  role  and  relationship  with  other  documents,  and  its  internal structure. The Act also lays down the requirement of periodic updates of the strategy - at least once every four years.

In order to increase the efficiency of programming and implementation of development policies and  to  improve  the  quality  of  public  institutions,  the  Ministry  of  Regional  Development,  in collaboration with the Office of the Prime Minister and Strategic Advisory Group of the Prime Minister,  has  developed  the  assumptions  of  Poland's  Development  Management  System  -  a document adopted by the Council of Ministers on 27 April 2009. This document provides for a deep  reconstruction  of  the  current  programming  model  of  national  development,  including integration of socio-economic and spatial planning.

Poland 2030. Development Challenges Report, presented in July 2009 (developed by the Strategic Advisory Group of the Prime Minister), is the basis for a debate on the future of the country. This document,  one  of  the  first  such  extensive  reports  drafted  by  the  Government,  diagnosis  the current situation and presents the dilemmas that need to be solved in a horizontal perspective, combining sectoral measures; it also gives recommendations on how the identified dilemmas are to be solved.

The report is a tool to develop a strategic orientation in governing the state through:

- identification  and  description of the 10 key challenges and accompanying dilemmas opportunities and threats Poland is going to face over the next 20 years;

- multi-level  diagnosis  allowing  to  specify  the  situation  of  Poland  after  the  20  years  of transformation;

- determining  the  development  path  for  Poland,  which  will  allow  avoiding  the  risk  of development drift;

- recommending the directions of state policy corresponding to the challenges diagnosed in order to enable and promote the implementation of sustainable development model for Poland.

The  basic  group  of  documents  relevant  for  the  medium-term  national  development  strategy, emerging  in  parallel  with  it,  includes:  Long-term  national  development  strategy,  a  group  of integrated development strategies and the new National Reform Programme - the so-called new generation documents. The basis for creating a new system of strategic documents is the national development strategies organization plan, adopted by the Council of Ministers in November 2009 (as  amended),  which  sets  out  key  development  strategies  for  implementing  the  development objectives  contained  in  LTNDS  and  in  MTNDS  (while  reducing  the  number  of  the  current development strategies to nine documents of this type). Due to the adopted procedure and the schedule of work on these documents, they are closely correlated and mutually determine each other. These relations correspond to the provisions governing the hierarchy of strategies, according to which "the medium-term national development strategy takes into account the findings of the long-term  national  development  strategy"  and  is  implemented  through  other  development strategies. National Reform Programme is the document determining a set of activities, which the Polish Government is undertaking to implement the Europe 2020 strategy.

25

[[END OF PAGE 25]]

[[START OF PAGE 26]]

The  Multiannual  State  Financial  Framework,  prepared  under  the  provisions  of  the  Act  of  27 August 2009 on public finances (Polish Journal of Laws [Dz.U.] No 157, item 1240, as amended) is an important element of the new system. It contains a forecast on the amount of funds to be allocated from the state budget for the implementation of strategic development objectives in a four-year perspective.

#### EU documents

The Europe 2020 strategy for smart, sustainable and inclusive growth adopted by the European Council  on  17  June  2010,  is  a  key  document  for  drafting  the  medium-term  strategy  for  the country in the context of Polish membership in the European Union. Europe 2020 strategy is of fundamental importance for the development of the EU over the next decade. It specifies the actions to be undertaken in a coordinated manner by the Member States to accelerate getting out from the current crisis and to prepare the European economy for the future challenges. The strategy determines three priorities the implementation of which shall take place both at EU and at national levels: smart growth (fostering knowledge, innovation, education and digital society), sustainable  growth  (more  resource  efficient  production)  and  inclusive  growth  (increasing professional skills and fighting poverty). The implementation of priorities under the Europe 2020 strategy will result achieving five measurable, interdependent objectives, outlined in the strategy and concerning:

- checkbld increased spending on R&D,

- checkbld increased employment rate,

- checkbld increased share of people with higher education in society; smaller percentage of early school leavers,

- checkbld reduced CO2 emission rate, 20/20/20 objectives on climate and energy achieved,

- checkbld reduced number of people living in poverty.

The instrument for the implementation of Europe 2020 strategy at the EU level consists of seven flagship  initiatives  and  of  10  Integrated  Guidelines  for  economic  and  employment  policies  of Member States. Medium-term goals of national development strategies support to a large extent the implementation of all these projects. 6

Objective II.2. MTNDS Economy efficiency improvement » Flagship initiative: An industrial policy for the globalization era ;

Objective II.3. MTNDS Economy innovation improvement » Flagship initiative: Innovation Union;

Objective II.4. MTNDS Human capital development » Flagship initiatives: Youth on the Move; An agenda for new skills and jobs;

Objective  II.5.  MTNDS  Increasing  the  use  of  digital  technologies  »  Flagship  initiative:  Digital agenda for Europe;

Objective II.6. MTNDS Energy security and safety of the environment; and Objective II.7 MTNDS Improvement of transportation efficiency » Flagship initiative: Resource Efficient Europe ;

Objective  III.1.  MTNDS  Social  integration  and  Objective  III.2.  MTNDS  Providing  of  access  and public services of defined standards » Flagship initiatives: An agenda for new skills and jobs and European platform against poverty and social exclusion.

Other medium-term objectives of the national development strategies will contribute indirectly to the implementation of Europe 2020. The actions within the framework of objectives under II.1.

> 6 Actions  to  follow  up  directly  in  the  implementation  the  Europe  2020  Strategy  are  indicated  in  this document (highlighted in the tables).

26

[[END OF PAGE 26]]

[[START OF PAGE 27]]

Strengthening macroeconomic stability and I.2. Providing funds for development activities, which include response to the barriers to growth identified for Poland by the European Commission - a high  structural  deficit  of  public  finances  and  a  low  overall  level  of  investment  expenditure, including for transport and energy infrastructure - are particularly important.

The realization of these objectives will proceed, among others, in the rhythm of the European Semester, which is currently the basic instrument of EU economic policy coordination.

Also  the  renewed  EU  Sustainable  Development Strategy, aimed at balancing economic growth and high living standards of environmental protection, adopted by the European Council on 26 June  2006,  is  an  important  document  which  focuses  on  issues  related  to  the  management  of natural resources, including the recommended methods of production and consumption, aimed at  protecting  the  Earth's  limited  resources.  The  strategy  aims  to  increase  prosperity  through action in areas such as: protection of the natural environment (economic development without destroying the environment), equity and social cohesion (creation of a democratic society with equal development opportunities for everyone), economic welfare (increased employment and stable jobs), meeting the obligations on the international scene (international cooperation and assistance  for  developing  countries  entering  the  path  of  sustainable  development).  The  EU Member States, including Poland, are obliged to achieve the objectives of this strategy on the basis of documents and national policies.

Under  MTNDS  also  actions  resulting  from  the  so-called  Climate  and  Energy  Package  are implemented.  Climate  and  Energy  Package  is  a  series  of  legislative  solutions,  adopted  on  17 December 2008 and aimed at controlling and reducing greenhouse gas emissions in the EU. The package assumes a 20% reduction in greenhouse gas emissions in the EU, compared to the year 1990,  a  20%  share  of  renewable  energy  in  total  energy  consumption  in  2020  (the  share  for Poland is 15%), and 20% increase in energy efficiency by 2020.

MTNDS  also  takes  into  account  objectives  formulated  in  other  materials  and  documents  of strategic nature, of both national and European legislation, relating to the activities relevant for the functioning of the state, economy and society.

## MAIN AREAS OF INTERVENTION, OBJECTIVES AND DEVELOPMENT PRIORITIES

### Strategic Area I. Effective and efficient state

#### Objective I.1. Transition from development administration to development management

The  way  the  state  is  organized  is  an  important  element  of  changes  aimed  at  improving  its efficiency  and  effectiveness,  namely:  its  structure,  division  of  powers  and  responsibilities, coordination and collaboration mechanisms and process management skills, all of them ensuring that the state is able to respond effectively to the challenges resulting from internal and external conditions.  This  applies  to  the  chief  state  authorities,  central  government  and  its  subordinate institutions as well as to entities operating at different territorial levels of the country, namely in local government and self-government administration.

Modern  public  institutions  are  institutions  that  are  effective,  accessible,  open  to  the  citizen's needs, transparent and friendly, ready to support civic participation and civic energy. The speed of their reaction to the changing needs and expectations will largely shape the public opinion on the  efficiency  of  the  state,  and  above  all  will  create  climate  of  social  acceptance  for  changes planned under various public policies. Public institutions will also promote active inclusion of the citizens in development processes. Thus, public institutions should be flexible in order to react fast  and  effectively,  to  ensure  that  the  solutions  used  by  them  bring  the  greatest  possible benefits  to  the  citizen,  the  entrepreneur  and  the  state.  Therefore,  a  key  element  in  the functioning of public institutions will be moving from administration to management. This means shifting the focus from a passive approach, aimed at following the procedures to an active one, associated with the search for best practices and with the implementation of objectives. To make the transition from administration to management possible, it is necessary to review and analyze the mechanisms and principles of the functioning of public institutions, both those operating at national level and those operating at different levels of territorial administration. This review will result  in  the  competencies  of  public  institutions  becoming  organized  and  it  will  lead  to  the introduction of necessary structural and organizational changes.

Completing the process of implementation of a new system of strategic documents, reflecting the  vision  and  the  developmental  tasks  of  the  country,  will  also  be  conducive  to  managing development effectively. It is also important to build mechanisms ensuring consistency of socioeconomic, regional  and  spatial  programming  as  well  as  implementation  of  strategic  objectives through  efficient  preparation  and  implementation  of  programs.  It  is  necessary  to  improve  the administration management systems through increased use of tools based on process approach and risk management.

Simultaneously, there will be measures introduced to improve the quality of the law created. The quality of law is a decisive factor in the operation of the state. It determines the effectiveness of state  authorities,  it  affects  the  quality  of  life,  it  is  decisive  for  the  competitiveness  of  the economy.  Functional  law  should  be  adequate  to  the  needs  of  regulations,  reducing  the intervention of the state in the lives of individuals or of business organizations and companies to a  reasonable minimum. It should also correspond to the dynamics of development of modern civilization, keeping pace with the social, economic and technological processes that are taking place. A review of the existing legislation shall constitute basis for necessary changes, ensuring consistency, timeliness and adequacy of the provisions while eliminating unnecessary regulations and simplifying the legislative process.

The development of ICT will be conducive to better communication between public institutions and  citizens,  it  will  increase  the  availability  of  infrastructure  in  this  area,  and  will  foster  the implementation  of  the  open  government  idea,  giving  the  citizens  an  opportunity  to  actively participate  in  creating  the  law  and  in  implementing  policies.  Modern  technologies  make  it possible to spread the formula of e-consultation at each stage of development of public policies. At  the  same  time,  it  would  be  necessary  to  use  and  strengthen  the  existing  channels  of communication between the government and the citizens.

28

[[END OF PAGE 28]]

[[START OF PAGE 29]]

##### Priority directions of public intervention:

###### I.1.1. Organized competences will enable the implementation of development activities

As a result of the review of public institutions, a number of changes will be introduced in the scope of competence of individual actors (government and its subordinate entities, government and self-government administration, within self-government administration) with a view to avoid duplication of their responsibilities and to improve coordination necessary for the implementation of development activities. This is particularly important in relation to the units of local self-government. Organizing their competencies will cause transferring to regional and/or local level a part of the tasks of the government administration and of non-budgetary institutions that, due to their nature, should be addressed by local self-government. When the scope of the changes  is  defined,  the  way  of  funding  of  the  tasks  shall  be  adjusted  accordingly,  which  may involve modifying the system of local government revenues. The solutions developed, as a rule, should  cause  no  increase  in  the  burden  of  the  state  budget  but  should  only  improve  the efficiency of public spending, including its better adaptation to the tasks, which will be fostered by greater flexibility in the management of funds.

The process of introducing a new system of strategic documents shall be completed, based on long- and medium-term national development strategies and on 9 Integrated Strategies, as well as  taking  into  account  the  concept  of  spatial  development  of  the  country.  This  process  will consist in making the inventory of and consolidating the currently applied programs and other implementation documents as well as in developing and implementing new, programmes and instruments, necessary for Integrated Strategies. At the same time the voivodeship development strategies  will  be  updated,  which  should  include  the  objectives  of  the  medium-term  national development  strategy,  of  the  national  strategy  for  the  national  development  and  of  relevant cross-regional strategies. Documents will be prepared related to the next EU financial perspective (2014-2020),  adapted  to  the  new  cohesion  policy  (taking  into  account,  among  others,  the conditionality and thematic concentration), to Common Agricultural and Fisheries Policy as well as to priorities of Europe 2020 strategy.

###### I.1.2. Increasing the efficiency of public institutions

Changes in the structure and in the way of governance of public institutions will be the key to increasing their efficiency. The principles of ministerial accountability for government departments  shall  be  reformulated.  Uniform  rules  shall  be  set  governing  the  functioning  of administration, among others its remuneration,.

Introducing of the managerial public governance model, characterized by, among others, focus on the citizen, on examining the results instead of the procedures, and on increased participation of  non-state  actors  in  performing  the  tasks  will  improve  the  efficiency  of  public  institutions. Mechanisms will be introduced of periodic assessment of public entities in their implementation of tasks as well as in the effectiveness of their response to social and economic needs, as defined in  the  approved  policies,  programs  and  plans,  including  those  resulting  from  international cooperation within the European Union. Costs and benefits analysis of the activities performed by the public institutions as well as civil monitoring will be used to improve their effectiveness.

Stabilization  of  the  personnel  of  public  administration  will  be  important,  as  well  as  the improvement  of  its  competence,  knowledge  and  skills  and  introduction  of  proper  incentive schemes. A professional body of staff should be a characteristic feature of both the administration  and  the  government.  Towards  this  end,  a  modern  training  program  for  civil servants  shall  be  developed,  with  an  emphasis  on  strengthening  the  role  of  leaders  and leadership  in  administration,  on  disseminating  ethical  principles  (codes  of  ethics),  and  on partnerships and networking in administration.

Improving communication with the citizen and the internal flow of information will be ensured by a central platform for sharing information between public institutions and individuals / citizens as well  as  entities.  At  the  same  time,  the  ideas  and  principles  of  open  government  will  be implemented, consisting in facilitating the participation of citizens and of civil society organizations in the creation of and consultation on the new legislation and policies as well as on services  of  public  benefit,  based  among  others  on  open  resources  (data,  public  information, culture, knowledge, etc.). Public institutions will also develop new ways of involving citizens in the process of consultation as regards complex development programs that combine education and information with public debate.

30

[[END OF PAGE 30]]

[[START OF PAGE 31]]

A  new  model  of  governance  should  ensure  the  transparency  of  activities  and  functioning  of mechanisms to prevent the occurrence of corruption, including through instruments to combat fraud affecting the public administration.

###### I.1.3. The introduction of uniform rules for e-gov in administration (e-government)

In the context of administrative efficiency, standardization and interoperability of the IT solutions applied  is  important.  A  single  IT  centre  for  the  whole  government  is  planned  to  be  created, making it possible to implement uniform rules for e-gov, as regards, for example, ordering the equipment  centrally,  and  concerning  the  type  of  software  used  as  well  as  the  exchange  of information and communication. Digitization of public administration will be necessary, allowing a  simplification  of  administrative  processes  and  the  creation  of  structured  digital  knowledge resources; it  will  also  make  auditing  the  quality  of  public  information  possible,  with  a  view  to create a platform for information flow. A priority task, conditioned by the development of ICT infrastructure,  will  consist  in  providing  access  to  the  widest  possible  range  of  public  services rendered electronically, allowing full interaction with the office, and making it possible to handle matters at a distance. These actions will help to simplify the procedures for handling matters and will  facilitate  access  to  the  information  required  at  any  stage  of  the  administrative  procedure. Interactive administration portals, enabling instant access to the service or information required, tracking the status of official matters and personalization services (matching the offer to user's profile and  requirements)  shall be  used  for this purpose.  Electronic Platform  of Public Administration  Services  (ePUAP)  should  be  -  among  others  -  developed  in  this  direction.  An essential  element  in  introducing  the  e-government  will  be  training  of  administrative  staff, responding to immediate needs arising from the use of modern tools.

###### I.1.4. Law quality improvement

The  activities  shall  be  continued  aimed  at  systemic  reform  of  the  Polish  law  as  well  as  of  the operation  of  public  administration,  with  a  view  of  making  the  institutional  environment  more supportive to the development of entrepreneurship in Poland and more citizen-friendly, ensuring at the same time that law functions properly and  citizens are protected against their rights being violated (for example corruption, fraud, unfair competition). In this context, it will be important to reduce bureaucracy, as well as the number of permits, records of regulated activities, licenses, authorizations, and the administrative burden of regulations.

It  will  be  necessary  to  strive  to  modify  the  law-making  mechanisms  and  the  principles  of  its operation,  to  review  the  existing  regulations,  except  those  functioning  as  expected,  and  to undertake actions to repeal unnecessary legal act. Increased attention shall be paid to the effects of  the  introduction  of  draft  legislation  and  to  their  influence  on  the  financial  and  economic balance of costs and benefits. The regulatory inflation, observed for a long time, shall be limited and the awareness and ability to use other, non-legislative forms, of shaping social and economic life  shall  be  strengthened. The projects will be accompanied by regulations based on facts and professional analysis of the consequences of their implementation. Consultation procedures will be improved to ensure that stakeholders and experts have a real impact on the formulations of solutions.  The  area  of  law  application  requires  significant  improvements.  It  is  particularly important to ensure a systematic monitoring of the law functioning and a radical reduction of the disparities in the application of the provisions, which undermine legal certainty and prestige of the state. The access of the poorest social groups to legal and civil advice at the prejudicial and judicial stage of proceedings should also be increased.

###### I.1.5. Ensuring spatial order 7

A system of monitoring of spatial processes will be built, based on the European and national spatial  information,  with  a  strong  role  for  the  integrated  information  system  on  real  estate. Evaluating  the  implementation  of  spatial  policy,  as  well  as  of  other  policies  with  a  significant impact on the spatial structures, shall be an important element of such a monitoring system.

In  the  medium-term  perspective,  it  is  advisable  to  significantly  increase  the  coverage  of  the country with spatial development plans, making such plans obligatory for the development areas. Study  of  conditions  and  directions  of  spatial  development  of  municipality  and  local  spatial development  plan  are  the  basic  documents  for  space  management.  An  obligation  shall  be introduced  to  prepare  spatial  development  plans  for  functional  areas,  including:  urban  areas, metropolitan  areas  in  particular,  developmentally  sensitive  areas,  i.e.  areas  where  natural resources are protected and used for economic purposes, cultural heritage sites, areas at risk of natural  disasters,  mountainous  areas,  areas  not  yet  covered  by  the  process  of  planning  (the coastal zone, territorial sea, exclusive economic zone) and border areas.

Delimitation will take place at the stage of voivodship spatial  development  plan,  with participation  of  entities  of  national,  regional  and  local  level  -  this  concerns  in  particular metropolitan  areas.  Moreover,  to  ensure  continued  and  sustainable  development  of  inland coastal areas and of marine areas, the Integrated Coastal Zone Management mechanisms will be implemented and spatial development plans for marine areas shall be elaborated.

An appropriate system of regulations and decisions will be introduced, allowing - among others to  control  the  suburbanization  processes,  to  make  plans  for  development  areas  and  to  use functional planning, taking into account the social aspect as well as the need to protect cultural and natural landscape. The planned system of controls and decisions, as well as the monitoring of spatial  phenomena,  shall  be  introduced  following  the  principle  accounting  for  environmental aspects.

> 7 Specific activities for the provision of spatial order were included in the "National Spatial Development Concept 2030" adopted by the Council of Ministers on 13 December 2011 and in the documents for its implementation.

34

[[END OF PAGE 34]]

[[START OF PAGE 35]]

A greater emphasis shall be put on strengthening social participation in spatial planning. Local real  estate  stock  will  be  supported  to  facilitate  land  purchase,  expropriation  and  exchange,  as well  as  mergers  and  secondary  divisions  of  land  and  acquisition  by  the  municipality  of  the  redivided land for the purposes of common facilities (streets, parking lots, technical facilities, green areas).  In  addition,  economic  incentives  will  be  introduced  fostering  a  more  efficient  use  of space. People responsible for land management will take actions to develop land assigned for this  purpose,  using  the  public  resources,  whereas  economic  instruments  will  be  developed, compensating municipalities for the expended funds to encourage them to draw up local spatial development  plans.  These  changes  will  also  constitute  an  incentive  for  municipalities  to cooperate  within  the  functional  areas  of  the  cities  as  well  as  within  the  developed  functional areas, identified in spatial development plans of the voivodeships.

One of the major challenges in this area is to ensure proper water management as a component of  biodiversity,  but  also  as  the  basis  for  regional  development  and  for  economic  growth. Sustainable water management is also important for protection against floods. This issue is linked closely to spatial policy and to ensuring spatial order.

#### Objective I.2. Providing funds for development activities

The  primary  task  of  the  state  over  the  next  decade  will  be  to  provide  funds  for  development activities.  This  task,  in  the  face  of  high  deficit  and  debt  and  the  consequent  need  to  increase savings discipline requires making difficult choices and adopting comprehensive solutions.

Polish  economy  has  coped  with  the  global  economic  crisis  relatively  well,  but  the  crisis  has highlighted  structural  weaknesses  of  the  public  finance,  for  example  a  high  risk  of  structural deficit of public finance and of public debt.

The existing barriers that prevent or restrict the provision of an adequate level of funding for the development  activities,  and  thus  for  increasing  the  competitiveness  of  Polish  economy  alongside the high public deficit and debt, include: inefficient system of financing public policies and  scarce  funding  for  local  government  units,  the  structure  of  public  expenditure  not responding  to  the  challenges  economy  has  to  face  (large  share  of  rigid  expenditure,  limited resources for investment), and a poor use of extra budgetary funds. The problem remains valid despite the new solutions introduced in recent years (Multiannual State Financial Framework MASFF, performance budget). The insufficient link between financial planning and the strategic objectives of development policy leads to a situation where funds are dispersed or overlap, and are  spent  on  activities  and  initiatives  not  forming  a  coherent  whole  (for  example,  some development  programs,  including  multiannual  ones).  Moreover,  a  thorough  analysis  and evaluation of the effectiveness of public expenditure is lacking. Only the compliance of spending with  the  Budget  Act  is  assessed.  Neither  the  formalized  regulatory  impact  assessment  (RIA) serves the task.

Economic growth (3,9% GDP in 2010) 8 of  Poland,  which  remains at a good level compared to other European countries, can potentially support the achievement of the set objective, as well as the EU funding (which however in the next programming period, namely 2014-2020, may be somewhat  lower  compared  to  the  funds  received  by  Poland  under  the  Financial  Perspective 2007-2013) and other funding, including from private sources.

Therefore, in order to create the conditions for achieving the development objectives, set for the medium and for the  long-term,  it  is  necessary-  on  the  one  hand  -  to  take  several  actions  to balance the public finances, and on the other hand - to modernize the structure of expenditure towards increasing funding for pro-development activities and improving their effectiveness as well  as  to  seek  and  make  better  use  of  the  off-budget  sources  of  development  financing.  A significant  potential  in  this  regard  lies  in  the  effective  use  of  the  public-private  partnership instrument, and even the public-private-social partnership instrument as the source of financing development undertakings.

> 8 Source: CSO.

36

[[END OF PAGE 36]]

[[START OF PAGE 37]]

##### Priority directions of public intervention:

###### I.2.1. Modernization of public expenditure structure

The budgetary resources available should be concentrated and focused on specific areas, most important  for  developing  the  competitiveness  of  the  economy.  Attempts  will  be  made  to modernize  the  structure  of  public  expenditure,  consisting  in  gradual  reallocation  of  public expenditure  towards  an  increased  spending  on  development  activities  (education,  health, transport,  communication  infrastructure,  environment,  research  and  development,  culture). Spending on R&D and education in particular will be increased, and simultaneously changes will be introduced in the financing of research and in the management of science. The investment in transport infrastructure, as well as in telecommunication and energy will also be increased. This will require rationalization of other budget items and proper addressing of the necessary transfer expenses.

Classification of expenditure will be tailored to the needs of development policy, so as to fully reflect the resources allocated for development activities.

###### I.2.2. Improving the efficiency of public funds

Actions will be  continued  to increase the efficiency of expenditure. Earmarking  in the Multiannual  State  Financial  Plan  (MASFP)  of  funds  for  the  implementation  of  integrated development strategies, implementing the performance budget in full, defining competencies in the  scope  of  financing  public  policies,  changing  the  funding  model  of  such  policies,  and introducing a system to assess the effectiveness of public expenditure - all these measures will foster concentration of resources and will enhance their effectiveness.

Mechanisms will be introduced to eliminate the dispersion of budgetary resources and to ensure that they focus on the selected areas, relevant for the development of the country. One of such mechanisms will consist in actual linking of budget planning documents, prepared both at the national  level  (MASFP,  state  budget)  and  by  local  self-government  units  (long-term  financial forecast of local government, local government budget) with key strategic documents, namely: LTNDS,  MTNDS,  and  the  Integrated  Strategies.  The  criteria  for  territorial  allocation  of  funds intended for financing national public policies having a strong territorial  impact  (health,  active labour  market  policies,  culture,  education,  higher  education,  etc.)  shall  be  worked  out  or developed.  It  is  also  possible  to  introduce  changes  in  the  LGUs  revenue  system,  which  will become  better  tailored  to  the  needs  of  local  governments,  and  will  increase  the  financial autonomy of local government units in carrying out the development activities.

37

[[END OF PAGE 37]]

[[START OF PAGE 38]]

The existing development programs, including in particular the multi-annual programs, shall be organised. This will lead to reducing their number and to focusing on key development activities, resulting  from  the  vision  adopted  for  the  country.  These  activities  will  require,  among  others, ensuring a full compliance of the currently implemented performance budget with the strategic documents - to ensure transparency in the financing of development activities, to identify the development  expenses,  and  to  ensure  that  such  expenses  are  better  linked  to  the  MTNDS development  priorities  and  are  focused  territorially.  At  the  same  time,  the  multiannual  state budget  planning  needs  an  improvement;  the  financial  plans  should  secure  the  funds  for development activities in a period longer than the currently provided for (4 years). Such MASFP should be a point of reference for the annual budget.

In addition, a system will be introduced to assess the effectiveness of budget expenditures, which shall constitute a basis for making decisions on the allocation of funds in the subsequent years.

###### I.2.3. Increasing the use of extra budgetary funds

Given the amount of resources needed to achieve the development objectives set, including in particular those concerning infrastructure investment, it is necessary to diversify the sources of financing development.  The  limited budgetary  resources for development  activities will, therefore, be supplemented with, among others: a bigger use of public-private partnerships, the EU funds under the 2007-2013 budget perspective and under the 2014-2020 perspective (with increased use of financial engineering instruments such as: guarantees, loans, preferential loans Europe 2020: Digital agenda for Europe and  equity  instruments,  such  as  seed  funds  and  venture  capital),  as  well  as  direct  foreign investment.  It  is  important  at  the  same  time  to  ensure  efficient  service  for  businesses  and citizens  by  banks,  and  to  mobilize  the  private  sector  to  cooperate  and  share  responsibility  for safe environment in all the spheres of business and the public activity. Instruments should also be used to encourage entrepreneurs to finance investment form funds other than their own, thus creating greater opportunities for development.
