#### Objective I.3. Improvement of the conditions for satisfying the individual needs and activity of citizens

Modern  states  are  highly  powerful  thanks  to  the  awareness,  activity  and  prosperity  of  their citizens  -  both  in  their  professional  and  social  life.  Citizens  want  the  state  to  provide  the conditions for satisfying their individual and collective needs and aspirations. A state fulfilling its subordinate role in this  regard  encourages  the  building  and  strengthening  of  confidence of  its citizens.

A hardly effective justice system is a particular problem which results in, among others, too long proceedings in general courts and unsatisfactory law enforcement It is particularly important to reduce the divergence of application of the existing provisions, undermining legal certainty and prestige  of  the  state.  It  would  be  important  to  change  the  legal  mentality  and  the  attitude towards citizens, through easing excessive formalism and drawing more attention to the purpose of the provision and to its proper interpretation. At the same time, it is crucial to shape attitudes of respect for standards and for the rights of others, as well as attitudes of morality, honesty and trust. Compliance therewith does not only reduce the need for state intervention in the relations between citizens or businesses, it also promotes economic development and social prosperity.

Currently we observe a low level of social capital in Poland, which largely results from a low level of  mutual  trust  between  citizens  and  public  institutions  and  from  low  confidence  in  the relationship between citizens and social groups. It is necessary to change the method of financing the activities of public institutions and public administration, as well as related regulations and practice, so that citizen would no longer feel like a suppliant but finds himself to be the centre of attention; the number of entities and of individuals with whom the administration interacts and communicates in an open and approachable way should grow steadily. Underdeveloped culture of social dialogue at the stage of policy making and regulation drafting adversely affects the level of  credibility  towards  public  authority,  increasing  the  feeling  of  having  no  influence  on  the national  decision-making  processes.  The  lack  of  universal  responsibility  for  public  good  is accompanied  by  the  acceptance  of  'non-citizen'  behaviour  and  by  a  lack  of  practice  and  of mechanisms  for  cooperation  between  citizens.  Also  a  low  level  of  public  debate,  which  is dominated by the winner-looser model instead of looking for consensus, is an obstacle for the social capital development. At the same time this debate, appropriated by the media, is often not conducive for dissemination of good practices and mechanisms, or of positive impulses for social cooperation. A kind of resignation of public media, especially of television, from the standardcreating  role  that  would  enable  the  dissemination  and  promotion  of  social  and  civic  values  is particularly worrying.

39

[[END OF PAGE 39]]

[[START OF PAGE 40]]

The  emergency  response  system  needs  to  adapt  to  the  increasingly  emerging  crises,  such  as natural  disasters  including  floods,  resulting  from  climate  and  weather  conditions  of  a  special intensity.  Gaps in the organization of information flow between different levels of governance and  institutions  involved  in  emergency  response,  the  lack  of  coordination  between  crisis management structures and the competent institutions, including local authorities, as well as the weaknesses in the operation of the  coordination centres - all cause a substantial discomfort.

The  traditional  forms  of  crime,  such  as  theft,  burglary  or  robbery,  are  accompanied  by  the development  of  organized  crime,  applying  more  advanced  technologies  and  techniques. Computer crimes are a new and increasing phenomenon, such as: computer fraud, identity theft, manipulation of balance sheets, computer espionage, computer piracy and sabotage, violation of intellectual and industrial property. All these phenomena have a negative impact primarily on the economic market and on the possibility to carry out business in accordance with the principles of fair  competition.  New  trends  in  combating  threats  to  public  safety  indicate  the  need  for prevention  of  losses  resulting  from  these  crimes,  which  significantly  affect  the  development potential  of  individuals,  of  society  and  of  the  state.  Therefore,  reducing  the  losses  caused  by crime may be an important source of further financial reserves to be used on pro-growth policy of the State.

Analyzing the national security issues and the related tasks, it is also necessary to indicate the scale of deaths in traffic accidents in Poland (one of the highest rates in Europe). Environmental crime,  including  environmental  pollution  and  destruction  of  wildlife,  including  the  protected species, etc. represent a significant threat.

Despite  those  obstacles,  there  exist  capabilities  and  conditions  with  the  potential  for  the development of an efficient and effective state. They include: entrepreneurship of citizens, their motivations and aspirations to create conditions for optimal development and for high standard of living, relatively high level of education, sustainable and stable democratic governance system that  allows  for  developing  modern  democratic  governance,  increasing  activity  of  citizens  in decision  making  and  in  the  exercise  of  power,  as  well  as  physical,  economic,  population  and socio-cultural resources of the country ensuring the continuity of development processes. Given the  above  circumstances,  the  state  will  create  system  solutions  (legislation  and  institutional mechanisms  as  well  as  other  instruments)  and  real  opportunities  for  promoting  the  public interest and for satisfying the needs and aspirations of as many citizens as possible, including the entrepreneurs.

40

[[END OF PAGE 40]]

[[START OF PAGE 41]]

##### Priority directions of public intervention:

###### I.3.1. Improving the efficiency of justice

The judicial  reform  shall  be  consequently  continued,  with  the  aim,  first  of  all,  to  increase  the efficiency, the quality and the effectiveness of the national judicial system, taking account of the wide spreading use of modern information and communication technologies. This will result in better guarantees for effective enforcement of the rights of the citizens, including entrepreneurs, before common courts, thus increasing public confidence in the courts. It is essential to improve and simplify the procedures. This should lead to shortening the duration of proceedings and to consistent  law  enforcement  in  all  cases  of  violations.  The  measures  taken,  including  the implementation of existing normative solutions, aim and will continue to aim at streamlining the judicial proceedings and at liquidating structural backlog, particularly in courts where the number of such backlogs is the largest. Continuous efforts taken to simplify and improve the procedures for  civil  and  criminal  litigation,  as  well  as  the  digitization  of  judicial  proceedings  and  of  office management will play a significant role in improving the public image of judicial system.

It is particularly important for the justice system to be consistent for the citizen and pervious at each stage of legal proceedings, starting from the operating authorities and public prosecutor's offices,  through  courts,  lawyers  and  other  participants  of  the  proceedings,  and  ending  with appeals  bodies,  including  international  courts  (ECJ,  and  others).  It  is  equally  important  is  to monitor the functioning of the law and to ensure fast response when the law is infringed; it is also important to ensure that balanced and proportionate penalties (including administrative) are inevitable.  All  this  will  foster  building  the  public  confidence  in  the  state  as  well  as  in  its  law enforcement structures and in its administration. It will, therefore, be necessary to increase the effectiveness  of  cooperation  and  coordination  between  these  institutions.  They  should  assist citizens and provide them with a complete sense of legal security.

Effective management of the courts requires improving their organizational structure to ensure an efficient use of personnel. In courts, functions related to law enforcement and legal protection will  be  separated  from  administrative  functions  involved  in  providing  conditions  for  law enforcement and legal protection. The allocation of responsibilities among persons in charge of judicial structures shall also be revised in order to facilitate the implementation of the rule of law and order.

In  order  to  facilitate  access  to  justice,  actions  will  be  required  facilitating  access  to  the  legal profession, in particular to that of a judge; this will be achieved i.a. through a movement of highly skilled  persons  from  other  legal  professions.  Also  the  quality  of  postgraduate  practical  legal should be improved. Provision of better access to legal information and legal services, including access to legal aid for the poorest people, and the constant increase in the availability of judicial decisions shall  be  another  important  area  for  changes.  Mechanisms of  amicable  settlement  of disputes, including mediation, shall be extended and promoted.

A  comprehensive  legal  education  model  shall  be  developed  and  implemented,  taking  into account  the  new  role  of  legal  professions,  as  well  as  training  of  administrative  staff  and introduction of elements of economic analysis of law and management as compulsory subjects. Solutions  will  be  introduced  in  the  area  of  professional  career  of  judicial  staff,  with  effective evaluation being the basis for determining their career paths. The training and motivation system for  professional  judges  and  prosecutors  will  account  for  the  skills  necessary  for  their  reliable cooperation with other courts, in particular those from other EU countries.

Changes will be necessary in the prosecution organizational and procedural system, so that it can act  as  a  well-organized  and  professional  division  of  state  apparatus,  equipped  with  proper instruments  and  guarding  the  rule  of  law  in  close  cooperation  with  the  law  enforcement authorities, courts and tribunals. The transformations undertaken shall cause a higher ministerial, pro-social commitment of this body of justice, which will carry out its official duties with realistic understanding of the interests of the citizens and of legal entities as overriding. At the same time, the prosecution authorities should reconcile this approach with the requirements of legality in the interpretation and application of legal norms - in a proper and consistent manner.

41

[[END OF PAGE 41]]

[[START OF PAGE 42]]

Actions  aiming  at  strengthening  the  position  of  the  victims  of  crimes  and  at  raising  public awareness of their rights, as well as at adequate training of services having contact with these persons, and at developing cooperation mechanisms for various actors and institutions assisting those people, shall all improve the image of the justice system.

In  order  to  improve  the  efficiency  and  effectiveness  of  judicial  executions,  actions  will  be continued  to  deformalize  and  speed  up  the  enforcement  proceedings,  and  to  increase  the availability  of  judicial  enforcement  as  well  as  the  effectiveness  of  administrative  judicial supervision over court bailiffs and judicial proceedings.

It is essential carry out the necessary organizational and procedural reforms in terms of services of court-appointed curators to create a professional probation service. It is also planned to take actions to reduce  the  prison population  and  to adjust  the  number  of  inmates  to  the infrastructural capacity of the Prison Service, to promote the penalties alternative to imprisonment, and to increase the scope of and the possibility to use electronic monitoring.

IT  solutions  will  be  introduced  consistently,  which  will  significantly  facilitate  the  functioning  of the  courts  and  will  allow  for  creating  an  electronic  database  of  cases,  thus  improving  the management  of  the  judiciary  system  and  increasing  its  efficiency.  The  quest  for  the  full digitization  of  justice  will  entail  the  introduction  of  electronic  transmission  of  documents  and electronic  case  files,  allowing  for  the  greatest  possible  mutual  electronic  contact  between  the court  and  the  parties.  Natural  and  legal  persons  will  have  access  to  on-line  services,  allowing them to use the contents of court records on-line, and to apply for copies from court registers via the internet, and copies such obtained shall be regarded as official documents. Access shall be created  to  clear  and  transparent  legal  information  in  the  form  of  a  system  for  providing information  via  helpline  and  on-line.  Citizen  -  judiciary  system  of  electronic  communication methods  (e-courts)  shall  be  introduced  and  court  proceedings  shall  be  conducted  using  new technologies (e-hearings).

###### I.3.2. Social Capital Development

The development of social capital is one of the key challenges of Poland and serves to increase the scale and stability of civic engagement and cooperation, among others by increasing citizen participation in the governance and their greater involvement in formulation and implementation of national development policy. Actions taken in this area should be treated as a long-term investment, having in mind that the state should only create the conditions for this process, favouring bottom-up initiatives of civil society.

Given the barriers described above, to stimulate the development of social capital the state will, first of all, promote the attitude of active, informed citizenship, as well as pro-business and proinnovation attitudes. Actions will also be taken to increase the scope and results of the activity of civil society.

Changes will be introduced in school curricula, based on integrating or increasing the range of issues  related  to  civic,  cultural  and  media  education,  and  on  strengthening  the  activities enhancing creative thinking and the ability to cooperate. The curriculum will be complemented with practical aspects of civic education, for example, the role of voluntary bodies in dealing with important  local  issues,  as  well  as  with  elements  related  to  economic  education,  such  as  for example the subject of social entrepreneurship. The proposed changes should be introduced as a part of general curriculum from the earliest grades, and their effects should be included in school certificates. These changes will be accompanied by adequate training programs for teachers to help them develop the students' teamwork skills using new technologies.

Support for citizenship education in teaching other than formal will also be an important element of  the  education  process.  It  is  particularly  important  to  create  appropriate  legal  regulations fostering the development of informal civic education and extend such education to cover people who have completed their formal education, including those aged 50 +.

Participation  in  culture  will  be  promoted  through  an  open  access  to  the  digital  heritage resources, through developing culture and shaping basic culture skills, including the ability to use digital media by all citizens. In urban areas, the activities increasing the supply of culture will be mostly  supported,  while  in  rural  areas,  emphasis  will  be  put  on  improving  access  to  cultural services and their quality.

The  importance  of  lifelong  learning  as  an  integral  part  of  changes  in  the  system  of  citizen education will increase. The idea of volunteering as well as the cooperation of volunteers with community organizations will be promoted in order to strengthen civic society, especially among 50+ citizens and among the residents of rural areas. It will also be important to launch the longterm  volunteer  programs,  particularly  for  people  who  have  completed  secondary  education (comprehensive or technical  secondary  schools),  as  well  as  to  implement  employee  volunteer programs in the offices of local and central government.

Actions aimed at greater involvement of citizens in the legislative process will promote public participation. Measures will be introduced to facilitate voting in general elections, such as voting by Internet, extended voting time, or polling stations and voting system adjusted to needs of the disabled. The inclusion of citizens in the legislative process will be done primarily by defining and implementing  general  principles  for  public  consultations  with  a  wider  use  of  digital  tools, including  online  consultations,  by  open  access  to  public  information  and  knowledge,  by increasing the possibility of exploiting civic initiative at the local level, and by building a platform for  exchanging  information  and  experience  related  to  the  activities  of  the  State.  Modern methods of public debate will be developed, particularly in relation to issues of high complexity (like  the  development  of  nuclear  energy),  which  will  consist  in  combining  information  and educational campaigns with an open debate involving the widest possible cross section of society rather than the narrow expert circles. Modern, innovative methods of building social consensus on development policy will be introduced, making use of such instruments as the deliberative poll, foresight, social pact. It will be imperative to strengthen the importance of the institution of civil  and  social  dialogue  and  to  raise  the  awareness  of  administrative  staff  on  its  role  in  the processes of management of national development.

43

[[END OF PAGE 43]]

[[START OF PAGE 44]]

To provide a better model of public policy making and of the law making process, social dialogue between government, employers' organizations and trade unions must be effective and it must give the social partners a real sense that they have an impact on state policy. For this reason, it is necessary to further strengthen the institution of dialogue and to raise the level of knowledge in this area. A well-functioning social dialogue enhances social peace and helps to develop solutions fostering innovativeness of the Polish economy.

Expanding the scale of activities of NGOs and creating better legal conditions for their operation by,  among others,  supporting  the  development  of  social  entrepreneurship,  introducing  publicsocial  partnership  and  allowing  the  use  of  existing  infrastructure  for  the  purposes  of  civic initiatives, will support the potential and the activities of NGOs. The simplification of the law will be  an  important  element  in  facilitating  the  possibility  for  citizens  to  organize  and  undertake actions to achieve common objectives.

Active local communities will be strengthened through the creation of public space or making it available,  among  others  by  constructing  new,  or  making  use  of  the  existing,  infrastructure elements of culture and sport (libraries, community centres, sports clubs), and by promoting the existing statutory mechanisms, such as local initiatives. District centres, whose the activity helps to  integrate  the  residents of  rural  areas,  will  be supported  as well as  the  sports organizations. This particularly applies to sports clubs, which have a significant potential to associating people and  are  a  source  of  creation  and  promotion  of  extensive  positive  experience  with  a  view  of building social capital. In addition, measures will be taken to identify communities that explicitly require external support.

The  role  of  public  media  will  be  redefined,  so  that  their  main  task  will  be  to  fulfil  the  public mission  of  creating  a  platform  for  public  debate,  disseminating  reliable  information  and knowledge, promoting citizenship, and thinking in terms of public interest.

Also the role played by churches and religious associations in the processes of social integration, identity building and creating active social attitudes will be significant from the point of view of cooperation under the social capital development programs.

###### I.3.3. Enhancing citizen security

Due  to  the  fact  that  the  threat  of  loss  of  life  in  traffic  accidents  in  Poland  is  still  one  of  the greatest  in  Europe,  there  will  be  programs  launched  to  improve  road  safety,  correlated  with infrastructure  improvement  and  with  traffic  rules  awareness  and  education  campaigns,  and promotion of safe behaviour on the road. The most important tasks will be, among others, to establish  and  develop  automated  traffic  surveillance,  to  develop  an  integrated  system  of management of accidents as well as an integrated system of passenger and freight handling, to improve  forms  and  channels  of  communication  with  the  public  as  regards  road  safety,  to promote Intelligent Transport Systems, and to create a system of financing of investment in road safety.

At the same time, relocation of police and prosecutor's staff shall take place, necessary to reverse the  negative  trends  in  vulnerable  areas  and  regions,  and  the  number  of  staff  and  amount  of equipment  shall  be  increase,  if  necessary.  Alternative  crime  prevention  methods  will  be  used (non-police),  including  cooperation  with  the  research  and  private  sectors,  central  and  local administration, civil society and the media with a view to improve the effectiveness in combating crime,  both  common  crime,  the  most  burdensome  kind  for  the  residents  of  the  country,  and organized crime, resulting in great losses of the State Treasury (including through  the establishment  of  an  interdepartmental  co-ordinating  body,  conducting  strategic  and  planning activities,  in  compliance  with  the  Internal  Security  Strategy  of  the  EU  and  with  other  strategic programmes that provide for including crime prevention in the state strategic policy).

45

[[END OF PAGE 45]]

[[START OF PAGE 46]]

Actions will be intensified in the field of health security. It will be important to ensure access to health  care,  along  with  an  adequate  level  of  these  benefits,  which  requires  a  change  in  the financing  policy  of  medical  care  and  a  reasonably  deployed  and  well-developed  health  care infrastructure. A health care system shall be worked out and implemented that will ensure its effective functioning in a situation where public funding is limited. It is necessary to develop a new  funding  model,  taking  into  account  the  supply  of  medical  care  at  private  expense.  In constructing the system changes, demographic challenges and lifestyle diseases will be taken into consideration in the first place.

In  view  of  the  demographic  challenges  over  the  next  decade,  emphasis  will  be  put  on  those segments of health care that will - on the one hand - promote fertility and health prevention of the youngest part of population (infertility treatment programs, support to extend the promotion of  prenatal  diagnosis,  appropriate  standards  of  care  during  childbirth,  and  maternal  and  child health care), and - on the other - will be responsive to the epidemiological challenges of an aging population  (better access and  quality of geriatric, oncology, cardiovascular, neurological, orthopaedic,  rheumatologic  and  endocrinological  services,  as  well  as  the  long-term  care). Particular emphasis should be put on the development of human resources in health care (there should be incentives to encourage students to specialise in priority areas and to study subject studied by an insufficient number of students) and of health infrastructure. Moreover, in view of the steadily declining number of professionally active nurses and midwives, and of a significantly declining interest in training for those professions, the system of basic and postgraduate training of nurses and midwives will be tailored to the needs in terms of staff in this field.

Areas and measures to prevent and reduce the growing incidence of lifestyle diseases, such as diabetes,  mental  diseases,  orthopaedic  disorders  and  effects  of  addiction,  in  particular  among children and adolescents, shall be developed, and they shall be supported by medical and nursing care  at  school.  Fight  against  cancer  and  against  cardiovascular  diseases  will  continue  to  be treated as a priority. Due to the growing number of transport  injuries and a high percentage of traffic deaths, as well as permanent disabilities due to the lifestyle diseases, there is a need to pay  particular  attention  to  building  a  modern,  efficient,  integrated  system  of  cooperation between  emergency  services.  Good  functioning  of  the  National  Emergency  Medical  Services, defined as a structure of closely cooperating units, is of particular relevance in this context.

The  activity  of  the  state  will  be  focused  not  only  on  treatment  but,  first  and  foremost,  on prophylaxis and disease prevention. These will include increasing health awareness and healthy lifestyle through promotion and health education (especially among those with the lowest socioeconomic  status),  encouraging  civic  participation  in  the  form  of,  for  example,  volunteering  of elderly  people,  and  increasing  the  level  of  physical  activity  of  the  population,  in  particular  of children and adolescents.

In order to improve the functioning of the system, it would be necessary to introduce a digitized health care system (e-health). Medical services database shall be created as well as Poles' health database. The doctor will have access to complete patient history; this will seal and improve the system which will become more transparent and functional. Consequently, the efficacy of health services will grow.

Further consolidation of food security will be important - maintaining production potential (i.a. maintenance of  the  soil  in  good  agricultural  condition),  as  well  as  agricultural  production  and food supplies in the quantity and at the quality strategic level necessary to feed the population. At the same time, actions aimed at ensuring food market safety, through increasing the quality of agricultural and food products (food production by integrated, organic, traditional and regional methods and from local raw materials will be promoted), will receive high priority. State will also take care of raising the knowledge and awareness consumers on of healthy nutrition and disease prevention. Additionally, initiatives will be supported, which aim to raise other products safety level (for example, toys, cosmetics, as well as electrical and electronic equipment).

46

[[END OF PAGE 46]]

[[START OF PAGE 47]]

The above topics are inextricably linked to environmental security, understood as counteracting the negative effects of restructuring and of environmental pollution, and thus helping to improve health and the quality of life of the society as a whole. The system for monitoring compliance with  environmental  law,  currently  functioning  in  Poland,  is  fragmented  and  inefficient.  Taking into  account  the  interest  of  citizens,  actions  will  be  taken  to  strengthen  and  improve  the performance of environmental protection agencies in Poland.

In  connection  with  ascribing  an  important  role  to  the  digitization  process  in  future  socioeconomic development of Poland, the IT infrastructure and public/private IT systems will become an  increasingly  important  area  of  concern.  Their  protection  against  accidental  threats and deliberate  attacks  requires  the  development  of  network  monitoring  systems,  as  well  as  early detection  of  threats  for  the  security  of  the  network,  prompt  response  to  threats  emerging threats,  and  the  identification  of  perpetrators.  In  view  of  the  fact  that  public  institutions increasingly  use  digital  technology  to  achieve  their  objectives,  one  of  the  key  issues  will  be  to provide them with a secure digital infrastructure.  Also a proper preparation of the users of IT systems,  i.a.  through  actions  aimed  at  increasing  their  awareness  on  ICT  security,  will  be important - it will enable the users to minimize on their own the risk resulting from such threats.

A priority interest of the state is to increase its ability to respond effectively in crisis situations and  to  address  situations  that  may  pose  or  pose  a  risk  to  life  and  health  of  citizens,  to  the environment and to property, that is, first of all, to improve and develop the emergency response system. To this end, it will be necessary: to develop and improve cells constituting the rescue system, including the modernization of equipment, proper selection and training of personnel, and  adequate  financing.  It  will  also  be  necessary  to  develop  and  properly  maintain  the forecasting, warning, alerting and notifying systems to ensure their good performance, including in the field of nuclear safety. Specialised activities will include, among others, the implementation of risk management plans for risks of natural origin and risks caused by human activity. The best possible use should be made of personal and material potential of Polish armed forces for crisis management system. To increase the effectiveness of emergency response it will be required to couple the efficiency of state institutions with the civic capacity to cooperate, resulting from the social capital. It is necessary to increase citizen involvement in crisis management: shaping public awareness  of  their  responsibility  for  own  safety  and  for  environment  safety,  and  promoting appropriate  behaviour  in  an  emergency  situation. This  implies  the  need  to  redefine  the mechanisms of action and interaction between state actors and civil society in the processes of both  the  threats  and  their  effects  immediate  removal.  It  is  also  planned  to  organize  legal regulations related to civil protection.

Strengthening  the  cross-border  aspects  of  internal  security  has  become  Polish  interest  of  an increasing  importance.  In order to  prevent,  reduce and  effectively eliminate  the  security  risks, cooperation  will  be  developed  with  the  services  of  neighbouring  countries  and  with  relevant international organizations.

It  has  become necessary to integrate and to coordinate better the activities of the institutions responsible for internal security (including in terms of public order and public security), having the power to carry out preventive measures, including introducing the required changes to the system of civil protection. The system of internal security should enable an effective response to major  threats,  such  as  natural  disasters  and  catastrophes,  organized  crime,  corruption  and terrorism, as well as to common crime, which is the most troublesome for citizens. Ultimately, it is assumed that a coherent, integrated and complementary system of state security (internal and external) will be created, linking security with development of the country.

###### I.3.4. Consolidation of national security

The strategic development of the Polish security policy for the next decade will be characterized by  a  comprehensive  approach.  Main  areas  of  activity  will  cover  both  international  areas  and safety-critical fields of activities in the country. It is assumed that work on the integrated system of state security shall be undertaken and completed fast.

The main tasks of Polish security policy include protecting and ensuring the following: the duration of the state as the institution, the society and the civil community; territorial integrity of the country; political independence and sovereignty; proper functioning of state institutions; internal stability, as well  as  comprehensive  and sustainable socio-economic development, enhancing the level and the quality of life.

Poland  is  not  facing  any  military  threat  at  the  moment,  while  being  exposed  to  the  dangers  of  a various  nature  coming  from  both  the  international  environment  and  the  internal  system.  They include: economic challenges and threats, ecological and biological hazards, IT hazards (cybernetic), transnational organized crime and terrorism.

Polish  participation  in  the  NATO  and  in  the  European  Union  enhances  national  security,  ensuring better defence of national  territory. The participation  in  NATO  involves  readiness  to  collectively defend all of the Allies, also through participation in external operations, as well as continuous improvement of the capability of the Alliance to achieve its tasks. Membership in the European Union  gives  Poland  a  chance  of  permanent  and  sustainable  socio-economic  development  and  of participating in the creation of the capacity of common emergency response and of common defence under the Common Security and Defence Policy.

Further  professionalization  and  modernization  of  Polish  armed  forces  (especially  expanding  the operational capabilities which are key for the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland to perform their tasks effectively), as well as building and strengthening the high prestige of professional army, also through disseminating knowledge on the army, and on actions enhancing international security, and on civilian actions for the benefit of the community - they will all be important.

49

[[END OF PAGE 49]]

[[START OF PAGE 50]]

Legal  conditions,  investment  incentives  and  institutional-coordinating  mechanisms  will  be  created fostering the development of advanced equipment and technologies in military-defence, which could also be used in increasing the level of modernity of the economy of Poland.

Further improvement of the Polish border monitoring system will be implemented. These borders are at the same time the external borders of the EU. This would require, among others, support of border and customs services. At the same time, a comprehensive migration policy will be developed.

EUROPE 2020: Guideline 1 Ensuring the quality and the sustainability of public finances Guideline 2 Addressing macroeconomic imbalances

### Strategic area II. Competitive economy

#### Objective II.1. Strengthening macroeconomic stability

Macroeconomic policy is what determines socio-economic and territorial development. It creates the basic conditions for business activity of citizens and of business entities, for their investment and consumption decisions. It can minimize or increase the risk of engaging in economic activity as well as the related costs; it can reduce or increase the price that businesses, households and individuals pay due to cyclical economic fluctuations. Reasonable macroeconomic policy reduces the susceptibility of the economy to "external shocks", and with greater stability of the economy, it minimizes the spread between the periods of recession and of boom.

On the other hand, the role of development policy is to create conditions for medium-and longterm economic growth, prosperity, as well as social and territorial cohesion. Given the scale of the impact of the two policies on businesses and on citizens, it is extremely important for them to be complementary and mutually supportive. This means that the task of macroeconomic policy is to create the best possible framework for the implementation of development activities under other  public  policies,  whereas  development  policy  must  be  conducted  so  as  not  to  stand  in contradiction  with  the  objectives  of  macroeconomic  stability  and  with  the  objectives  of  the policy-mix.  Therefore,  it  is  necessary  to  take  into  account  the  interdependencies  between  the activities for macroeconomic stability and the planned interventions in different areas achieving public development objectives.

Development  policy  will  be  affected  by  the  situation  of  the  European  economy.  In  turn,  the inherited  starting  point  -  the  strengths  and  weaknesses  of  the  Polish  economy  and  the opportunities and threats it will have to face - will determine the actions undertaken, particularly in  the  first  years  of  MTNDS.  These  actions  may  determine  the  direction  which  the  Polish development will follow in the coming decades.

In  the  ten-year  horizon  of  MTNDS  implementation,  Polish  development  policy  will  face  a challenge  of  reconciling  two  perspectives.  On  the  one  hand,  it  will  be  necessary  to  support processes allowing to maintain the current rate of eliminating the development gap in relation to the  more  developed  economies  of  Western  Europe,  and  to  flexibly  adapt  to  the  emerging internal and external challenges. On the other hand, the implementation of MTNDS will need to address the urgent reform challenges, which will also lay foundations for achieving the objectives set out in the long-term development strategy of the country.

The  challenges  resulting  from  international  context  and  from  the  situation  of  Polish  economy must  be  responded  to  through  ensuring  macroeconomic  stability  of  the  country,  including through improving the public finances and preparing for full participation in the third stage of the Economic and Monetary Union. In the context of development objectives in the long run, it is necessary  to  ensure  the  funding  for  the  increased  expenditure  on  infrastructure,  science  and health, as well as to redefine the direction of economic incentives arising from the tax system and public spending, so that the saving rate and the gross investment rate would increase by about 3-5 percentage points, and the rate of employment of people aged 20-64 would grow to 71% in 2020 and would maintain the upward trend over the next decade to eventually exceed 75%.

In the first place, that is prior to 2015, the economic policy will focus on macroeconomic stability, on  securing  the  sources  of  future  funding  of  modernization  of  the  country  and  on  laying  the foundation for future growth. In the years 2016-2020, the emphasis will be placed on making use of  previously developed advantage and on accelerated modernization of the country, not only through expenditure on infrastructure, but also on science and education. In a broader context, it is necessary to account financial plans for the necessary increase in spending on health and on pensions.  This  means  that  reforming  public  finances  and  increasing  the  rate  of  savings  and  of investment are the tasks that need to be completed before 2015, and continued in the 20162020 period.

51

[[END OF PAGE 51]]

[[START OF PAGE 52]]

##### Priority directions of public intervention:

###### II.1.1. Recovery of public finances

The reforms necessary to achieve the priority objective - public finances recovery - will create the so-called outcome-income mix, and as such will consist in increasing savings discipline and at the same time rationalizing budget expenses and ensuring increased revenues for the state budget. One of the steps taken in this direction was the introduction of changes in Open Pension Funds.

The  purpose  of  changes  undertaken  will  not  only  be  to  reduce  the  public  deficit  but  also  to ensure the sustainability of changes that have been achieved (achievement and maintenance of the MTO), to secure the pro-development investment, and to increase incentives for investments, savings and work.

Limiting  budget  expenditure  should  not  affect  the  areas  that  are  key  for  achieving  the development  goals  agreed  upon  (investment  in  education,  science,  research  and  innovation, culture,  health,  transport,  communication  infrastructure,  and  environment),  but  should  be focused  on  inefficient expenditure  (transfers), in particular  those  expenses  that  have  a demotivating impact on undertaking and maintaining employment. In this context, measures will be taken to reduce sub-sector specific entitlements in the pension system, and to increase the efficiency of social benefits in case of illness, maternity, disability or accidents at work. It is also important to develop and implement solutions that lead to a gradual raising of the retirement age and to equalising the pensionable age between men and women. Changing the system of financing  inefficient  sectors  of  the  economy  is  an  important  task.  Thanks  to  the  introduced spending  rule  which  will  apply  until  the  excessive  deficit  procedure  is  repealed  and  which assumes that the discretionary and new rigid expenditure of the budget cannot grow faster than 1% per year in real terms, the expenditure on public administration and on employment in the public sector will be rationalized, as well as other elements of public consumption. In particular, the rationalized system of purchasing goods and services in central government administration, introduced in 2011 will be developed further, based on the functioning of the central unit for central government purchases (Shared Services Centre).

Increasing the budget revenues will be possible thanks to changes in the tax system, which would consist  of  widening  the  tax  base  -  all  social  groups  will  ultimately  be  covered  by  the  same taxation rules, and in sealing and rationalizing the tax system.

The effective implementation of MTNDS activities will foster fiscal consolidation by raising the average rate of economic growth in the period 2012-2020. If the medium-term economic growth in  Poland  is  around  3.5%,  it  may  be  assumed  that  achieving  and  maintaining  the  MTO  would prevent further increases in the public debt.

###### II.1.2. Increasing the rate of domestic savings and investments

In order to quickly alleviate the gap in GDP per capita, Poland has to invest more, to adapt new technologies more quickly and to increase economic activity.

Increasing  the  level  of  savings  and  of  domestic  investment  as  well  as  boosting  innovation throughout the economy is of key importance for the development opportunities that Poland will have  in  the  period  until  2020  and  beyond.  The  achievement  of  high  economic  growth  in  the coming decades will be conditioned by Poland joining the knowledge-based economies, and this in turn will depend on the expenditure on research and development and on investment in the capital-intensive,  modern  sectors  of  economy.  This  will  be  fostered  by  gradual  changes  in  the structure  of  public  expenditure  towards  reducing  the  negative  balance  between  the  current expenditure - consumerist or transfer in nature, and the expenditure on pro-development areas. The use of EU funds will allow for achieving a higher growth in gross fixed capital formation in the coming  years,  but  in  the  long-term,  it  would  be  crucial  to  increase  the  rate  of  private  and domestic savings.

Thus,  it  is  important  to  create  incentives  to  increase  savings  and  to  search  for  investment opportunities based on external funds.

###### II.1.3. Integration with the euro area

Stable level of economic equilibrium is a state when the predictability of the occurring processes is  high,  and  thus  the  danger  of  rapid  changes,  resulting  from  both  internal  and  external conditions, is limited. A higher level of equilibrium implies: stable public finances, stable prices (their  increase  does  not  exceed  a  certain  range,  which  is  "safe"  for  the  economy),  small fluctuations in the exchange rate of national currency, and low interest rates. These elements are the basic criteria of Maastricht, and meeting them is a condition for any EU country to enter the euro area.

Measures  taken  for  the  recovery  of  public  finances  will  allow  Poland  to  meet  the  fiscal convergence  criterion,  but  actions  will  also  be  needed  in  other  areas  of  the  economy.  The intention is to integrate Poland with the euro zone as fast as possible. This process, however, should be conducted in a manner that maximizes the benefits and reduces the risks related to adopting the common currency. The date of adopting the euro has not yet been determined due to the considerable uncertainty as to when will the formal conditions be fulfilled for the adoption of the euro, and also due to the unstable situation in the euro area.

The experience gained during the crisis of the euro area showed that adopting the euro may be very  beneficial,  but  it  may  also  involve  a  high  risk.  This  depends,  among  others,  on  proper preparations and on the way of conducting economic policy in the country, including economic reforms which contribute to sustainable fulfilment of the nominal convergence criteria. Reforms enhancing the competitiveness of economy, and in particular, reform of the product market and of  labour  market,  are  an  integral  part  of  Polish  integration  strategy  with  the  euro  zone.  The introduction of the euro in Poland can bring direct benefits, such as reduced exchange rate risk and smaller costs of foreign exchange transactions, decreased nominal interest rates, and greater price transparency. In the long-term, the participation in the euro area  promotes  the intensification  of  trade,  fosters  investment  and  enhances  integration  of  financial  markets.  In addition to development benefits, arising from the very membership in the euro area, also the significance of political benefits of participation in the common currency zone has increased.

###### II.1.4. Developing export of goods and services

Nowadays,  the  global  economy  becomes  more  open,  and  the  flow  of  goods  and  services becomes  increasingly  easier.  To  benefit  from  these  processes  and  to  avoid  technological backwardness, Poland should adopt an active, export-oriented policy. The existing competitive advantages, based mainly on low labour costs and cheap raw materials, need to be replaced with new assets. The transformation of Polish exports composition, and also of the structure of added value  created  is  a  key  factor  determining  whether  Poland  will  benefits  from  globalization  and from its continued high pace of economic development.

Developing  export  potential  of  the  Polish  economy  is  important  for  several  reasons.  Export creates additional production and increase in employment. It is  important to be able to achieve a positive balance in foreign trade or to improve it. Simultaneously, a strong competitive pressure mobilizes business entities, leading to a more efficient use of production factors. Export also has some beneficial effects in terms of internal diffusion of knowledge, skills and standards - from the exporting companies - through business partners - to the entire national economy.

To  be  able  to  compete  on  international  markets  requires  the  development  of  pro-innovation orientation, based on new technologies, including in particular technologies corresponding to the model  of  sustainable  and  environmentally  friendly  development.  An  equally  important  role  is played  by  information  and  communication  technologies.  This  direction  entails  the  need  to engage highly skilled and better-paid employees. At the same time, it positively affects the use of available labour resources.

Diffusion  of  knowledge,  including  the  hidden  one, is  particularly  intense  within  the  cluster structures,  in  which  business  entities,  business  environment  institutions,  local  authorities  and research and educational institutions join their forces in building a competitive advantage. The development of clusters  in  areas  of  Polish  product  specialities  can  and  must  be  an  important lever for the development of Polish exports, both in high-technology sectors (ICT, engineering, aerospace and automotive) and traditional sectors (fashion, amber, yachts and motor boats, agrifood industry) as well as in services (creative industries, film, medical services and others).

55

[[END OF PAGE 55]]

[[START OF PAGE 56]]

The  instruments  fostering  the  growth  of  export,  supporting  Polish  companies  in  entering  new markets and promoting Polish goods and services, will be developed over the next decade. The system  for  promotion  of  the  national  economy  shall  be  strengthened.  The  entrepreneurs  will benefit  from  high-quality  information  services  provided  by  specialized  operators.  The  system supporting the identification of trading partners will be extended. Assistance will be provided in overcoming barriers to entering the market, such as obtaining the required certificates, permits and licenses.

Mechanisms  supporting  the  operation  of  domestic  companies  on  foreign  markets  will  be implemented  and  made  better  use  of.  Loans,  insurance  and  export  guarantees  will  be  made available.  Specific  solutions  in  this  scope  will  be  addressed  to  SMEs.  In  Poland,  like  in  other countries, a specialized bank should be established, focusing on the promotion of exports and offering this kind of financial instruments.

Support capacity will be tailored to the specific needs of exporters. The competence of staff in the  exports  development  will  be  expanded.  Managerial  and  marketing  studies  will  equip graduates  with  the  knowledge  necessary  to  operate  on  international  markets.  Appropriate educational programs will be prepared. Knowledge on the existing support instruments and on the practical benefits of their use will be promoted.

Internationalization of Polish enterprises will also be promoted. Entering foreign markets should be accompanied by establishing representative offices, branches and subsidiaries. Joint ventures with  foreign  partners  can  deliver  significant  benefits  for  both  the  economy  and  individual companies.

Activation  of  exports  will  be  a  major  priority  in  economic  relations  of  Poland  with  other countries. Economic policy will be an important element of foreign policy.

Direct foreign investment will continue to play an important role in the development of exports. Conditions  will  be  created,  at  national  and  local  level,  to  encourage  the  influx  of  foreign investment  to  Poland.  Large  corporations,  while  locating  their  production  facilities  as  well  as service  or  research  centres  in  Poland,  grant  access  to  international  distribution  channels  and provide global marketing support.

#### Objective II.2. Growth of the efficiency of the economy

Recent  years  have  highlighted  the  progressive  changes  in  the  structure  of  the  economy, measured by the shares of economic sectors in the gross value added created. The importance of the services sector, especially of market services, has been gradually increasing (64.8% in 2010), while  the  share  of  agriculture  has  dropped  (3.5% in  2010).  The  process  of  modernization  of manufacturing sectors continues, however, the pace of structural changes is too slow, which has an impact of the GDP growth and on the prosperity of the Polish population.

In order to move towards a civilizational leap, it is assumed that a shift will be made from the existing structure of the economy to a knowledge-based economy.

Although the industry will be dominated by medium-tech sector, the sectors of high-tech and of services with the highest added value will record the fastest rates of growth.

Agriculture  will  experience  evolutionary  development,  based  on  systematic  raising  the  added value attributable to each employee. The structural changes will lead, among others, to stronger links between the farms and the market. In order to achieve competitiveness in the market, the quality  of  food  will  be  crucial  -  it  will  replace  the  existing  competitive  advantage  of  Polish agriculture associated with low production costs and low prices.

In  the  context  of  achieving  this  objective,  the  level  of  state  intervention  gains  particular importance. Both, a too low and a too high level of state intervention may disrupt the natural free  market  competition.  Hence  the  important  role  of  the  state  as  the  creator  of  appropriate legal,  administrative  and  security  conditions  as  well  as  of  adequate  infrastructure  for  business development, and of a signpost showing the directions for development of industry. An example of active state policy will be public support for the most innovative Polish clusters, showing the greatest  development  potential,  and  able  to  create  highly-competitive  products  and  services, which are Polish and European export specialties.

##### Priority directions of public intervention:

###### II.2.1. Raising the productivity of the economy

The low level of capital and labour productivity in Poland is caused by an outdated structure of production, dominated by raw material industries and by production characterised by low-level of  processing.  Although  structural  changes  have  been  taking  place  since  the  90's  of  the  XX century, namely the importance of agriculture and of traditional industries have been declining while the importance of market services has been growing, these changes remain insufficient.

To  make  the  economy  more  productive,  it  would  not  be  enough  to  only  reduce  the  costs  of labour. The most important task in the coming years will be to make changes in the structure of the  economy,  so  that  the  manufacturing  sector  would  be  dominated  by  industries  that  use advanced technologies, that exploit the latest advances in microelectronics, in materials science or in computer science. Also the development and use of IT technologies in all of the sectors / industries of the economy will be of great importance, in particular in the new areas where these technologies  have  not  yet  been  used.  Modern  industries  contribute  substantially  to  the productivity growth of the economy, not only thanks to their high productivity but also to their favourable impact on other industries.

The  development  of  high  technology  and  its  wide-spreading  use  in  enterprises  also  leads  to changes in the employment structure, enhancing growth of labour productivity. The demand for unskilled labour keeps decreasing, while the demand for workers having high skills, knowledge and experience keeps increasing.

An important task to be performed under MTNDS 2020 will be to continue privatization of state enterprises and to introduce changes in the financing of the ineffective state sectors / industries /

57

[[END OF PAGE 57]]

[[START OF PAGE 58]]

departments, in order to gradually decrease their production, and to re-train and transfer their staff to departments with higher potential productivity.

In  the  second  period  of  MTNDS  2020  implementation,  namely  in  the  years  2016-2020,  the potential  of  state  will  be  directed  at  supporting  industries  and  services  with  the  highest productivity potential. Until then, it will be necessary to train the employees properly, so that the labour market could quickly respond to the increased demand for highly skilled labour force.

During the whole period of strategy implementation, also actions aimed at increasing the work productivity  level  will  be  supported,  namely  changes  in  the  way  of  training  of  the  staff,  and greater  focus  on  learning  foreign  languages  and  computer  operating  skills  at  all  stages  of education.

In order to ensure sustainable economic development of the regions, local economic systems will also be supported to ensure that local resources and human capital for are used for production and local products are sold as close to the place of manufacture as possible.

###### II.2.2. Increasing the share of medium- and high-tech industries and services

The  structure  of  production  in  Poland  is  still  unfavourable  in  comparison  with  the  highly developed  countries.  It  is  true  that  recent  years  have  brought  positive  changes  (decrease  of production  in  heavy  industry,  increased  highly  processed  production),  but  the  economy  is  still dominated by the low-processed goods industries whose production requires a relatively large quantity of materials, labour and energy, and a small share of knowledge and innovation.

In  2009,  the  share  of  medium-  and  high-  tech  industries  in  the  production  sold  in  industry amounted to 31.7%. 9

To succeed in catching up with the developed countries, it is necessary to institute changes in the economy that will enable an efficient adaptation of the world's most productive technologies and the  implementation  of  own  modern  innovations.  This  would  be  enhanced  by  optimizing  the structure of public funding mechanisms and by improving the efficiency of funding R&D&I , inter alia by limiting the scale of non-reimbursable intervention instruments primarily to financing R&D

> 9 Source: CSO.

58

[[END OF PAGE 58]]

[[START OF PAGE 59]]

and social and breakthrough innovation. Financing of other innovations will be based to a greater extent  on  the  financial  engineering  instruments  (co-financed  by  private  capital),  directed primarily to private entities for specific projects.

To stimulate the growth of medium- and high-tech industries it is necessary to support research and  development  activities.  Supporting  the  processes  of  commercialization  and  protection (especially  patents,  licenses,  etc.)  of  intellectual  property,  which  adds  value  to  research  and development, will become a priority.

Changes in the economy should be effected in two ways - on the one hand, in the direction of rapid modernization of the internal structure of the industry towards high-technology products, and  on  the  other  hand  -  in  the  direction  of  intensive  implementation  of  ICT  into  the  service sector.  Such  changes  are  possible,  inter  alia,  by  supporting  development  of  the  best  Polish clusters which, thanks to joint projects with clusters from other EU countries, contribute to the creation  of  innovation,  to  the  diffusion  of  knowledge,  and  to  the  increase  in  the  degree  of internationalization of entities that are parts of such clusters.

In  a  market  economy,  the  impact  of  the  state  on  the  actions  taken  at  the  market  should  be limited to what is necessary, not to disturb the existing competition. Therefore, the role of the state will principally be limited to creating conditions for the development of medium- and hightech industries and services.

One  of  the  main  activities  in  this  area  will  be  for  business  environment  institutions  (BEI)  to strengthen the local capacities and the so called smart specializations, in particular to support the creation and development of new technology companies. In the context of medium and highly advanced technologies, BEI will be particularly important, including: technology transfer centres and resorts, research and development institutions and technology and industrial parks. Also all BEI providing financial support will become very important.

In the first period - until 2015 - the economy will continue to be modernized primarily as a result of  the  import  of  advanced equipment and license purchases due to the still low share of own research  and  the  limited  financial  resources  dedicated  for  this  purpose.  After  this  period,  a widespread development of own abilities to create new technological  solutions  is  assumed to occur.  It  is  particularly  important  to  promote  new  technological  solutions,  which  would  be conducive to the safety of the citizens (consumers) and the public.

Another  measure  aimed  at  the  development  of  high  technology  industries  is  the  creation conditions encouraging foreign investors to invest their funds in Poland, in order to develop hightech sectors.  R&D investments of the green-field type are particularly preferred.

In  order  for  the  implementation  of  the  above  described  activities  to  be  possible,  a  proper preparation of personnel for medium- and high-tech industries is necessary. It is important to increase the number of people with scientific education - educated in technical, mathematical and interdisciplinary fields.

###### II.2.3. Increasing the competitiveness of and modernization of the agri-food sector

Further transformation of the agri-food sector will be aimed at boosting its competitiveness and efficiency. Measures will be implemented to stimulate the modernization and improvement of innovation and profitability of the agri-food sector, to strengthen the environmental and public functions  of  agriculture  and  fisheries  and  to  support  the  diversification  of  income  sources  of households involved in agriculture or fisheries. Structural  changes,  such  as  increased  area  and economic size of farms and increased employment of the population leaving the agriculture in non-agricultural sectors of the economy will also contribute to the creation and development of small and medium enterprises and to the formation of entrepreneurial attitudes among the rural population.  These  activities  are  important  both  in  the  period  until  2020  and  the  period  until 2030,  because  the  structural  changes  in  agriculture  are  slow, and  the  problem  of  hidden unemployment cannot be solved in the short term.

Stimulation of the development and implementation of research, support for the formation of clusters and for dissemination among farmers and fishermen of information on modern technical and  organizational  solutions  through,  among  others,  professional  consoling,  will  be  also  an important element of this efforts.

The rational management of resources in agriculture and fisheries requires above all promoting the  continuation  of  good  agricultural  practices  and  support  of  such  forms  and  methods  of creation  of  manufacturing  space,  which  promote  environmental  protection  and  integrated development of agriculture.

60

[[END OF PAGE 60]]

[[START OF PAGE 61]]

Competitive advantage of agriculture is conditioned by the operation of farms focused on mass production and high quality products. Therefore, conditions will be created for the development of  various  forms  of  farms,  in  accordance  with  the  dominant  factors  shaping  the  conditions  of development -both current and anticipated in the future.

The introduction of these measures will result in increased participation in the international food market - not only due to price competition but also thanks to the quality and innovation of the products.

###### II.2.4. Improving conditions for the operation of businesses

The increasing number of small and medium enterprises helps to strengthen the competitiveness and efficiency of the economy. In order for this number to increase steadily, the conditions for business  operations  need  to  be  improved,  as  they  often  make  it  more  difficult  for  the  small enterprises to enter the market.

Poland was at a distant, 70th place among 183 countries surveyed by the World Bank in 2010 in terms of the business environment (113 in terms of ease of starting a business) 10 . Therefore, in the first period of the MTNDS implementation (until 2015) it is necessary to reduce the artificial burdens  and  administrative  barriers,  including  the  simplification  and  consolidation  of  the  tax system, and in the second period (after 2015) - to fully implement the digitalization of public services  (e-government),  so  that  the  entrepreneur  would  be  able  to  resolve  most  of  the administrative issues on the Internet.

The  importance  of  non-cash  transactions  will  increase,  which  will  reduce  costs  of  running businesses and will increase the security of transactions. By 2020, non-cash turnover will become a dominant form of payment.

> 10 Source: Doing Business 2011, Making a Difference for Entrepreneurs, World Bank, Washington 2011.

61

[[END OF PAGE 61]]

[[START OF PAGE 62]]

Electronic  forms  of  economic  activity  will  greatly  expand  in  the  second  half  of  this  decade, together with the accelerating development of electronic commerce, which will allow for small and medium enterprises to reach the market more easily, thus increasing their market coverage.

Development of the infrastructure - especially transport infrastructure, which facilitates business access  to  new  markets  (suppliers  and  buyers,  as  well  as  various  types  of  resources)  and accelerates the implementation of business processes - will simplify the business activity. Despite the  fact  that  certain  costs  have  already  been  incurred  and  numerous  investments  have  been started - Poland lags far behind the developed EU countries. Connections between major urban centres, connections leading to these centres, and connections within their functional areas as well  as  connections  increasing  the  accessibility  of  rural  areas  will  be  built  or  upgraded  in  the following  years.  This  will  allow  for  the  diffusion  of  the  economic  growth  of  the  most  vibrant centres towards the current peripheral areas and will affect the competitiveness of regions and of the entire economy. At the same time, initiatives will be supported that encourage the use of products  in  the  areas  of  their  production,  so  as  to  eliminate  the  need  of  transport  over  long distances.

Leisure patterns change with the increasing wealth of society and with changes in the economy. Many people are increasingly eager to use the so called leisure industry in their free time - they are increasingly willing to spend their free time and holiday away from home. Therefore, it will be important to create adequate conditions for the development of companies organizing leisure and tourism (including cultural and creative businesses). This applies to both to the areas that have a  high  tourist  potential  due  to  their  natural  advantages,  and  also  to  urban  areas,  which often have too few leisure opportunities in relation to the existing demand for such services.

#### Objective II.3. Increasing the innovativeness of the economy

In  a  global  economy,  stimulated  by  the  ongoing  parallel  processes  of  trade  liberalization  and rapid technological progress - organizational and marketing competitiveness should be the major source of Poland strong competitive position. For this purpose it is necessary, among others, to implement an economic policy allowing for a gradual transition from a model of technological development based on the ability to absorb and adapt solutions created in the leader-countries toward a strategy based primarily on creating and exploiting own knowledge.

The actions taken so far in order to build the innovative potential of the Polish economy have not produced  satisfactory  results.  Despite  some  progress,  Poland  -  in  comparison  with  other countries - still has to make up a considerable distance. The biggest problems are the size and structure  of  research  and  development  funding,  especially  private  research  and  development funding, and poor cooperation between science and business. The R&D expenditures in Poland amount to about 0.7% of GDP annually, which represents about 1/3 of the EU average. 11 Only a small  percentage  of  companies  are  innovative  and  benefit  from  the  protection  of  intellectual property (a small number of patents).

It is assumed that over the next decade a significant increase in innovation in the economy will take place. A broad perception of innovation will be promoted, not only in the technology and product  areas,  but  also  in  organizational,  process  and  marketing  dimensions,  including  in  the services sector. Conditions will be created for implementing innovation in business and thus for building a strong competitive position on Polish, European and global markets.

Progress  in  this  area  will  be  possible  due  to  a  broad  spectrum  of  activities  involving  the modernization  of  public  systems  aimed  at  strengthening  research  and  innovation,  ensuring  a favourable  regulatory  environment,  supporting  the  development  of  financing  instruments, concentrating of funding on pro-quality tasks in higher education and research institutions of the highest calibre, and  creating attractive perspectives for education, training  and  careers. Modernization  activities  addressed  at  the  improvement  of  the  functioning  of  the  sector  of science  and  of  higher  education  will  include  a  greater  integration,  as  well  as  better  use,  of opportunities  arising  from  participation  in  the  European  Research  Area  and  European  Higher Education Area.

Development  activities  addressed  by  public  intervention  should  correspond  to  the  specific characteristics  and  capabilities  of  each  region.  Because  the  innovations  are  created  and  used primarily in urban centres, characterized by high capacity in knowledge, science and economy, these will be the focus of the major part of the effort involved. Warsaw has to play a special role in  this  regard,  it  should  become  a  dynamic  centre  of  innovation  and  an  important  place  for economic decision-making in Europe. Other regional centres and major cities will develop their specialties  based on  the  local  potential,  often  reinforced  by  foreign  investment.  Investment  in education  facilities  will  create  conditions  for  strengthening  regional  potentials,  favouring  the development of human capital, and thus innovation and creativity.

Progress in innovation, closely linked with its diffusion to various regions and sectors of economy and society, will acceleration the development of the entire country, including specific problem areas. Strengthening the academic centres in less developed regions through close cooperation with the best national universities and research institutes will be particularly important.

##### Priority directions of public intervention:

###### II.3.1. The increase in demand for research results

Increasing  the  competitiveness  of  Polish  economy  requires  much  more  activity  in  the  field  of research and development conducted by the native companies. The main barrier for the greater use  of  innovation  by  companies  is  the  high  cost  of  development  and  implementation.  In  the coming years, conditions will be created for stimulating private sector participation in financing this type of activity.

> 11 Source: CSO.

63

[[END OF PAGE 63]]

[[START OF PAGE 64]]

Increasing companies' interest in R&D and implementation of innovations will contribute to the development of instruments for their financing. The market model, with the dominant role of the mechanisms  of  credit  and  Venture  Capital  funds,  will  be  the  priority.  However,  until  these institutions become fully developed and efficient, state sponsored activities involving alleviating market  imperfections  will  be  required.  In  order  to  support  the  broadest  possible  group  of beneficiaries and to ensure the restoration of financial resources, revolving mechanisms will be applied.  Guarantee  instruments  will  also  be  developed,  activating  the  resources  of  private financial institutions. The potential of public-private partnerships will be broadly used. Initiatives will be undertaken to engage foreign capital. Instruments to stimulate private investment will be accompanied by a further increase in public spending.

The increase in demand for the results of R&D will be influenced by the mechanisms of operation and funding of national research institutions based on market mechanisms. It is assumed that together  with  increased  professionalism,  efficiency  and  excellence  of  the  work  carried  out  in these institutions, private sector interest in their offer will increase. Such a state of affairs shall result  from  a  well  developed  system  of  parametric  evaluation  of  research  units.  Linking  their funding to the quality and efficiency of research performed, including a widespread use of the competition system, will become a rule. The next step would consist in the commercialization of research  results,  promotion  of  close  relations  with  the  market,  and  creation  of  efficient transmission  mechanisms  between  science  and  the  economy.  Simultaneously,  funds  will  be distributed using a competition system, open both to private and to governmental research units. Such  a  mechanism  would  enhance  the  potential  of  the  top  research  teams,  force  them  to compete for funds, improve the quality of scientific work and ensure that the results obtained will be relevant to the investments incurred and to market demand.

An  important  additional  instrument  for  increasing  the  demand  for  innovation  will  be  the institution of public procurement. Government administration and local government entities are important  actors  in  the  market,  and  their  spending  may  significantly  incentivize  the  private entities  to  conduct  R  &  D.  Public  procurement, particularly in areas such as transport, energy, environment,  health,  education,  information  and  communication  can  create  a  significant potential for creating innovative solutions. It shall be started by the public authorities, which will formulate  their  orders  in  a  way  encouraging  increased  participation  of  new  technologies  and innovations in the goods and services purchased.

Financial  incentives  will  work  together  with  other  regulatory  solutions,  creating  favourable conditions for taking  up  activities  related  to  research  and  development.  Such  activities  will  be encouraged  by  the  presence  of  open  and  competitive  markets.  Therefore  initiatives  aimed  to facilitate business and promote the freedom of enterprise will be continued. Monitoring of the market in  parallel  with  monitoring  of  the  regulatory  should  be  continued,  so  that  no  rules  or standards  would  prevent  the  development  and  introduction  of  new  technologies  nor  the emergence of new markets.

Understanding  the  significance  of  innovation  in  terms  of  the  effective  operation  of  business enterprises and in terms of gaining competitive advantages is an essential element in creating demand for it. Efforts will be undertaken for building culture of innovation among entrepreneurs. Promoting  awareness  of  the  benefits  of  implementing  continuous  improvements  in  market activity, in products, in processes, and in methods, as well as in the use of new technologies will affect  the way the entire economy works. Such efforts should be accompanied by diffusion of skills related to planning and implementation of business development strategies.
