###### II.3.2. Increasing the degree of commercialization of research

In the construction of knowledge-based economy a major challenge for the coming decade is to increase  the  links  between  the  science  sector  and  entrepreneurs.  Knowledge  developed academic  scientific  and  research  centres  should  be  converted  into  commercial  success  to  a greater extent than before.

The primary task of the state will be to create an appropriate regulatory environment stimulating cooperation between universities, businesses and financial institutions. A need arises, therefore, to disseminate among entrepreneurs the information about the opportunities for R&D funding and  on  the  possibilities  of  cooperation  with  specialized  entities  or  academic  institutions conducting such activities. Government's key partners in this regard will be business associations and business organizations. The scientific community will be encouraged and mobilized, at the same time, to respond to the needs of research for the industry.

The instruments addressing the issue of inter-sectoral mobility of workers will play a key role in strengthening the links and partnerships between business and universities. These instruments will contribute to overcoming the differences frequently separating research from the needs of the business sector.

65

[[END OF PAGE 65]]

[[START OF PAGE 66]]

In this context, it is important to induce a deep change in social attitudes and cultural elements, so as to make them be more favourable to innovation.

Overcoming the differences between the business community and the researchers, as well as the fragmentation  of  markets  for  knowledge  and  technology  will  make  the  transfer  of  knowledge more effective. It  is  assumed  that knowledge  and  intellectual  property  management  units  will emerge  at  the  universities  in  the  first  half  of  the  decade,  and  that  in  the  second  half  of  the decade  they  will  practically  exist  everywhere.  Such  units  will  be  supported  by  innovative academic enterprise centres.

These actions will be based on further improvements in the quality of education. An essential element of the process of modernization of universities will be the increased role and increased quality  of  research  carried  out  in  university  centres.  These  endeavours  should  largely  be interdisciplinary or trans-disciplinary, and much of them will be implemented in cooperation with foreign partners and on behalf of companies. The number of foreign lecturers and students shall increase. The relations between regional universities and local communities shall improve.

Transfer of knowledge, especially through the cooperation of R&D representatives with business -  a  potential source of income for scientific units - should contribute to increasing the quantity and quality of research.

However, during the implementation of the strategy, the number of studies funded with public monies  will  still  be  significant.  In  the  allocation  of  research  grant,  projects  based  on  the cooperation  between  scientific  entities  and  economic  actors  will  be  preferred.  An  important criterion  for  the  selection  of  projects  will  be  their  degree  of  support  for  innovation  and  the possibility of practical implementation of their results. Resources will be concentrated thematically to priority directions of scientific research and development, reflecting the needs of economy and society. Local potentials will be supported, creating the so called smart specialties, as well as the R&D Infrastructure projects aimed at empowering national research units within the scope of the European Research Area.

The  competitiveness  of  the  economy  will  be  significantly  affected  by  commercialization  of research results. Towards this goal, the system of research support and research results diffusion will be improved. The key elements of such an improvement will be the following: conditionality of support grants, identification of key areas of support, marketing and promotion of research results  (including  abroad).  This  approach  will  help  to  build  new  offerings  based  on  market research. Undertaking the research work in collaborative areas or on the basis of contracts with enterprises will be promoted.

One of the important ways to increase the degree of commercialization of research results will be increasing the awareness and knowledge of the persons involved regarding the possibilities of patent protection of innovations and the benefits of patents. Research units (in particular those whose  R&D  activity  is  financed  from  public  funds)  will  implement  management  strategies  for intellectual  property  protection  in  order  to  provide  adequate  legal  standing  for  the  results  of their work and to facilitate the commercialization of innovations. Basic courses on entrepreneurship, innovation and management processes related to intellectual property rights should  be  available  in  all  areas  of  academic  studies.  It  is  also  necessary  to  provide  timely, affordable  and  predictable  patent  litigation  procedures.  Instruments  will  be  established  to support  national  entities  and  natural  persons  not  engaged  in  business  or  science,  to  obtain protection beyond the Polish borders for inventions, utility models and industrial designs.

###### II.3.3. Providing human resources for R & D

Building a knowledge based economy, combined with the ambitions for Polish economy to gain a competitive  advantage  resulting  from  innovation,  require  undertaking  of  a  diverse  set  of measures  aimed  at  ensuring  adequate  human  resources  for  R&D.  Effective  planning  and implementation of innovative production processes as well as effective marketing of innovative products require adequately trained professionals. Efforts directed to increase the level of higher education will be accompanied by measures to promote further education and training.

Development of human capital in R&D will be supported. It is essential to improve the quality of higher  education  but  also  to  increase  the  effectiveness  of  teaching  at  the  early  stages. Mathematics  and  science  skills,  the  inventiveness  and  the  ability  to  search  for  independent solutions will be developed at all stages of education; moreover, cooperation habits of will be formed. Curricula and teaching methods will be tailored to the current state of knowledge and to technological progress. Efforts taken to ensure that the university graduate is equipped with the knowledge, skills and competencies that are desirable on the labour market will be supported. Education will become more attractive to young people because conditions will be created for the development of their creativity and individual abilities.

67

[[END OF PAGE 67]]

[[START OF PAGE 68]]

The  number  of  students  at  publicly-funded  studies  in  maths,  science  and  engineering  will increase significantly, compared to the number of other students. This will be accompanied by actions  to  encourage  women  to  study  sciences.  Students  will  be  equipped  with  the  skills necessary  to  commercialize  research  results  and  to  manage  intellectual  property  rights.  Skills required for research projects and interdisciplinary cooperation will be formed. The relationships between  universities  and  businesses  will  be  developed,  including  through  internships,  joint research,  joint  development  of  educational  programs  and  promoting  (for  example,  through grants) fields of study that are essential for the development of the country. The opportunities of postgraduate  education  and  improvement  of  skills  will  be  increased.  Foreign  specialists  lecturers, research staff and managers - will be recruited. The access to international information resources  will  be  granted.  Universities  will  be  provided  with  modern  technical  equipment required for education at the highest level. Mechanisms will be introduced to compete for funds, promoting the top universities.

Assistance in profiling universities as academic or vocational will be important - this should be done  taking  into  account  the  directions  of  development  reflecting  the  potential  of  the  region where  a  given  university  operates.  Those  science  branches  will  be  developed  where  local research units and companies are among the leaders in Poland, in Europe or in the world. This should  be  accompanied  by  exploring  the  possibilities  of  success  in  areas  specific  to  local  or regional economies, as well as in areas conducive to building new advantages on the basis of the existing potential.

If  Poland wishes to strengthen its position in the field of science, technology and innovation, it should  attract  more  high-class  scientists  and,  first  of  all,  it  should  fully  exploit  its  own  human resources.  The  aim  will  be  to  create  such  a  system  of  recruitment  and  financing  of  doctoral programs  that  would  ensure  that  the  best  graduates,  with  abilities  and  high  motivation  for scientific work, will have the optimal conditions to continue academic career. Foreign scholarship programs will be implemented for young people holding a doctoral degree to allow them to gain experience in the renowned research centres and conditions will be created enabling them to work in the best national research centres.

###### II.3.4. Increasing the use of innovative solutions

In the coming decade, profound reforms of the system of promotion of innovation and R&D will be carried out. In the actions undertaken, the different potential of different sectors and regions has  been  taken  into  account.  The  growth  centres  operating  in  sectors  and  in  regions  will  be strengthened, and conditions will be created fostering the spread of development processes.

These efforts will be accompanied by establishing a creative-cooperative technological model of learning,  with  high  capacity  to  generate  own  solutions  and  to  absorb  the  foreign  ones,  and enabling to assume the role of international leader, at least in certain technological niches. The links between  local, regional and national scientific institutions will be developed and strengthened,  as  well  as  their  participation  in  international  networks  (European  and  global). Forecasting and analytical tools will be used regularly for the elaboration of priorities and plans for the development of science and technology, including using the foresight method.

Effort will be made to promote development of products and services in areas where Poland may become a leader on the European and on a global scale, namely in industries with high potential demand.  Such  specialised  areas  may  include  eco-innovation,  whose  importance  in  energy efficiency and in supplying clean and secure energy will significantly grow over the coming years (including renewable energy sources and clean energy, based on fossil fuels).

Local self-government units will become important partners in the formulation and implementation  of  innovation  policy.  The  Regional  Innovation  Strategies,  which  are  the  basic instrument for defining smart specializations of voivodeships, will become crucial in this scope. Conditions will be created to enable local authorities to take on a leadership role in integrating local academic, scientific and economic circles, as well as to coordinate the activities of entities operating in regional systems supporting innovation.

Business  environment  institutions  that  support  innovation  are  expected  to  strengthen  and develop.  Business  entities  supporting  academic  entrepreneurship  will  develop  their  activity  at universities. Formation of spin-off companies will be promoted. The efficiency and effectiveness of  technology  transfer  centres,  technology  incubators,  science  and  technology  parks,  and  of centres  of  advanced  technology  will  be  increased.  Entities  supporting  the  maximum  use  of creative  business  potential,  such  as  training  and  research  centres,  financial  institutions  and consultancy in innovation and intellectual property, as well as local and regional development agencies and other support organizations will be created and improved. Access to their services will  be  enabled.  A  platform  to  share  knowledge  will  be  created.  Effective  mechanisms  of searching for research projects that can be commercialized will emerge. A system of technology brokers will be created.

69

[[END OF PAGE 69]]

[[START OF PAGE 70]]

Cooperation  between  market  participants  will  be  promoted.  Support  instruments  will  be introduced aimed at joint venture companies. The scale of cooperation will increase, allowing especially  SMEs  -  to  create  solutions  and  use  technologies  unavailable  for  them  as  for  single units.

Effectively operating  clusters will foster competition.  They  will contribute to  connecting businesses and research institutions and to combining their resources, thus bringing knowledge faster into the market. They will cause the productivity to increase, will attract investors, support further  research,  strengthen  the  industrial  base,  and  will  lead  to  the  development  of  specific products and services. Clusters will also  attract  industrious  and  talented  individuals.  Economic entities operating in clusters will have an important competitive advantage. Cluster structures, as an effective mechanism of focusing resources and means, may become growth poles across the region. Businesses  operating  within  clusters will gain greater capacity  to seek  potential opportunities  for  foreign  expansion,  through  establishing  cooperative  relations  on  a  supraregional and transnational level. The functioning of clusters will be based on market participation model, with the leading role of the private sector in their initiating, funding and management.

Development of innovation requires an adequate infrastructure basis. To build such basis is a task for  the  next  decade.  Both  national  resources,  as  well  as  resources  from  EU  funds  should  be involved. The existing technical infrastructure for the development of innovation shall be built and strengthened, especially in regions endowed with sufficient potential in this scope. Particular attention will be paid to ensuring an easy and affordable access to broadband, which is especially important for the development of knowledge-based entrepreneurship.

The sales of goods and services with added value, created by intellectual property, keeps growing constantly.  In  modern  economies,  creative  industries  will  soon  have  the  same  significance  as traditional industries. Particular attention should be paid to the development of cities, which are the natural environment for the creative industries. Cities need to become attractive places to live in and to invest in, they should be an inspiring space, and should ensure that cultural heritage is  maintained  and  cultural  potential  is  developed;  they  should  also  be  open  to  new  cultural phenomena. This is the only way cities can attract and retain active and creative individuals.

#### Objective II.4. Human capital development

One of the key factors determining the development of the country and its competitiveness is the  quality  of  human  capital.  Development  (quantitative  and  qualitative)  of  human  capital  is understood  as  the  development  of  national  and  local  labor  markets  as  well  as  productivity growth of employees, and - consequently - as growth of production and services, development of innovation, increase in the productivity of the economy, and raise in the level and standard of living of all citizens.

Factors that will adversely affect the quantitative development of human capital, especially in the long-term, and that should be taken into account in advance by the active policy of the state, are mainly  the  demographic  factors.  The  following  factors  are  of  major  importance  for  the development of human capital in the context of demographic challenges: low fertility, increasing share  of  post-production  age  group  in  total  population,  negative  migration  balance,  low professional activity of the senior generation. According to available demographic forecasts, the number of Polish population will decline steadily. The dynamics of this decline will increase after 2015,  when  the  proportion  of  the  population  of  working  age  will  drop  significantly. 12 CSO forecasts predict a loss of working-age population of about 2 million people in the period 20102020.

Moreover,  territorial  diversification  of  the  existing  demographic  trends  will  become  more noticeable, also in terms of population density and of changing population density in individual Polish areas (areas of depopulation, areas of concentration of the population). These phenomena will constitute a significant risk for the development and a challenge over the coming years.

Among the main barriers to growth in Poland, two should be distinguished, namely a low rate of employment (64.6 %, 20-64, 2010) 13 and  the  mismatch  of  education to  labour market  needs. Poland is a country with one of the lowest in the EU levels of economic activity. The workforce is characterized  by  poor  mobility,  mismatch  of  the  supply  and  demand  structures  for  labour  by qualification,  low  labour  market  flexibility,  early  age  of  professional  deactivation,  diverse employment age for men and women, low profitability of work in relation to social benefits, low female employment rate, low employment of the disabled, and poor adaptation of curricula to the needs of labour market. An active policy of the state will, therefore, be focused on the areas constituting a barrier to growth in employment.

The most important features of human capital that increase  its  employability are:  knowledge, qualifications  and  professional  skills.  The  level  of  human  capital  is  largely  determined  by  the quality of education, including higher education and scientific research. Knowledge is becoming an  essential  factor  in  the  development  of  society,  and,  consequently,  of  the  state  and  the economy. It is, therefore, important to invest in the wider education policy and research, with the highest rate of return, although it is postponed.

According  to  forecasts  of  the  European  Centre  for  the  Development  of  Vocational  Training (CEDEFOP),  until  2020  Poland  will  face  a  growing  demand  for  highly  skilled  workers,  while demand for medium- and low-skilled labour force will drop. 14 In  this  context, the promotion of adult  learning  becomes  particularly  important,  especially  in  the  form  of  short  courses  and  at work. The rate of the lifelong learning in Poland is significantly lower than the EU average. The creation  of  a  cohesive  system,  under  which  the  labour  market  and  education  policies  will  be developed, will be accompanied by the development of lifelong learning and learning in all of the life roles.

> 12 Source: CSO, Eurostat.

> 13 Source: CSO.

> 14 Source: Source: Skills supply and demand in Europe: medium-term forecast up to 2020, publication of Cedefop 2010, pages 63-67.

72

[[END OF PAGE 72]]

[[START OF PAGE 73]]

Identifying the barriers and territorial potentials properly is essential for the activities supporting the development of human capital to be successful. Specific features of individual areas, resulting from,  i.a.,  their  socio-economic  functions,  such  as  urban  areas,  rural  areas  and  interactions between them, determine the directions in which regional labour markets as well as skills and competencies of human resources develop.

Also the increased awareness of entrepreneurs in the field of human resource management will positively affect the development of human capital.. The entrepreneurs, knowing how to manage their personnel, will use human capital better and more effectively.

##### Priority directions of public intervention:

###### II.4.1. Increasing labour force participation

Tasks  of  the  state  will  focus  on  reducing  disincentives  to  employment  or  to  undertaking  and maintaining  a professional activity. Efforts  are  needed  to  support  and  stimulate  labour participation by creating solutions that enable and facilitate the reconciliation of work and family life.  One  of  the  major  activities  in  this  area  is  to  increase  the  availability  of  various  forms  of institutional  care  for  children  and  to  build  an  effective  system  of care  for  dependents  and  the elderly. Simultaneously, efforts will be taken to increase the attractiveness of working as a source of income in comparison to alternative sources, especially to social benefits, so that the system would be aimed at promoting active job search and would increase the motivation to take up legal employment.

Special  focus  will  be  put  on  actions  aiming  to  enable  a  proper  career  start  of  young  people entering the labour market (promotion of the transfer between education and employment, so that  graduates  would  quickly  undertake  work  or  return  to  employment,  improvement  of  the profitability of the first job - changes in the system of social benefits, flexibility of employment and stability  of  employment  conditions,  renewable  seasonal  contracts).  Simultaneously  efforts will be supported to improve the quality of higher education, to promote undergraduate studies as sufficient to take up and pursue activities on the labour market, and to promote short forms of training.

Conditions will  be  created  to  increase  the  number  of  people  with  disabilities  employed  in  the open market, including, i.a., gradual increase in the participation of disabled children in public education (school infrastructure, qualified teachers), modernization of protected labour market and of its role, ensuring universal access to high quality rehabilitation, changes in the funding of support  for  employment  of  disabled  people,  adaptation  of  public  transport  for  persons  with disabilities.

The  progressive  aging  of  the  population  makes  it  necessary  to  extend  the  working  life  of  the Poles.  Systemic  reforms  will  be  introduced  in  the  system  of  social  benefits  (including  social security),  resulting  in  postponing  the  retirement  age  (including  the  limitation  of  specific entitlements in the sector pension sub-system) and in equalising the pensionable age between men and women. These measures will be accompanied by promoting older employees among employers  (for  example,  showing  strengths  of  older  employees  and  breaking  old  stereotypes) and  by  system  solutions  to  promote  employment  of  older  people.  Support  measures  will  be introduced to enable people aged 50+ to gain new knowledge and to supplement the knowledge they have in educational institutions, including institutions of higher education; this will allow for keeping older people in the labour market. Such measures will contribute to the activation of this group of people, both on the labour market and in social debate.

Actions favouring flexible forms of employment will aim to increase labour force participation. Increasing labour market flexibility should be accompanied by ensuring security in the framework of  flexicurity  in  its  four  pillars  -  flexible  and  predictable  conditions  of  contracts,  concept  of learning throughout life, active labour market policies, and modern social security system. These efforts will be very important because analyses show that Poland has one of the worst record among the European Union countries in implementing flexicurity 15 .

73

[[END OF PAGE 73]]

[[START OF PAGE 74]]

Also  the  elimination  of  barriers  associated  with  establishing,  conducting  and  developing  own businesses will promote the increase in labour market participation.

An integrated approach to development implies that - in addition to systemic changes - measures should be introduced to respond to the challenges of regional labour markets, in compliance with the nature and the needs of individual voivodeships and accounting for the differences between urban and rural areas. Guidelines for public intervention will take into account the situation and potentials  of  regional  diversity,  including  the  condition  and  quality  of  human  capital  and  the needs of the regions.

Moreover, changes in the model of operation of Public Employment Services (PES) are necessary, consisting principally in a departure from registration and payment of benefits related activities as the basic function of PES towards active recruitment. Activation services should be separated from  those  responsible  for  the  registration  of  the  unemployed  and  for  the  redistribution  of benefits.  This  requires  a  redefinition  of  the  rules  and  a  development  of  a  new  model  of cooperation  with  employers,  who  have  the  most  reliable  information  about  the  demand  for labour, as well as with other labour market institutions, both public and operating in the private sector. An important element of the system changes will consist in synchronizing the activities of public employment services and social assistance services.

The objective pursued in state policy by 2020 is to increase the employment rate to 71% (this objective is also included in the National Reform Programme - a document implementing directly the EU 2020 objectives).

###### II.4.2. Improvement of human capital

The challenges associated with intelligent, sustainable and inclusive growth, including the need to increase employment, faced by modern states and by their citizens, are conditioned to a large extent by the quality of human capital. What determines the quality of human capital, in turn, is the level of competence and skills of the citizens 16 . Improving the quality of human capital refers to  the  situation  where  citizens  gain  skills  and  qualifications  to  respond  to  the  needs  of  socioeconomic  and  personal  development  -  from  the  youngest  to  the  oldest  years  of  life  and  in different ways - in schools and in other institutions of formal education, but also in their jobs and thanks to community involvement, learning on their own and from each other.

It is important in this context to make a transition from the education model based primarily on school  training  and  on  transferring  encyclopaedic  knowledge  to  a  wide  education  model, combining  school  teaching  with  learning  other  than  formal,  i.e.,  lifelong  learning  and  learning hands-on (accounting for the specific needs of employers and the needs resulting from living in the information-driven society). In this model, education is not only associated with teaching in schools  and  institutions  of  higher  education,  but  also  with  training,  which  is  ubiquitous  and organized by a wide spectrum of entities active in the economy and in civil society, and whose status is equivalent to formal education. In this model, it is not only ministries and government departments that are responsible for educating citizens but also the social partners operating in the  economy and in  civil  society.  This  broad  approach  to  education  may  contribute  to  solving faster the problem of mismatch in labour supply and demand in its qualification and professional dimension,  which  can  be  observed  on  the  Polish  labour  market.  It  can  also  increase  the attractiveness of graduates as employees.

> 16 State of competence and qualifications of people in Poland and the priorities for improving their levels are presented in more detail in the strategic document arising from commitments in the European Union; The prospect of learning throughout life.

75

[[END OF PAGE 75]]

[[START OF PAGE 76]]

These changes would involve the need to adopt a new, more effective direction of investment in education (education and training), including the following: organizing and developing the system of  recognition  of  qualifications  acquired  outside  the  system  of  formal  education  (validation system); implementing systems for storing and transfer of information on educational achievements, consistent with European systems; implementing national qualifications framework 17 ;  incorporating  individuating  mechanisms  in  education  and  training  programmes; adjusting  educational  offer  to  the  specific  needs  of  learners  of  all  ages  and  of  the  needs  of employers; expanding the use of information and communication technologies (ICT) in education and training; developing 'distance' learning and training; developing educational and vocational counselling; reforming the scoring in competence examination systems; promoting new forms of adult education based on a system of validation.

These activities will be complemented by a new approach to the organization of early childhood education and care - not oriented to replace parents, but to support them in bringing up young children  using  more  diverse  forms  of  education  and  care  that  currently  available,  including  in particular  those  exploiting  the  potential  of  small  flexible  initiatives  organized  locally  without major investment.

In such an approach, the new investment trends in education and training will be supported by the  implementation  of  the  national  system  for  forecasting  labour  demand,  integrated  with  a system of information about the offer and the quality of schools, colleges and courses as well as with a career guidance system, to allow the learner to choose his/her educational investment wisely. With no such system in place, the anticipative actions, as well as the immediate actions to balance the market - will not be very effective. Measures to improve the quality of human capital need  to respond  to the challenges of regional labour markets, matching  their specific characteristics and needs. It is important, in particular, to improve the quality of schools offering vocational training and at the same time to promote vocational training among young people and other  stakeholders,  and  also  to  encourage  the  development  of  practical  training  in  higher education. Rationalising the selection of educational path will result in a better match between supply and demand sides of the labour market, raising the growth potential of the economy.

In the context of the changing needs of the labour market, a priority task of the state will be to provide equal educational opportunities at all stages of education and training, and to improve of the  quality  of  educational  services.  The  reform  of  education  and  training  will  be  focused  on enhancing their  quality  and  efficiency  to  respond  to  the  needs  of  knowledge-based  economy. Support  provided  in  this  area  will  contribute  to  an  effective  management  of  education,  to improved training  curricula  supplemented  with  innovative  elements,  and  to  strengthening  the effectiveness of training and education personnel. At the same time, changes will be introduced in the system of evaluation, remuneration and promotion of teachers that will support remaining in  the  profession  of  the  best  teachers  and  their  relevant  motivation,  and  will  increase  the attractiveness of the teaching profession to young people. It is necessary to enrich the curriculum with competencies and skills needed in the future work. It is also important to make changes in the functioning of schools accounting for the development of civilization - on the one hand - the increased  economic  activity  of  parents  /  carers,  on  the  other  -  the  need  to  include  modern elements in the curriculum, such as civic education, development of creative thinking and the ability  to  cooperate, engagement in important local issues, and development of digital literacy and social skills. Developing these competencies should also be an important element of higher education.

> 17 Organizing and defining levels of qualifications regardless of the place, manner and time of learning.

76

[[END OF PAGE 76]]

[[START OF PAGE 77]]

It is important for the state policy to encourage having children, ensuring at the same universal access to quality education, including early childhood education. Investment in human capital of the youngest translates in the long-term into tangible benefits in the form of a higher level of prosperity. Citizens  should  have  universal  access  to  quality  education  throughout  life  -  from various forms of early education until the old age (such as universities of the third age), and the task  of  Polish  schools  should  not  only  include  general  education  but  also  preparation  of graduates  to  meet  the  needs  of  the  labour  market. Hence  there  is  a  need  to  adapt  curricula accordingly,  to  introduce  a  new  model  of  vocational  training  based  to  a  greater  extent  on practical  learning,  on  flexible  learning  paths,  and  on  promotion  of  vocational  and  technical education; and in the area of higher education - to introduce practical training profile, raising the skills and knowledge of students in the sciences of key importance for the economy, to promote cooperation  between  the  institutions  of  education  and  the  business,  as  well  as  with  the  R&D sector and with the civil sector (NGOs).

Efforts will also be taken to better exploit the potential of universities, in particular, to support education in the fields of mathematics, natural sciences and engineering (due to their priority for the competitiveness of the economy). In addition to equal educational opportunities in regional terms (ensuring equal access to education), it is important to extend additional support for and stimulate the development  of  leading  research  centres  (for example,  National  Scientific Leadership Centres) in order to increase innovation and competitiveness of Polish science on the international arena (strengthening the position of Polish universities in international rankings).

Improving  the  quality  of  education  (teaching  and  training)  will  also  be  enhanced  by  the introduction  of  the  so-called  European  reference  tools  -  national  qualifications  framework, consistent with the objectives of the European Qualifications Framework, including the system of validation, as well as quality assurance systems in higher education and vocational education and training  (EQAVET),  systems  for  the  collection  and  transfer  of  educational  achievement  (ECVET and ECTS), and systems for classification and forecasting the supply of skills for the labour market (ESCO, Skills Panorama).

Taking into account the fact that health is a significant element conditioning the activity on the labour  market  and  having  a  decisive  impact  on  the  quality  of  human  capital,  pro-health  and preventive  activities  will  be  promoted  and  supported  with  a  view  to  prevent  diseases  which nowadays  are  the  major  cause  of  premature  labour  market  exit  and  of  making  use  of  social security resources (i.e. occupational diseases and lifestyle diseases). Projects for improving health are horizontal. Their implementation, however, should account for territorial diversity - such as health and social factors conditioning health, anticipated trends in this field, and access to health services.

###### II.4.3. Increasing labour mobility and spatial mobility

The policy creating conditions for flexible forms of employment, enhancing the mobility of the work force and the competencies of graduates (including digital and cross-cultural competence), as  well  as  circular  mobility  (in  particular  with  a  view  to  reduce  hidden  unemployment  in  rural areas), promoting non-agricultural forms of employment in rural areas, and providing universal broadband Internet access for all residents will increase labour force participation. Towards this end it would be appropriate to modernize the system of professional qualifications, including to introduce solutions allowing for the confirmation of competences acquired through non-formal and informal  learning,  as  well  as  a  support  system  for  increasing  or  changing  qualifications  of people who are disadvantaged on the labour market, etc.

Increasing the mobility of the population will be also important for economic development. It is necessary to increase accessibility to labour markets, both in material terms of material - such as transport  and  housing,  as  well  as  in  terms  of  digitization  of  peripheral  areas. Development  of transport infrastructure  should  allow  for  the  possibility  of  rapid  access  from  the  currently  less developed  (mostly  rural)  centres  to  the  growth  centres,  which  offer  more  employment opportunities. Reducing travel time to urban centres will translate into increased willingness to seek  employment  outside  of  the  place  of  residence  and,  consequently,  affect  the  economic development of regions from which the employees come from.

#### Objective II.5. Increasing the use of digital technologies

Contemporary  Poland  is  unevenly  developed  in  terms  of  access  to  what  is  called  'a  digital society'. Approximately  65%  of  Poles  use  the  Internet 19 ,  there  are,  however,  considerable differences in the use of the Internet in terms of such variables as education, age and place of residence of  the  population.  Younger  people  are  the  "digital  generation",  and  in  terms  of  the ability  to  use  the  digital  world  they  are  equal  to  their  peers  from  more  developed  countries. Meanwhile, people over 50 years of age make very little use of the information civilization.

Telecommunications  infrastructure  itself  is  also  developed  very  unevenly. The  situation  is especially difficult in poorly urbanized areas, where costs of network construction are higher and give a lower rate of return, which makes them less profitable for private investors. Despite the upward trend in broadband penetration, this increase is insufficient and Poland is still at a distant place among the EU Member States.

This  situation  is  a  product  of  -  inter  alia  -  the  availability  of  the  infrastructure  and  the  cost  of telecommunications  services,  as  well  as  the  use  of  digital  technologies  by  businesses  and individuals.

Digital Poland cannot be reduced to the reception of content and services generated abroad. This implies the need for an effective generation of services and content over broadband networks, tailored  to  the  interests  and  needs  of  individual  customers  and  communities. Although  the market  will  undoubtedly,  at  least  partially,  meet  the  needs  of  the  society,  it  is  the  state  that should identify strategic objectives and tools to be used for the Polish content and services to become adequate.

In the most developed countries of the world the access to ICTs is widespread. To increase the competitiveness of Poland,  it  is  assumed  that  the  same  level  of  access  to  the  technology  and infrastructure, as well as to content and services, and of digital literacy will be provided in our country  as  is  considered  a  standard  in  the  European  Union. In  the  next  few  years,  a  strong development of telecommunications services and solutions related to digitization (computerization)  of  the  country  is  assumed,  especially  regarding  further  development  of electronic  commerce,  e-health,  digital  public  services  (e-government),  e-PUAP  and  timework (telecommuting). Accelerated  digitization  of  the  country  will  be  crucial  for  the  diffusion  of development processes.

> 19 64.9% according to data from the publications: The use of ICT in enterprises and households in 2010, CSO, 31/03/2011

80

[[END OF PAGE 80]]

[[START OF PAGE 81]]

Our  current  backwardness,  together  with  the  rapid  pace  of  change  in  the  world,  require accelerated development of a digital society in Poland. Poland decided, therefore, to choose the "digital  momentum"  option,  consisting  in  consistent  implementation  of  tasks  and  processes conditioning the rapid development of the country. The development of the telecommunications infrastructure requires substantial investments  which, despite considerable  amounts,  are characterised by high rates of return.

Construction of the digital society is a horizontal task, i.e. concerning almost all fields of social and  economic  life,  and  thus  particular  tasks  related  to  digitization  of  the  country  will  be implemented under all the Integrated Strategies. It will be appropriate, therefore, to coordinate the  activities  of  various  actors  (government,  NGOs  and  private  entities)  at  various  levels  of management and financed from various sources.

The development of applications of digital technology will also account for various environmental aspects (energy-saving, modern green technologies, public education).

##### Priority directions of public intervention:

###### II.5.1. Ensuring public access to the Internet

The rate of development of broadband infrastructure in Poland is still too slow. This is due to the large  area  of  the  country  combined  with  relatively  small  population  density  and  population dispersion. High installation and operating costs of the infrastructure are related to the dispersal of  users. Despite the high cost of infrastructure, necessary to be incurred in order not only to meet the current requirements, but also to meet long-term future requirements, development of infrastructure should be a priority of the government in the next few years.

First,  it  is  necessary  to  enable  all  households to  have access to the Internet, and - in a slightly longer term - provide them with access to fast and ultra fast Internet. Measures to promote the process of turning an analogy TV and radio into digital ones are also important.

It is essential for the state intervention to fit properly to the character of territorial differentiation.  Different  activities  will  be  addressed  to  the  areas  having  the  character  of metropolis  (in  this  case,  the  activities  should  focus  on  the  development  of  next  generation network - NGN, because of a relatively high availability of the Internet), and to the areas of rural character,  with  low  population  density  (where  the  availability  of  the  Internet  is  low,  and investment is not profitable for private investors).

In  this  context,  the  construction  of  regional  broadband  networks,  implemented  by  local governments and aimed at reducing geographical risk of digital exclusion is becoming particularly significant. The task of ensuring universal broadband Internet access, makes it a must to achieve an effective coordination of projects to build broadband networks.

81

[[END OF PAGE 81]]

###### II.5.2. Promoting the use of digital technologies

A developed digital society requires not only the development of an appropriate infrastructure but also the development of needs, attitudes and skills making it possible for the members of society to take full advantage  of  new  technologies.  The  progressive  digitization  will  be accompanied  by  promotion  of  online  services,  especially  among  those  50  +  and  among  rural population, as it  is  in  these  groups  that  the  percentage  of  Internet  users  is  significantly  lower than in developed EU countries.

The promotion of the use of digital technologies should proceed in two ways - firstly, through the development of digital literacy and, secondly, through the popularization of digital services, stimulation  among  the  potential  users  of  the  need  to  have  Internet  access,  and  through development of the availability of electronic solutions in the field of education, health, justice, economy, and especially the administration and e-PUAP.

In order to build digital Poland it is important not only to stimulate the supply side - that is, to increase  the  number  of  high  quality  services,  but  also  the  demand  side  -  that  is,  to  build awareness of these services and to promote their use. A key task of the state in its endeavour to build digital Poland will be the development of citizens' competence in the area of digital literacy. The development of digital literacy will be translated into greater demand for various types of digital services which, in turn, should encourage the market to take action in these areas.

The  differences  in  the  skills  of  using  new  technologies  can  lead  to  the  phenomenon  of  digital exclusion. In Poland, the main variables associated with the division between experts in digital technologies and persons lacking these skills are age, place of residence and education. While among young people the use of the Internet is widespread, the digital exclusion of the elderly is becoming a problem. For this second group, the digital skills are not a natural component of the socialization processes and are strongly correlated with the level of education.

In the development of digital literacy, major efforts will be predominantly focused on two groups of people: people who teach (teachers, staff of other educational and culture institutions, staff of non-governmental  organizations)  and  need  to  use  Internet resources for preparing  and conducting  courses,  training,  and  coaching,  and  the  elderly  people  who  actually  may  have  a sufficient access to the Internet in the household but often fail to see the usefulness of using the Internet.

82

[[END OF PAGE 82]]

[[START OF PAGE 83]]

Developing  digital  literacy  among  educators,  who  are  often  models  of  behaviour  for  other members of the community, can contribute  to  a  rapid  spread  of  these  competencies  in  their environment. As a result of using digital technologies, students should be inspired to use these technologies in a creative way, and to acquire the ability to obtain useful information. The use of new  technologies  will  also  help  to  raise  awareness  of  the  younger  generation  as  regards measures restricting the consumption of materials and the generation of waste (for example, use of e-manuals), as well as CO 2 emission.

###### II.5.3. Ensuring the quality of digital content and services

Low level of the Internet use results not only from the problems of infrastructure and from the low level of skills, but also from a deficit of relevant content and services tailored to the needs of users. As a consequence, some people who declare having access to the Internet see no need to use it.

It should be noted that providing users with Web content and services is not, and never will be, the  exclusive  domain  of  the  state,  but  it  is  the  state  that  is  responsible  for  creating  the appropriate  environment  (mostly  legal)  to  build  a  legal  offer  on  the  Internet  for  personal, businesses and offices of various levels.

Readiness  of  Poland  for  digitalization  -  in  comparison  with  other  countries  in  the  region  -  is relatively poorly developed 20 . It is in particular the readiness of public administration that seems inadequate - the staff often does not use modern technologies properly. An important task of the state is to develop the supply of Internet services, including, in particular, the development of digital public services (e-government), e-PUAP, digitalization of heritage resources, and opening of the public resources. In order to make it possible, it is necessary to prepare the staff of public administration  accordingly,  by  appropriately  disseminating  knowledge  on  digital  technologies and on the possibilities of their use.

> 20 Report  prepared  by  World  Economic  Forum,  on  the  e-readiness:  The  Global  Information  Technology. Report 2010-2011. Transformations 2.0., Word Economic Forum, Geneva, 2011.

83

[[END OF PAGE 83]]

[[START OF PAGE 84]]

Increasing the Internet access will be particularly important in poorly populated areas, because - together with rapid development of digital public services (e-government) - it will increase the accessibility to public services. In these areas, public funds may be used for the construction of broadband  Internet  access,  including  by  the  use  of  free  or  divided  frequencies  (wireless broadband).

Taking  into  account  the  time  and  the  resources  required  for  this  process  as  well  as  the importance of the digitalization for the cultural heritage - it should be noted that it is impossible to carry out this process without the participation of public funds. Of particular importance is the role of public institutions in developing standards for this process and in ensuring proper storage of digitalized resources of the national heritage. One of the primary objectives of digitalization is to  disseminate  the  digitalized  resources.  To  create  the  right  conditions  to  release  those resources, relevant provisions of copyright law should be adjusted appropriately.

#### Objective II.6. Energy security and the environment

One  of  major  development  challenges  for  Poland  in  the  coming  decade  will  be  to  achieve sustainable  development  through  harmonious  combination  of  economic  growth  with  the requirements of environmental protection. The preservation of natural resources without their deterioration,  and  ultimate  increase  of  their  durability  and  quality,  cannot  be  regarded  as  a barrier in the development of the country. It is rather a prerequisite for further improvements of the  quality  of  life  and  of  the  human  right  to  have  access  to  the  natural  environment  in  good condition. The  main  task  becomes  thus,  on  the  one  hand  -  to  meet  growing  demand  for  raw materials and energy, and on the other hand - to find solutions to minimize the environmental impacts,  not  inhibiting  the  economic  growth, but  creating  new  incentives  to  stimulate  it, especially on areas that have not been urbanized .

The growing demand for raw materials and energy is primarily due to socio-economic changes over the last several years, associated with rapid economic growth and rising standards of living and  is  to  be  considered  of  lasting  nature.  Appropriate  measures  should,  therefore,  focus  on reducing energy- and resource-intensity of the economy, maximizing the economic effect. This approach  should  allow  for  providing  the  quantities  of  raw  materials  and  energy  required  for constant  development,  along  with  reducing  the  negative  impact  Of  the  economy  on  the environment.

During the past two decades, numerous successful measures aimed at rationalizing and stopping the  over-exploitation  of  national  natural  resources  were  undertaken. These  measures  resulted primarily  in  a  systematic  improvement  of  energy  efficiency,  accompanied  by  minimizing  the extraction  of  coal. In  parallel,  public  awareness  of  the  need  to  protect  and  preserve  natural resources  (particularly  scarce  and  non-renewable  resources)  increased  substantially.  However, the  growing  demand  for  energy  will  cause  coal  mining  maintain  its  level  of  production  in  the coming years, despite a decrease in the share of coal in electricity production resulting from the development of new energy sources. Despite a decline in energy demand of the Polish economy by more than half (between the years 1991 and 2007), it is still one of the most energy-intensive economies  of  Europe. Polish  primary  GDP  energy  consumption,  expressed  in  constant  prices (base year is 2005) and in purchasing power parity in 2008 amounted to 0.191 kgoe/euro05ppp 21 , and was 25% higher than the European average.

84

[[END OF PAGE 84]]

[[START OF PAGE 85]]

The development of competitive fuel and energy markets contributes to improving the energy efficiency. The  main  barriers  to  further  improvements  in  this  area  are:  a  small  number  of connections of the Polish market for electricity and gas with the European systems, a still too low share  of  the  electricity  exchanges  in  the  trading  of  electricity  on  the  domestic  market,  and incomplete deregulation of gas prices.

The  next  decade  should  witness  vigorous  actions  aimed  at  changing  the  structure  of  energy sources  and  energy  efficiency  of  manufacturing  processes  and  of  electricity  transmission,  at increasing energy and fuel use efficiency by different sectors of the economy (mainly transport, housing,  industry),  including  the  public  sector,  as  well  as  at  increased  use  of  energy  efficient equipment and technologies.

Enhancement of energy security will be accompanied by - besides the diversification of energy sources - diversification of direction of energy supply. Diversification of energy sources will result from  the  increase  in  the  share  of  energy  from  renewables  and  from  development  of  nuclear power. In 2010, almost 88% of primary energy came from solid fossil fuels, while about 10% from renewable  sources. 22 An  appropriate  strategic  choice  is  to  build  nuclear  power  stations;  with adequate security of radioactive waste; this energy source is considered to be the cleanest one in environmental terms. Nuclear power, compared to energy from fossil fuels, is characterized by a significantly lower cost of energy production, and additionally - negligible emissions of CO2 and dusts. Despite the significant investment costs, nuclear power is considered in the long-term to be the most cost effective because of the cost of energy production, the high rate of capacity utilization,  and  the  long  effective  lifetime  of  the  plant  reaching  up  to  about  50  years.  Low diversification  of  the  directions  of  energy  supply  manifests  itself  in  the  fact  that  over  90%  of supplies, of both oil and natural gas, come from the East. Investments will be continued to build the  infrastructure  allowing  for  supplies  of  energy  from  other  directions.  Work  will  be  also conducted  with  a  view  to  exploit  shale  gas  through  identification  of  resources  and  through preparation and implementation of transparent legal and administrative structures.

Key investment priorities will include the construction and development of interconnections in the north-south direction, and the integration of gas transmission systems in the region around the  Baltic  Sea.  These  connections,  along  with  the  expansion  of  liquefied  natural  gas  (LNG) terminal  in  Świnoujscie  and  of  internal  transmission  networks,  will  constitute  an  extremely important part of the process of building a regional gas market.

In  the  past  20  years,  considerable  successes  were  achieved  in  the  field  of  environmental protection. The loads of pollutants discharged into surface waters and into the air were reduced, industrial waste management problems were brought under control, the impact of solid organic substances  was  limited,  and  a  significant  portion  of  the  risk  to  human  health  stemming  from industrial sources was eliminated, together with the environmental threats to biotic and abiotic environment. However, air quality still remains a major problem, especially in urban areas - as a consequence of the so-called low emissions and emissions from transport and households.

> 21 Kgoe - kilogram of oil equivalent, euro05ppp - market rate euro value in 2005, currency buyng value taken into account.

22

> Source: Fuel and Energy Economy in 2009-2010, CSO, Warsaw 2011

85

[[END OF PAGE 85]]

[[START OF PAGE 86]]

Gradual  improvement  in  the  awareness  on  environmental  protection  and  on  energy  saving, however, is accompanied by unclear legal conditions and by low efficiency of law enforcement. Therefore, a key task is therefore to establish and implement an effective and stable legal and institutional  system  which  would  ensure  effective  protection  of  valuable  natural  areas  and species, would halt the decline of biodiversity, and where possible - enhance the biodiversity. It is particularly important to address the issue of the rational use of natural resources in the school curriculum, starting in the early school years.

Polish  ecological  network  is  rather  fragmented  and  does  not  constitute  a  system  of  coherent areas connected  functionally and  territorially, so as  to  guarantee  the  conservation  and sustainable use of biodiversity in the long run. Actions aimed at countering the fragmentation of space  and  at  creating  solutions  conducive  to  the  protection  of  natural  resources  will  thus  be undertaken,  particularly  through  the  creation  of  ecological  corridors  allowing  for  migration  of fauna  and  flora  systems  on  regional,  national  and  international  levels.  Improvements  of  the environmental quality will simultaneously affect the quality of life of the society, expecting to be provided with opportunities for recreation in appropriate environmental conditions.

The challenge for societies around the world is to adapt to the current and future impacts of climate changes, allowing for minimization of harm and dangers those changes cause for humans and in the environment (the intensification of catastrophic events, prolonged droughts and other natural disasters and extreme weather events, rising ocean and sea levels).

In the context of adaptation to climate changes in Poland, the starting point would be to identify sectors / areas particularly sensitive to climate changes and to determine for them appropriate plans plan of necessary adaptation procedures.

##### Priority directions of public intervention:

###### II.6.1. Rational management of resources

Natural  resources  are  the  foundation  of  the  economy  and  they  affect  the  quality  of  life. High resource  intensity  of  the  Polish  economy  may  undermine  the  development  of  modern, competitive  and  low  carbon  economy. The  appropriate  tasks  will  be  implemented  through  a comprehensive and integrated approach to the efficient use of resources. Therefore, actions to be  undertaken  will  cover  all  key  areas,  including climate  changes,  energy  efficiency,  resource policy, agriculture, transport, construction,  water  management,  waste  management  and conservation of biodiversity.

Numerous actions aiming to reduce the consumption of energy and the demand for resources by the economy, as well as to reduce environmental burdens, will be supported by the state. In a situation of limited access to natural resources, the need to use recycled materials is particularly important for sustainable development.

The basis for rational management of resources should be comprehensive information regarding their  size  and  quality. One  of  the  instruments  of  the  proper  management  of  resources  is  to include the principles of sustainable development in the planning processes.

What  is  key  in  this  context  is  the  proper  recognition  of  the  national  geological  resources (especially  energy  related)  and  the  security  of  the  deposits  considered  essential  to  the  energy security of the country. Strategic deposits will be included in the strategic spatial development and land  use plans,  which  will  promote  the  rational  management  of  the  areas  in  which  these deposits are located and will also guarantee a differentiation of methods of deposit protection in the  investment  process. Simultaneously,  work  will  be  continued  on  the  reconnaissance  of  the extraction potential of unconventional hydrocarbon resources (such as shale gas and gas trapped in the pores of the rock). Assuming the evidence of deposits at the currently estimated level of 346 - 768 billion m 3 in the most likely scenarios (1.92 trillion m 3 in  the maximum scenario) and the  production  costs  at  a  competitive  level  in  relation  to  the  cost  of  gas  imports  in  about  10 years,  it  is  possible  to  obtain  a  significant  increase  in  the  supply  of  gas  from  domestic sources. This  will  require  intensive  exploration  works  in  the  coming  years. In  parallel,  in  the planning  of  the  future  exploration  and  exploitation  of  the  shale  gas,  intensive  cooperation between industrial and research institutions will take place as regards the impact of shale gas extraction on the environment. Successful completion of geological exploration of unconventional gas (years 2015-2016), together with the inclusion of strategic deposits in spatial planning,  and  with  the  development  of  these  deposits,  will  significantly  increase  the  level  of energy security of the economy and of the population.

86

[[END OF PAGE 86]]

[[START OF PAGE 87]]

The  development  of  modern  technologies  of  extraction  of  geological  raw  materials  will  be supported. It  is  also  necessary  to  develop  a  long-term  policy  for  the  protection  of  geological resources and for their use for production purposes.

It is estimated that some parts of the country may face a problem of water shortage within the next 20-30 years. To prevent the degradation of waters, and thus the reduction of the volume of good quality water, preventive measures will be taken aimed at a more rational use of water, as well  as  at  equilibrating  the  collection  and  recovery  of  water  resources,  and  at  a  high  level  of waste water treatment. Planning documents concerning the management of water resources will include water impoundment in the entire country, and will take into account the management of rainwater in urban areas. Reimbursements will be provided for water services in accordance with the  "polluter  pays"  principle,  and  incentives  will  be  offered  for  users  to  make  efficient  use  of water resources 23 .

Polish  natural  resources  are  undoubtedly  an  important  element  of  Poland's  wealth,  however, they are substantially underappreciated. Conservation of biodiversity is essential if Poland is to meet the requirements of intergenerational equity understood as access to the wildlife resources for future generations. The main goal in this area is halting the decline of biological diversity and ensuring a favourable conservation status for a possibly large number of species and habitats.

Ongoing efforts to protect and maintain biological diversity will involve the provision of effective and proactive protection of valuable natural sites,  habitats and species in areas belonging to the Natura 2000 network, while taking account of the developmental processes and aspirations of the  country,  its  regions  and  local  communities.  Actions  will  be  implemented  to  stop  the defragmentation of the environment and to maintain the continuity and protection of ecological corridors. The size of protected areas will be increased, and the so-called green infrastructure in the vicinity of protected areas will be created. Restoration of adversely impacted ecosystems will be carried out, including aquatic ecosystems, marshes, swamps and bogs, also those degraded and abandoned by their current users. This kind of action will be accompanied by integration of the active protection of cultural and natural landscapes, as a carrier for potential development.

Actions will be taken to preserve agricultural land in the state of readiness for production, taking into  consideration  the  requirements  of  the  environment  and  activities  aiming  to  optimize  the genetic base of crops, livestock and fisheries.

The effectiveness of the rational management of the environment is determined by increased public  awareness and it requires promoting green behaviour of the Poles via conducting welltargeted and properly selected information and education activities.

###### II.6.2. Improving energy efficiency

Improving  energy  efficiency  is  one  of  the  priorities  of  the  EU  energy  policy.  The  Climate  and Energy Package sets a 20% target improvement in energy efficiency by 2020.

Improvement  in  energy  efficiency  may  be  perceived  from  the  perspective  of  producers  and  of consumers of energy. With regard to ensuring the necessary supply of electricity and heat, activities are planned aimed at restoring and strengthening the existing facilities and at building new power lines,  in  particular  with  a  view  to  allow  for  trans-border  transmission  of  electricity  between neighbouring countries and for the implementation of the so called smart grid solutions. It is also assumed  that  the  existing  regulatory  mechanisms  will  be  changed,  such  changes  consisting  in particular in the implementation of the heat pricing methods based on reference prices and in the introduction of incentives to optimize the costs of the supply of heat.

88

[[END OF PAGE 88]]

[[START OF PAGE 89]]

The modernization of electricity sector will include improving the efficiency of the transmission grid,  the  reducing  the  loss  of  electricity  in  the  transmission  networks,  and  improvements  in efficiency and loss reduction in the facilities of  manufacture and of use of energy through the introduction  of  energy-saving  and  highly  efficient  technologies.  In  order  to  reduce  the  failure rates of the system and the electricity transmission losses, as well to enable the integration of different energy sources (RES, nuclear energy, etc.), significant modernisation investments will be required. Stronger connections in the north-south axis transmission lines and the development of connections with north-eastern areas of the country are becoming particularly important. Efforts will  also  be  made  to  support  measures  in  favour  of  co-cogeneration  of  electricity  and  heat, increasing the final energy efficiency of the power plants, particularly in areas where it is possible to use the so-called "district heating". In addition, the development of diffused, outside of grid, energy generation sources will be encouraged, in particular including those based on production capacity - primary and secondary - of agriculture and fisheries) will be supported.

Factors crucial for reducing energy consumption will include: efficient use of electricity and heat by end users in various sectors of the economy - in the energy sector itself, in construction and in industry,  together  with  fuel  efficiency  in  the  transport  sector,  and  a  reduction  the  energy intensity  of  materials  and  equipment.  In  compliance  with  Directive  2010/31/EC  on  the  energy performance of buildings, it is expected that the system of assessment of energy performance of buildings will be improved through a gradual change of energy standards for new buildings as well  as  for  the  converted  ones.  The  role  of  energy  performance  certificates  of  buildings  is expected  to  be  strengthened  and  their  quality  to  be  improved,  and  such  certificates  will constitute the basic selection criterion when buying or renting a building or an apartment. It is also  anticipated  that  the  technical  and  construction  requirements  will  gradually  become more stringent in order to be optimal in terms of costs, while accounting for the technical progress in the construction sector and significantly reducing energy consumption in buildings. The ultimate objective  of  this  system  is  to  promote  energy  efficiency  of  buildings  and  to  increase  the awareness  as  regards  the  possibilities  to  achieve  energy  efficiency  in  buildings,  taking  into account  economic  calculation.  Improving  energy  efficiency  will  be  enhanced  by  the  use  of available  and  proven  technologies  in  the  area  of  energy  efficiency  of  buildings  and  district heating, which may result in savings in final consumption of heat and energy of about 15-35%, compared  to  the  pre-retrofit  of  the  buildings.  In  addition,  consistent  marking  of  energy consumption of appliances and energy using products, and establishing minimum standards for energy consuming products are assumed.

Efforts  aimed  at  increasing  energy  efficiency  among  both  manufacturers  and  end  users  will  be supported by actions to develop competitive fuel and energy markets. Markets for coal and liquid fuels can be considered competitive, but the markets for natural gas, electricity and district heat require further liberalization measures. In parallel, actions will be taken to protect and 'activate' consumers  in  these  markets.  The  connection  of  the  national  system  with  the  Scandinavian countries and the countries of Central and Eastern Europe through the electricity exchanges will expand  access  of  the  market  participants  to  foreign  electricity  exchanges,  contributing  to  the development of competition in the country.

New  pricing  methods  for  heat  will  be  introduced,  based  on  using  the  reference  prices  and incentives to optimize costs of heat supply. An important task will be to review the State regulatory powers as the tools of liberalization in the area of natural gas, electricity and heat supply networks.

Improvements  in energy efficiency will be well served by developments  of innovative technologies in the energy systems. Such improvements will result, in particular, from the advent of a new generation of machinery and equipment for energy and heat production based on the renewables - on using the nuclear reactors for the cogeneration of heat and electricity and on IGCC  gas-steam  cycles  (steam-gas  power  plant  technology  with  integrated  fuel  gasification). Reducing the environmental impact of energy generation will foster the implementation of clean coal technologies (CCT).

89

[[END OF PAGE 89]]

[[START OF PAGE 90]]

By 2020, Poland will fulfil its commitments to reduce greenhouse gas emissions imposed by the provisions  of the  Climate and  Energy  Package  under  which  the  European  Union  is  required  to reduce the total emissions by 20% until 2020, compared to 1990.

###### II.6.3. Increasing the diversification of fuel and energy supply

One  of  Poland's  priorities  in  the  next  decade  will  be  to  ensure  energy  security  through  the diversification  of  production  and  supply  of  fuels  and  energy.  Numerous  investments  will  be necessary to eliminate the risk of power deficits and to enable a significant increase in power generation capacity after 2020.

Security  of  supply  requires  the  diversification  of  both  sources  and  directions  of  fuel  and  energy supplies. Over the next ten years, coal will remain the main fuel for electric power generation. It will be important, therefore, to meet the domestic demand for coal and to ensure its stable supply to customers, together with the required quality parameters. As to the oil and liquid fuels markets, basic  investment  will  include  the  construction  of  infrastructure  for  transport  of  crude  oil  from different regions of the world, in particular from the Caspian in the framework of Euro-Asian Oil Transportation Corridor project, as well as the expansion of transmission, transhipment and storage infrastructure for petroleum and liquid fuels. Given the need for further reduction of the emissions of  key  pollutants  from  combustion  processes  of  fuels,  and  to  radically  reduce  greenhouse  gas emissions, it will be necessary in the coming decades to modify the Polish energy mix. Plans exist to develop natural gas energy generation to the level that would balance the growing demand for electricity, while simultaneously reducing emissions. Power generation based on natural gas will be important for the supplementary balancing of energy shortages and for reducing the volatility of production from RES. It will also be used as an energy source in co-generation. Diversification of gas supplies  will  be  supported  by  the  construction  of  transmission  systems  enabling  the  delivery  of natural gas from the north, the west and the south. Access to geographically distant sources will be possible  thanks  to  investments  ensuring  the  storage  capacity  -  building  a  terminal  for  receiving liquefied natural gas (LNG) in Świnoujscie and increasing its capacity. Efforts will be made in the area of nuclear power. The construction of the first nuclear power plant covering about 7% of net demand for electricity will start within the period covered by this strategy document (using modern nuclear safety regimes). Due to public concern related to the construction of nuclear power plants, it is necessary to conduct an information campaign and educate the public in this field. The share of RES  in  final  energy  consumption  will  increase  to  a  minimum  of  15%  in  2020,  in  line  with  the objective set for Poland in the Climate and Energy Package. Promoting the use of renewable energy will  help  to  increase  regional  energy  security  and  to  create  conditions  for  the  development  of diffused  power  generation  sources,  based  on  locally  available  raw  materials,  and  thus  the development of less developed regions, rich in renewables.

Legislative action aimed at removing barriers to investment, in particular for large infrastructure projects and investments is also assumed to take place within the period covered by the strategy.
