###### II.6.4. Improving the environment

Major factors determining the quality of the environment include: clean air, clean water, clean soil,  and  proper  waste  management.  In  all  these  areas  there  are  still  issues  that  need  to  be regulated  and  adjusted  to  a  level  compatible  with  the  strategic  lines  of  action  defined  by  the European Union. Investing in water conservation and in waste water services, waste management and air protection, as well as actions to enable market participants to address the challenges of sustainable development are, therefore, important. All these undertakings should be  accompanied  by  mechanisms  to  improve  environmental  management,  including  access  to environmental data.

Long-term measures to reduce dust emissions and other air pollution, especially in the sectors of highest emissions (energy, transport), as well as from diffused emission sources (small industrial plants,  small  boiler  rooms)  and  from  private  sources  in  housing  (the  so  called  low  emission sources) will contribute to improvements in air quality.

In  order  to  achieve  these  goals,  the  use  of  innovative  technologies  in  the  industry  will  be promoted as well as of alternative fuels and solutions to increase fuel efficiency in transport and energy, and the use of low-carbon fuels in housing.

A management system will be created for national levels of greenhouse gas emissions. By 2020, the EU will reduce emissions of greenhouse gases by 20%, compared to 1990. After the adoption of the 2005 reference level in the Climate and Energy Package, Poland, together with other EU countries, will reduce the greenhouse gas emissions in accordance to the ETS emissions trading scheme by 21%, while in the non-ETS area, Poland will be able to increase emissions by 14% in 2020,  compared  to  2005.  Long-term  policy  aimed  at  reducing  emissions  will  be  supported  to encourage changes in production technologies.

Actions  will  be  strengthened  to  protect  groundwater  and  surface  water  by  reducing  pollution from  point  and  area  sources.  Activities  improving  water  quality  will  be  related  to  wastewater management systems, especially fulfilling the objectives and tasks of the National Programme of Municipal  Wastewater  Treatment  (NPMWT).  The  aim  of  the  programme  is  to  build  sewage treatment plants and sewage pipeline systems in agglomerations, as well as performing parallel tasks in the areas not covered by NPMWT.

92

[[END OF PAGE 92]]

[[START OF PAGE 93]]

In order to reduce the amount of pollution resulting from agricultural activities, the development of  knowledge on the environment will be promoted by supporting good agricultural practices. Measures  will  be  also  undertaken  to  protect  the  unique  ecosystems  and  flora  and  fauna associated  with  agriculture  and  fisheries  as  well  as  measures  aiming  to  minimize  the  risk  of introduction of alien species into the environment that would endanger native species.

Completion of the construction of an effective system of waste management will be necessary, especially  of  municipal  and  hazardous  waste.  The  main  goal  of  the  waste  management  policy should be to prevent waste the production of waste "at source" and to support the maximum recovery of materials and / or energy contained therein. Appropriate actions will also include the introduction  and  implementation  of  the  "3R"  (reduce,  reuse,  recycle)  rule  as  well  as  the management of the closed loop waste circulation. These will include among others: introduction of  a  selective  waste  collection  system  in  Poland,  construction  of  installations  for  the  recovery (including recycling) and disposal of waste, closure and reclamation of municipal waste landfills that do not meet the standards set by law or that are harmful to the environment, elimination of the illegal landfills, reduction of the amount of waste deposited in landfills, for example, through subjecting it to recovery. Legislative changes to remove obstacles to investment in the priority areas of modern waste management will be introduced.

An important area of action  will  be  also  the  promotion  of  sustainable  production,  sustainable consumption  and  sustainable  industrial  policy. The  process  of  transformation  towards  a  green (low  carbon)  economy  will  be  accompanied  by  structural  changes  in  the  domestic  economy, mainly  due  to  the  dynamic  development  of  the  sector  of  medium  and  high  technologies  and services.

Another  important  task  will  consist  in  reducing  health  problems  arising  from  environmental pollution, to be achieved mainly by reducing releases of hazardous substances and by effective control  of  the  substances  introduced  into  the  environment  (E-PRTR  Regulation,  REACH) 25 . General measures aimed at the promotion of sustainable development are also important, such as: implementation of Integrated Product Policy (IPP), promotion of environmental technologies, implementation  of  environmentally  friendly  patterns  of  production  and  consumption. In  this context,  activities  under  the  concept  of  efficient  use  of  resources  that  aim  to  integrate environmental and economic areas so as to maximize resource productivity with minimal impact on  the  environment  are  becoming  particularly  important. A  noise  protection  policy  is  also assumed to be implemented, including the reduction of the impact of noise sources, construction of  noise  barriers,  development of  noise monitoring  systems,  construction  of  urban  ring  roads, and use of quiet road surfaces.

General  support  will  also  be  given  to  projects  aimed  at  the  development  and  promotion  of Poland's  own  environmental  technologies,  at  creating  ecological  attitudes  of  Poles,  at  the development of environmental education, at creating conditions for the formation of green jobs at promotion of "green" purchases in the public administration and in business.

Methods  of  eliminating  natural-spatial  conflicts  and  barriers  to  sustainable  development  and minimizing the negative effects arising from possible conflicts, especially between development programs and protected areas, including those belonging to the Natura 2000 network, will be defined. Methods  used  for  this  purpose  will  include the  institution  of  environmental  impact assessment. As  part  of  arrangements  on  space  management,  initiatives  will  be  undertaken  to reduce  the  potential  negative  effects  of  further  economic  development  for  the  environment, especially  in  connection  with  the  development  of  communication  networks,  for  example,  by providing an appropriate amount of properly localized animal crossings on Natura 2000.

> 25 E-PRTR  -  European  Register  of  Pollutant  Release  and  Transfer;  REACH  -  Registration,  Evaluation  and Authorisation  of  Chemicals  -  Regulation  (EC)  No  1907/2006  of  the  European  Parliament  and  Council  of 18 December  2006  concerning  the  Registration,  Evaluation,  Authorisation  and  Restriction  of  Chemicals (REACH), establishing a European Chemicals Agency, amending Directive 1999/45/EC and repealing Council Regulation  (EEC)  No 793/93  and  Commission  Regulation  (EC)  No 1488/94  as  well  as  Council  Directive 76/769/EEC and Commission Directives 91/155/EEC, 93/67/EEC, 93/105/EC and 2000/21/EC, concerning the  safe  use  of  chemicals  through  their  registration  and  assessment  and  in  some  cases,  releases  and limitations trade and use of certain chemicals.

93

[[END OF PAGE 93]]

[[START OF PAGE 94]]

In  the  economic  transformation  to  the  green  path,  sustainable  infrastructure  will  play  an important role. Another priority will be shaping the quality of urban space. The growing role of large  urban  centres  requires  the  creation  of  standards  for  sustainable  development  in  urban areas as well as changes in approaches to environmental protection in these areas. Measures will be implemented for sustainable urban planning, fostering increase in the quality of life in urban areas, including the creation of public space, of urban green infrastructure functional areas, of zones  of  aeration  in  the  cities  as  well  as  of  quiet  zones. Cities,  as  urban  agglomerations  and industrial, large clusters of population, affect the environment significantly, not only within their borders, but also in a wider adjacent area.

Measures  will  be  taken  to  manage  the  urban  environment  and  adapt  cities  to  the  climate changes. Low-carbon solutions will be implemented, among others in the field of sustainable urban transport, infrastructure, improvements in the efficiency of heating, upgrade of lighting, etc.

###### II.6.5. Adapting to climate change

A key task in this area is the development and effective implementation of system solutions for adapting  to  changing  climatic  and  hydrological  conditions,  including  minimizing  the  effects  of natural  disasters  and  of  extreme  weather  events.  Adapting  to  climate  change  will  involve  the adjustment  of  risk  sectors  and  areas  (agriculture  and  forestry,  water  resources  and  water management, biodiversity and ecosystems, coastal zone and maritime management, transport, energy and mining, construction, metropolitan areas, spatial planning, local communities) to new conditions and climatic events, including ensuring the supply of energy and fuel, the prevention of temporary water shortages and the improvements in the crisis management systems.

Actions will be taken to reduce the impact of drought and prevent the development of steppe. It is necessary to reduce the risk of the extreme weather events as these become more frequent, particularly  regarding  flooding  along  the  waterways.  All  actions  aiming  to minimize  the  risk of flooding will be provided for in the flood risk management plans. The findings of these plans will be included in spatial planning at all levels. Flood risk management plans put particular emphasis on prevention, protection, proper security, including flood forecasting and early warning systems, and take into account the characteristics of the river basin or catchment. These plans may also include the promotion of sustainable land use practices as well as effective water retention and controlled flooding of certain areas (such as polders, dry reservoirs) in the event of flooding. In addition, in the area of water management plans, infrastructure operations and monitoring and warning  efforts  will  be  implemented,  such  as  the  development  of  small  and  large  forms  of retention,  proper  maintenance  of  mountain  rivers  and  streams,  restoration  of  riverbeds  and wetlands,  and  the  introduction  of  effective  flood  warning  systems.  Introduction  of  a  modern planning system based on GIS technology should solve the problem of issuing building permits in areas  of  landslides  and  flood  plains.  It  is  necessary  to  develop  new,  standardized  spatial information systems, accessible via the Internet, and including the information on environmental hazards,  allowing  for  the  identification  of  hazards  related  to  the  systems  of  land  registration. Acceleration  of  the  decision  making  processes  and  reduction  of  administrative  barriers  in  the field of risk management requires, among others, effective water management in the catchment system.

One of the important actions is to protect the coastal zone as the most vulnerable to climate change, including the provision of effective and safe coastal protection for the environment and natural  values,  minimizing  the  effects  of  erosion  of  the  coastline  and  ensuring  an  effective pollution control of the sea and parts of maritime rivers. Equally, marine environment should be used a sustainable way, guaranteeing that current and future generations will have the possibility to use this environment and to conduct activities embedded within it. What it means is that the structure, functions and processes of marine ecosystems need to be fully taken into account, that marine  species  and  habitats  should  be  protected,  and  that  the  loss  of  biodiversity  caused  by humans should be inhibited. The effective protection of the Baltic Sea and the maintenance of the  marine  environment  in  good  quality  requires  a  concerted  effort  to  reduce  pressures  and negative impacts on the environment of the Baltic Sea. All this will be implemented on the basis of functional planning for coastal and marine areas.

#### Objective II.7. Increasing the efficiency of transport

Transport is one of the key factors determining economic development of the country. Availability of transport infrastructure and its adequate capacity allows for the diffusion of economic growth from the strong regions to the ones developing more slowly.

Poland's transport infrastructure is one of the weaker elements of the Polish economy. It does not match the entrepreneurship of the Poles, nor the intensity of production and exchange or the mobility of residents. The current problems  include: a high degree  of wear  of several infrastructural  elements,  both  lines  and  terminals,  the  occurrence  of  bottlenecks  and  missing links,  uneven  regional  distribution,  inadequate  availability  of  the  network,  lack  of  network adjusted  to  the  high  speed  traffic,  poor  connections  between  the  built-up  areas,  insufficient infrastructure  elements  and  poor  systems  integrating  different  types  of  networks,  network elements causing nuisance for the residents and for the environment, a small number or the lack of intelligent and innovative solutions. The high number of traffic accidents, deaths and injuries remains a significant problem.

96

[[END OF PAGE 96]]

[[START OF PAGE 97]]

The long-term objective for Poland is to achieve network density and capacity corresponding to the developmental needs of the country and of the regions. This will facilitate the development of the regions, by increasing the territorial accessibility of peripheral areas, and will at the same time  allow  for  making  the  best  use  of  the  transit  position  of  our  country. The  planned infrastructure  investments  will  be  aimed  at  creating  an  integrated  and  coherent  transport system. This will involve the designation of a two-tier structure: the core network - defined by nodes and links of key importance for our country and accounting for the process of international integration; and the complementary network - aimed to ensure territorial cohesion. The creation of  a  coherent  transport  network  and  the  eradication  of  peripheralization  of  our  economy  are among the main determinants of the development of Poland.

The  most  important  goal  for  Poland  for  2020  is  to  increase  the  external  and  internal  (interregional  and  local)  territorial  accessibility. Measures  should,  therefore,  be  taken  aimed  at eliminating  peripherality,  both  of  the  country  and  of  its  regions. The  second  leading  objective fostering improvement of territorial accessibility is to create a coherent transport system, making it  possible  to  transport  goods  and  people  efficiently,  using  different  means  of  transport, and taking  into  account  the  ecological  characteristics  of  rail  and  inland  transport. The  model  of funding  will  be  changed,  increasing  the  revenues  from  the  use  of  transport  infrastructure (depending on the type of transport) and involving extra-budgetary funds.

##### Priority directions of public intervention:

###### II.7.1. Increasing the efficiency of management in the transport sector

The long lasting process of completing the formalities necessary before launching an investment in  transport  infrastructure  (including  environmental  conflicts)  implies  a  need  to  change  the management of investment processes, including to further simplify the  process  that  condition the  preparation  and  implementation  of  an  investment. Better  integration  will  be  ensured  of transport  investments  with  spatial  development  plans  and  with  environmental  plans,  with regional policy and with legislative adjustments.

The model of financing infrastructure in Poland is neither uniform, nor coordinated or optimized, and  as  such  it  does  prove  as  efficient  as  it  could  be. This  model  is  made  up  of  numerous components,  it  provides  complex  procedures  and  complicated  requirements,  it  is  inconsistent with regard to various entities which construct and manage infrastructure. Moreover, instead of being  complementary,  various  sources  of  financing  seem  to  'compete'  with  each  other.  High investment  costs  and  the  burden  on  the  public  finance  sector,  including  state  and  local government  budgets,  will  require  a  coherent  model  of  financing  the  transport  infrastructure (state  budget,  EU  funds,  capital  market,  private  investors,  loan  guarantee  instruments,  toll collection  system);  changes  will  also  be  required  in  the  principles  of  financing  transport investments  by  local  government  units  (LGUs). As  regards  decisions  on  new  infrastructure projects,  such  as  the  Central  Airport  and  High-Speed  Rail,  their  implementation  will  require adequate preparation and acquisition of private funds. New infrastructure investments should be a  future  source  of  revenue  for  the  state,  which  would  then  be  allocated  for  operation, maintenance  and  development  of  the  existing  facilities,  as  well  as  for  further  investments,  if possible. The  investment  projects  to  be  implemented  should  be  aimed,  in  the  long-term,  at generating revenues, to enable the maintenance  and  management  of  infrastructure, in accordance with the principle "user pays".

Also the possibility should be considered of increased participation of the private sector in the implementation  of  projects,  in  particular  in  the  form  of  public-private  partnership  (PPP). The mechanism  of  public-private  partnership  can  provide  an  effective  alternative  for  financing investment  from  the  domestic  or  the  European  budget,  based  on  past  experience  in  similar projects. The  combination  of  private  and  public  resources  in  the  long  run  could  increase  the effectiveness  not  only  of  financing,  but  also  of  the  management  of  infrastructure  projects. In order to create an effective system of financing infrastructure, it is necessary to open this system to  private  capital,  as  well  as  to  use  more  extensively  the  instruments  such  as  bank  loan guarantees. Such  mechanism  would  bring  about  better  results  in  terms  of  timely  delivery  of investments and in terms of proper allocation of risk, resulting in spreading the expenditure of the financing authority over time. The changes in fees should not result in deterioration of the competitive position of the Polish entities abroad.

97

[[END OF PAGE 97]]

[[START OF PAGE 98]]

The  new  sources  of  financing  the  construction  of  infrastructure  should  take  into  account  the access charges paid by the users. The level of charges should be based on the type of transport and it should be calculated to account not just for normal operation and maintenance, but also for the necessary development. The system of charges should also take into account the external costs  of  a  given  branch  of  transport. A  system  of  tariffs  will  be  established,  comprising  the charges  for  using  the  infrastructure,  depending  on  the  type  of  transport  and  the  length route. These measures will increase the pool of finance allocated for construction of transport infrastructure.

Preparation  and  implementation  of  infrastructure  investments  requires  the  management approach,  oriented  towards  achieving  the  objectives  and  towards  increasing  the  efficiency, including  in  particular  financial,  asset  and  risk  management. Organizational  and  management structure,  including  the  current  system  of  management  and  of  monitoring  the  preparation  of investment projects and their implementation, must be supplemented by an effective system for monitoring  maintenance  of  the  existing  infrastructure  and  by  a  permanent  mechanism  of financing  the  needs  in  this  regard.  Also  organizational  changes  are  required  to  improve  the efficiency of the system.

Prioritization  of  investments  will  be  introduced  at  national  and  regional  levels,  accounting,  for example,  for  the  need  for  access  to  the  labour  market  or  for  the  location  of  the  region. The investments  to  be  implemented  will  be  selected  based  on  reliable  indicators,  research,  and analyses, including costs and benefits analyses. Thanks to this activity, investment closing stages will be effectively realized (in particular the linear ones).

A user-friendly transport system will be developed as well as regulations fostering the integration of the transport system. Key actions aimed  at  the deployment  of  intelligent transport management systems will include: the improvement of facilities for collection and distribution of data  on  the  status  and  use  of  transport  infrastructure  (for  example,  information  about  traffic jams), the development of a system of management and control of urban traffic and of the roads outside  urban  areas,  the introduction  of systems  increasing  traffic safety (including the construction and development of automated traffic surveillance), the development of integrated management of accidents, as well as the development of an Integrated System for Passengers and Freight.

###### II.7.2. Modernization and expansion of transport links

In the coming decade, it will be a priority to invest in transport networks of national importance in all branches and in the inter-nodes, aimed to improve the access to the interior of the country and to enhance the economic exchange, including between major centres of economic growth and between urban centres (metropolitan network), as well as within their functional areas. At the  same time,  investments  will  be  made  aimed  at  improving  the  external  accessibility  of  the country, with the key role of the TEN-T network in the creation of an effective transport policy and of a coherent network of infrastructure in the European Union.

In  the  context  of  ensuring  territorial  cohesion,  it  would  be  of  necessary  to  expand  the infrastructure links connecting urban centres located in peripheral areas with the metropolitan network.  In  addition  to  the  priority  for  investment  in  roads  and  railway  lines  of  national importance, transport investments in these areas and selected local projects will be supported, which  provide  additional  connections  between  towns;  also  systems  supporting  national  and provincial transportation will be assisted. Construction of these transport links will depend on the financial capacity of the state budget.

There is a need to place greater emphasis on the complementarity of activities undertaken by different actors at national, regional and local levels (vertical  complementarity), as well as within various sectors of the transport sector (horizontal complementarity). Complementary activities aimed at creating an integrated national transport system will be implemented at regional and local levels, to ensure that they form a coherent and coordinated whole.

In  relation  to  road  infrastructure,  a  coherent  network  of  motorways  and  expressways  will  be created, essential to the effective  functioning  of  the  transport  system  on  national  and international  scale,  and  serving  major  transportation  corridors  and  providing  functional  links between  major  centres,  including  in  particular,  between  Warsaw  and  the  main  voivodeship centres.  The  reconstruction  of  national  roads,  which  will  improve  the  road  safety,  will  be continued. Travel conditions for the transit traffic and traffic services in metropolitan areas and in big  cities  will  be  improved.  A  program  will  be  implemented  for  the  construction  of  ring  roads around cities and large towns (including around Warsaw) as well as a program of traffic calming on the roads passing through cities, towns and small villages. There will be changes introduced in the hierarchy of priorities for some road projects. Transport investments will be subject to the criteria  of  transport  network  and  systems  formation  logistics,  with  a  view  to  increase  the integration  by  strengthening  such  elements  as:  intermodal  systems,  intelligent  traffic  and handling control systems.

99

[[END OF PAGE 99]]

[[START OF PAGE 100]]

Rail infrastructure projects will be implemented to modernize the main lines and the supporting infrastructure  (including  railway  stations),  and  to  modernize  or  replace  the  rolling  stock.  The investment  in  rail  infrastructure  network  will  be  accompanied  by  the  development  of  rail passenger and freight traffic,  particularly  in  the  most  promising market  segments.  Obtaining  a high  standard  of  railway  connections,  by  upgrading  the  lines  between  major  Polish  cities,  in particular the sections that run radially from and to Warsaw (including the Central Trunk Line), is task  of  equal  importance  from  the  perspective  of  eliminating  peripherality  and  improving territorial accessibility of rail connections.

In order to ensure the effective functioning of air transport, it is necessary to develop the existing airport and navigation infrastructure to suit the growing demand in terms of capacity. Actions should be based on the continuing expansion of airports within the TEN-T network. At the same time,  to  increase  the  capacity  of  Polish  airspace,  it  is  necessary  to  fully  implement  the  Single European Sky (SES - Single European Sky) in systemic and operational terms, and at the same time to implement the state-of-the-art technological solutions provided for in the ATM Master Plan.

Implementation of the major transport projects, in terms of scale of investment and of funding, such  as  High  Speed  Rail  and  the  Central  Airport,  would  strengthen  Polish  transport,  both internally  and  externally  -  in  the  European  and  in  the  global  dimension.  However,  the  final decision  on  undertaking  such  a  significant  investment  effort  by  Poland  will  depend  on  many factors,  and  will  take  into  account  such  aspects  as  the  current  and  expected  macroeconomic situation  of  the  country  and  the  resulting  forecasts  of  demand  for  transport,  as  well  as  the conclusions  from  the  feasibility  studies  undertaken  for  the  two  projects  and  their  economic viability.

Moreover, the integration  of  transport  systems  is  planned  -  connecting  the  air  transport  with other kinds of transport, and thus raising their inter-modality, in particular within the framework of  the  TEN-T  network.  These  actions  will  create  favourable  conditions  for  the  development  of airports in the eastern areas of Poland. These airports, mostly small, will be designed to support local communities and - as airports serving general / business aviation traffic, as well as national and  emergency  traffic.  It  is  also  necessary  to  develop  and  implement  new  procedures  which would increase airport capacity.

Poland's inland waterway network does not create a single, integrated transport system, but a collection  of  separate  elements of  a  different  quality.  Efforts  will  be  undertaken  to  modernize and develop inland water transport infrastructure (shipping routes and ports); relevant projects will be implemented subject to the availability of funds.

The marine areas of Poland represent over 10% of the area of the country, and length of the shoreline is 528 km. The maritime policy of Poland aims at maximizing the benefits for citizens and for the economy arising from the location of the country and from the marine biotic and mineral resources. Support for the development of national maritime sector is foreseen, among others by ensuring a possibility of shipping and handling transoceanic connections. The economic potential  of  Polish  sea  areas  in  the  future  will  be  based  on  the  development  of  sea  ports,  in particular  those  of  primary  importance  for  the  national  economy  (Gdańsk,  Gdynia,  Szczecin, Świnoujście). In the context of the increasing turnover and the potential of ports for storing and handling of goods, it is important to build modern intermodal terminals and logistics centres in the  ports  themselves  and  their  hinterland.  Developing  and  modernizing  access  to  ports  and harbours,  both  from  the  sea  and  land,  including  the  construction  of  deep-water  berths  and approach channels to ports - roads, rail and inland waterways - will be essential. Moreover, an important factor in the development of Polish marine areas will be maritime tourism. The marine space also creates expectations for diversifying energy supplies, as well as for exploration and exploitation of natural resources.

###### II.7.3. Facilitation of traffic in urban areas

The two main reasons for the frequent lack of smooth urban traffic flow are the inconveniences of the public transport (lack of convenient connections, poor coordination of various modes of transport,  overcrowding)  and  the  transit  traffic  through  town  centres.  To  transform  the  city transport network into an efficient and functional element of the infrastructure, the regions need to  focus  on  improving  the  quality  of  the  public  transport  offer.  One  of  its  key  elements  is  to organize  an  uninterrupted  movement  of  people  within  the  metropolitan  area  that  would  be consistent with the residents' expectations and to facilitate the movement to and from the outer areas.

It  will  be  important  to  introduce  advanced  systems  of  management  and  traffic  control  in  the cities, so as to transform the urban transport network into an efficient and functional element of infrastructure,  providing  convenient  connections  to  other  regions  and  the  European  transport system.  Tariff  systems  integration  within  the  conurbation,  including  the  introduction  of  a common fare for travel by various means of transportation - which would largely facilitate the use of public transport and increase its popularity.

101

[[END OF PAGE 101]]

[[START OF PAGE 102]]

Another  important  factor  affecting  the  attractiveness  of  public  transport  is  the  adequate frequency  of  transport  services  to  ensure  transport  capacity  that  meets  the  demand.  In  this context it  is  important  to  develop  the  high-speed  rail,  which  has  the  largest  shipping  capacity from among the available public transport means and thus relieves congestion on other means of public transport.

The lack of bypasses of large cities is an equally important factor, which results in transit vehicles going  through  urban  centres,  increasing  the  traffic  intensity  and  -  consequently  -  causing congestion. This process can be effectively halted by building a bypass of the agglomeration and organizing a smooth movement of trucks.

Activities will be implemented to improve the public transport using the simplest and often the cheapest solutions, commonly applied in EU countries (for example the traffic light control and adaptation to new roads and intersections, parking ban on the roads in the city centre and the creation of parking places in the areas where it is economically reasonable, including near to the service  outlets,  continuation  of  lowering  of  the  tram  tracks  to  the  level  of  the  road  or  their reconstruction to enable their use by the buses and other emergency vehicles).

Given  the  limited  fossil  fuel  resources  and  an  increasing  pollution  from  their  combustion  in heavily urbanized areas, it is necessary to develop low-emission, alternative power technology vehicles.

### Strategic Area III. Social and territorial cohesion

#### Objective III.1. Social integration

The  accelerated  processes  of  restructuring  and  development  are  accompanied  by  social stratification    in  many  dimensions:  income, generations, health, education, housing, as well as government. Persisting social differentiation may become a barrier to maintaining the dynamic growth in the future. Inclusive growth means supporting an economy characterized by high levels of  employment  and  entrepreneurship  and  ensuring  economic,  social  and  territorial  cohesion. Convergence at the European level accompanied by an internal divergence is observed in many EU Member States.

Diversity  of  living  conditions  and  income  of  the  population  in  Poland  has  a  clear  spatial dimension. The main axes of disparities relate to differences between south-western and northeastern regions and between metropolitan areas and other units. The result is that the areas of low  development  that  are  at  risk  of  stagnation  include  not  only  sub-regions  and  peripheral regions,  but  also  poorly  communicated  with  the  surrounding  inner  periphery  areas,  often  the borderland areas of voivodeships.

The  threat  of  exclusion  in  Poland  is  also  territorial  (in  nature).  In  different  regions  there  are problem  areas  characterized  by  low  accessibility  and  quality  of  public  services,  limited  own resources and, consequently, low development potential. The regional economic development so far,  despite  some  measures  aimed  at  equalising  the  level  of  development  that  are  financed mainly from EU funds, has slightly reduced the inter- and intra-regional stratification in Poland. Economic growth and GDP per capita vary in both these systems. Mechanisms stimulating the diffusion  of  development  processes  from  urban  areas  are  currently  insufficient  to  significantly affect the other areas, particularly those characterized by weaker socio-economic indicators.

Differences in access to public services and their quality in the various territorial sections are also observed. The result is a relatively low level of human and social capital, resulting in the need for compensatory  measures  of  the  state  and  possible  strengthening  of  the  citizens'  passivity. Therefore, emphasis will be placed not only on redistributive measures, leading to the alleviation of poverty related to the level of income, but primarily on activity promoting measures, enabling the widespread participation in various spheres of socio-economic life. The task of development policy is to support the growth promoting measures in a way enabling as many businesses and citizens  as  possible  to  participate  in  and  benefit  from  such  measures  and  to  enable  more effective implementation of social policy focused on solving or alleviating the most difficult social issues.

The basic cause and consequence of social exclusion is poverty. In Poland the risk of poverty is the highest for the unemployed, large families, families of people with disabilities, but also the working poor, i.e. easily replaceable workers performing low-qualified and low-paid jobs.

Inclusive economic growth means a growth ensuring solidarity coupled with equal access to basic public services for all citizens. It also means not only the inclusion of the poorest, but also their integration on the labour market, which will have a positive impact on the development of the country. The task of the state is to efficiently use the potentials of individuals and social groups, despite  the  existing  deficits  and  dysfunctions,  including  to  build  a  model  of  recipient-oriented social policy. In the medium and long-term it will be necessary due to a decreasing number of people at working age. Therefore, a need will arise to use the reserves, which have not been used so far.

Effective  implementation  of  the  tasks  aimed  at  ensuring  cohesion  will  be  supported  by  the implemented concept of decentralization of the state, fulfilling the principle of the transfer of relevant decision-making powers to the level of governance where there is the largest amount of data  required  to  make  optimal  decisions.  Separate  responsibility  of  territorial  levels  for  the different  spheres  of  social  life  and  for  institutions  requires  some  consolidation  at  the  regional level and boosting the instruments of the voivodeship government in order to achieve the longterm objectives, mainly regarding infrastructure. It also requires the regulatory strengthening of integration activities at the local (gmina and poviat) levels, where various social institutions are subject to different legislation and sectoral management. Local government, which performs the majority of tasks in this respect, is often unable to implement them to the necessary extent and at  a  necessary  pace  using  its  own  resources  and  funds.  Therefore,  it  needs  more  support, including organizational and financial support. In the area of poverty alleviation and combating social exclusion, it is necessary to strengthen institutionally the social welfare organisational units and improve coordination between the various stakeholders in order to solve or mitigate such social issues as poverty and social exclusion.

103

[[END OF PAGE 103]]

[[START OF PAGE 104]]

The role of culture in the fight against social problems, including social exclusion, also needs to be emphasized. Projects in the fields of culture contribute to the development of modern society which  is  open  to  change  and  tolerant.  They  prevent  the  marginalization  of  individual  social groups, especially at risk of exclusion due to disability or due to their place of residence.

##### Priority directions of public intervention:

###### III.1.1.  Increasing  the  activity  of  the  socially  excluded  persons  and  persons  at  risk  of  social exclusion

The reaction of the state to exclusion and poverty will be primarily the policy targeted at the growth  of  employment  and  improving  access  to  basic  public  services,  including  high  quality education, resulting in an increased activity of the socially excluded and persons at risk of social exclusion.  The  state  will  also  support  the  development  of  social  economy,  primarily  through appropriate legislative and financial instruments.

An active system of social benefits aiming at taking up and maintaining employment will be built. The support will be provided not only to the unemployed, but also to the "working poor". The measures resulting in the higher profitability of work than social transfers are also required. The objectives that are expected to bring about the changes to the system of social benefits include also the better adjustment of support, promotion of activity by e.g. integrating the activities of various public services (reform of the illness, maternity, invalidity, accidents at work benefits). It is  necessary  to  implement  an  effective  system  of  e-skills  development  and  to  prevent  digital exclusion. Digital exclusion can lead to exclusion from the labour market, and this in turn is one of the reasons for social exclusion. An important task, conducive to promoting the activity of the excluded persons, is to prepare appropriate instruments aimed at identifying and enhancing the potential (skills) of individuals, which involves the programmes restoring the participation of the excluded people in social life. It would be a next step to effectively support these persons on the labour  market.  A  more  widespread  use  of  social  economy  (including  the  integration  of disadvantaged groups and creating jobs for them) should be one of the important instruments promoting the activity of the excluded groups.

A  particularly  difficult  task  is  to  stimulate  the  activity  of  people  with  disabilities.  An  emphasis should  be  placed  on  vocational  and  continuous  education,  better  preparation  of  the  local community, civil servants and employers for the participation of people with disabilities in socioeconomic life, as well as on adjustment of space in architectural and urban planning terms to the needs of this group - it is important for building social bonds. These actions, however, will not be fully effective without corresponding changes in the education system, aimed at integrating the disabled people.

###### III.1.2. Reducing poverty among the highest risk groups

In Poland, the highest risk of poverty is faced by families with many children, and therefore this problem affects mainly children and adolescents. It is necessary to offer support to families in the form of  selective  social  benefits,  emergency  social  assistance  and  the  tax  system  instruments addressed directly to families, , on the one hand, and to ensure equal development opportunities and  prevent  neglect  arising  from  households'  deficits,  on  the  other  hand.  The  issue  of  full accessibility of child care and educational services, conducive to equal development opportunities of children and reconciliation of work and family life, is of utmost importance, as are the policies consisting in providing schools with universal social benefits (healthy nutrition, medical care and physical activity).

The problem of poverty is also encountered by the disabled. Particular attention will be paid to preventive measures, such as: school education, skills training and lifelong learning, provision of facilities enabling mobility and presence of disabled people in public space, preparation of people with disabilities to articulate their needs, provision of information on civil rights and responsibilities;  adequate communication with the environment and participation in the social life of the local or professional community.

Efforts  to  improve  social  cohesion  within the  next  10  years,  particularly  after  2020,  must  take into  account  the  demographic  changes  associated  with  population  ageing.  The  key  will  be  to provide older people with access to health care services and long-term care services. Promoting the activity of this social group is also important in order to keep them on the labour market and increase their participation in social life. Since the risk of poverty of older people will increase, a need will emerge to develop a new model of assistance for this group of recipients.

105

[[END OF PAGE 105]]

[[START OF PAGE 106]]

Actions undertaken in the area of housing will also contribute to reducing social exclusion. During the period covered with the strategy, the housing policy will focus on eliminating the quantitative deficits in the segment of social housing and housing for persons requiring other social housing assistance.  Affordable  housing  will  be  offered  in  the  housing  rental  sector.  At  the  same  time activities will be carried out to reduce the deficit of expenditure for renovation of dwellings and reduce the demand for energy in the housing sector. It is also important to reduce the costs of housing  construction  and  rationalize  the  spatial  functionality  of  the  new  housing  stock  by creating  appropriate  resources  of  land  prepared  for  housing  development.  The  aim  of  the housing  policy  will  also  be  to  rationalize  the  principles  of  the  management  of  public  housing resources and resources of communal building societies..

#### Objective III.2. Providing access and specific standards of public services

The  availability  and  the  quality  of  public  services  demonstrate  the  level  of  the  country's development and the quality of life of its population. Since the access to such services (health, education,  culture,  sports,  administration,  security,  etc.)  significantly  affects  the  potential development of citizens, this issue requires special attention of the central and local government.

Public services include tangible or intangible goods, the supply of which is the responsibility of public  authorities  and  the  right  to  use  which  cannot  be  denied  to  anyone.  Public  services  are usually  performed  in  the  areas  of  activity  which  require  the  maintenance  of  an  appropriate material, personal or financial basis while ensuring the continuity of their provision at a certain level.  The  scope  of  public  services  is  variable,  both  in  terms  of  their  type  and  the  general availability,  depending  on  the  current  demand.  In  addition,  it  is  conditioned  by  the  financial potential of the public authorities.

The provision of basic public services is one of the important areas of local government activity. The transfer of these powers to local government resulted from the basic rule stating that the provider of services should be as close to customers as possible and their performance should be subject  to  citizen  control.  In  the  opinion  of  local  communities,  the  standard  of  living  depends directly on the quality of public services. The main indicator of the effectiveness of meeting the needs in this regard is the degree of satisfaction with the standards of provided services. While the efficiency of using the resources of an individual entity performing a specific public service can be determined by analyzing the cost of providing the service and the number of services in the given area along with their standard.

Public  services  make  up  a  large  group  of  diverse  activities  and  projects,  ranging  from  simple administrative  services,  such  as  issuing  ID  cards  and  driving  licenses,  registration  of  specific events  (births,  weddings),  to  making  administrative  decisions  (in  the  field  of  environmental protection,  spatial  planning  and  construction).  These  services  are  performed  largely  in  local government  offices,  which  act  both  as  the  organizer  of  the  services  and  as  their  provider. Currently, the standards for the implementation of such services are related, in principle, to the level of the customer service culture in a specific office.

Public  social  services  (health,  education,  culture,  sport,  welfare,  unemployment  services)  are generally performed by local government agencies or entities with legal personality, the founding body  of  which  is  the  local  government.  The  basic  problem  here  is  to  increase  the  economic rationality  and  in  the  case  of  services  for  which  the  standards  are  legally  defined  (social assistance, education), there is a need to maintain the standards prescribed by law.

Public services of a technical nature (public utility services) include water supply, sewage disposal and  treatment,  heat,  electricity  and  gas  provision,  municipal  waste  management,  transport services, road maintenance and modernisation. Such public utility services can be provided either by entities reporting to the local government (budgetary units, public utility companies), or by mixed capital companies or private companies. However, the responsibility for providing these services always rests upon a specific local government unit.. In Poland the problem of unequal access to public services is still widespread and has a spatial dimension at national and regional levels.

In terms of administrative and institutional efficiency of the state, the standards of public service provision do not correspond to the level of technological development and the changing socioeconomic  determinants,  creating  an  incoherent  system  in  which  different  bodies  and  entities providing  such  services,  provide  them  to  varying  extent  and  at  different  cost.  The  significant disparities are recorded in the provision of public services both between different regions of the country and between the growth centers (large cities) and less developed areas (e.g. rural areas) and outermost regions.

107

[[END OF PAGE 107]]

[[START OF PAGE 108]]

The  provision  of  public  services  involves  a  large  number  of  complex  and  lengthy  procedures, which  in  turn  lead  to  their  low  effectiveness.  It  is  also  often  accompanied  by  the  lack  of adjustment to changes in society and economy as well as by stagnation of procedures resulting in increasing disparities between the expectations of the society and the actual quality of services. The rules for financing of public services are also a problem, as they result i.a. in varied costs of the services provision. Despite the development of modern technologies, the technologies that could improve the functioning of the public service system are not sufficiently implemented. The non-state actors are not sufficiently involved in performing such tasks.

A  relatively  low  ability  of  the  administration  to  analyze  the  costs  and  quality  of  the  services results in limited possibilities for establishing and improving the standards of service.

The improvement of the provision of services by increasing their availability and quality will be the challenge for the state in the coming years. The measures undertaken should contribute to ensuring more equal access of all citizens to high quality public services. As a result of changes in the management system, the units providing all kinds of public services should have a flexible approach  to  changing  realities  and  keep  up  with  technological  developments.  By  2020,  the transparency of public services will be improved and a service will be introduced for efficient and reliable assessment of the effectiveness of their provision.

##### Priority directions of public intervention:

###### III.2.1. Improving the quality and availability of public services

The improvement of the quality of public services means extending the scope of their provision, implementing mechanisms to monitor the standard of provided services and citizen participation in setting the standard.

All services provided by public authorities will be reviewed in order to diagnose the condition of the public service system in the country, determine their scope and reduce the unnecessary and duplicating services. The construction of the public services management system will include the adoption of a system of standards binding for all of the providers, both with regard to the specific quality of these services, as well as the specific effects they are to produce.

The public service standards established at the national level will provide a reference point for determining the possibility of their fulfilment at the regional and local level, taking into account the specific characteristics of some areas, such as outermost or problem areas.

A broader and more comprehensive use of information and communication technologies and the development of the necessary infrastructure will contribute to the introduction of appropriate standards  of  public  service  provision  and  to  increasing  their  availability..  This  applies  both  to administrative services (forms, applications, requests), educational services, part of health care services (data management, partial diagnosis), cultural services (digitization of cultural resources and  national  heritage)  and  utilities  (development  of  sewage  treatment  and  water  supply infrastructure, enabling a selective collection of waste, etc.). The state will support to a greater extent the role of non-state actors, focusing in particular on social economy entities, in providing services and determining the method of their provision.

The creation of a system of provided public services and theirs standards should be accompanied by  increasing  their  availability.  Also  in  this  regard,  the  territorial  specification  should  be  taken into account. This will involve the use of the potential of new technologies and the identification of services which can be provided using those technologies (like culture) and which can only be supported in this way (health care). The availability of services will also be increased as a result of an  improved  transport  accessibility,  which  will  not  only  offer  the  citizen  the  access  to  public services in close proximity, but will also enable the citizen to choose the unit where the waiting time for a service is shorter or the service's quality is better.

108

[[END OF PAGE 108]]

[[START OF PAGE 109]]

Increasing the access to public services will be associated primarily with a strive to ensure equal access to the standards of these services in the territorial system of the country, with particular focus on rural and outermost areas.

###### III.2.2. Increasing the effectiveness of the public service provision system

Increasing  the  quality  and  availability  of  public  services  will  be  accompanied  by  increasing  the efficiency of their provision and financing system. This will be linked to the creation of a modern public service management system, which will use tools to measure the efficiency and control the costs of public tasks implementation. A component will be created within the framework of the public service management system that will allow to fulfil the second priority, i.e. to increase the  efficiency  of  public  services.  Such  a  system  will  be  equipped  with  tools  to  measure effectiveness, which will allow for periodic verification of standards, both in terms of their use, as well as their relevance in the context of the changing reality. The effectiveness of public services will  also  be  increased  by  adopting  a  new  approach  to  their  financing,  taking  into  account  the territorial  specificity  and  allowing  to  combine  public  and  private  financial  resources.  This  will allow  to  both  increase  the  access  to  public  services  and  improve  their  quality.  Public  opinion (cyclical polling of the expectations of the society) will be increasingly taken into account and the experience  of  the  private  sector  and  academic  centres  in  analysing  customer  satisfaction  and expectations will be widely used in the decision-making in this regard. An integrated information platform will be established for public services. At the same time, periodic audits of the quality of public  authorities'  performance  in  the  field  of  services  for  citizens  and  businesses  will  be introduced.  This  will  be  accompanied  by  educational  and  information  activities  aimed  at increasing  the  knowledge  and  awareness  of  the  citizens  about  the  benefits  of  using  remote access tools for these services.

[[END OF PAGE 109]]

[[START OF PAGE 110]]

Increasing  the  effectiveness  of  the  public  service  system  will  also  involve  a  wider  use  of  the private  sector  and  NGOs  as  suppliers  and  organizers  of  such  services.  As  regards  the  public authorities, efforts will continue to assign the responsibility for providing public services along with the transfer of necessary funding to the level of public administration, which will perform this task most effectively in terms of quality and economic criteria. This may mean changes in the assignment  of  responsibility  for  providing  and  managing  public  services,  both  in  terms  of decentralization of these tasks and, in special cases, shifting the responsibility for their delivery between  local,  regional  and  central  government  units.  The  efficiency  of  the  system  will  be increased also thanks to the changes, which will involve the separation of the function of the supplier (contractor) of public services from the function of their financing and quality assurance while maintaining cost control to prevent the overstating of costs by the providers of services.

The objectivization of funding schemes, which will maximally shift from the objective financing (concentrated  on  financing  the  maintenance  of  the  infrastructure  base  and  personnel)  to  the subjective financing, related to the financing of services actually provided in accordance to the required standard, will also contribute to increasing the availability and quality of public services. The rationalizing element of the system will be the mechanisms for co-financing selected public services by the service recipient, with ensuring the forms of assistance for people who, due to financial difficulties, are not able to bear those costs.

#### Objective III.3. Strengthening the mechanisms for territorial development  balancing  and  spatial  integration  in  order  to develop and make a full use of the regional potentials

The uneven pace of development is a natural element of business processes both in the world and in Poland. The challenge is to reconcile the development interests of the state related to the competitiveness  of  regions  and  its  strengthening  in  the  country  and  internationally  and  the prevention of widening of regional disparities. Therefore, the new regional policy must focus its efforts  on  strengthening  the  endogenous  potentials  of  all  the  territories  and  developing mechanisms to enhance the diffusion of the development processes of the main growth centres to the environment. Apart from these activities, the development processes in problem areas will be  supported,  primarily  by  increasing  their  capacity  of  absorption  of  growth  factors  (capital, skilled labour, knowledge, innovation).

This  approach  entails  assigning  a  special  role  to  urban  centres  and  their  surroundings  as  the centres of  development  processes  both  in  the  country  and  in  the  regions.  The  regional  policy aimed  at  developing  functional,  infrastructural  and  institutional  links  between  voivodeship centres assumes the full use of the development factors based on regional and local potentials and the flow of development impulses not only between voivodeship centres, but also to their further surroundings.

The  level  of  economic  development  of  the  country  depends  largely  on  the  performance  and position of the largest urban centres, with their functional areas. They are the engine stimulating the development of the entire country and its regions and increasing their competitiveness. The most effective of these, which are centres of growth and socio-economic development, generate the  largest  share  of  GDP,  bringing  together  the  potential  determining  the  strength  of  the economy.  They  are  often  a  place  of  concentration  of  job  creation  and  high  quality  human resources, they create and quickly absorb innovations, and get involved in networking with other centres  within  the  national  and  international  systems,  increasing  their  complementarity  and specialization.  However,  disparities  within  urban  areas,  where  the  most  of  the  social  and economic  problems  are  concentrated,  are  more  pronounced  than  disparities  in  the  entire country (between urban and rural areas, Warsaw and the rest of the country, east and west). Therefore,  it  is  necessary  to  pursue  a  national  urban  policy,  linking  sectoral  policies  affecting urban development and defining measurable objectives tailored to the challenges faced by the cities of various types.

In  addition  to  supporting  the  largest  urban  centres  and  their  functional  areas,  which  are  the engines of growth of the entire country and its individual regions, it is necessary to strengthen the capacity to absorb and diffuse the development processes by sub-regional and local centres, including the poviat towns, with their potential for territorial specialization. The development of poviat  towns  and  other  cities  of  local  importance,  which  have  the  potential  to  attract  nonagricultural investments or use the agricultural potential of surrounding areas (as a market and a place  for  processing  of  agri-  food  products),  stimulates  the  development  of  the  surrounding areas.

The dissemination of the developmental processes will also be enhanced by the support targeted at rural areas. Such projects will include extension and modernization of infrastructure, especially transport, revitalization measures and the development of public services and other functions in the  provision  of  public  services,  which  are  necessary  to  initiate  development  processes  at  the local level.

The diverse settlement, social and economic structure or rural areas in Poland and the varying degree of their links with cities require an increasingly precise orientation of the regional policy which should be integrated with the sectoral activities undertaken with regard to this category of areas  in  such  fields  as  telecommunications  and  transport  infrastructure,  agriculture,  industry, education and health.

111

[[END OF PAGE 111]]

[[START OF PAGE 112]]

The contribution of Polish regions to the achievement of national development objectives, i.e. growth and employment, varies. This is demonstrated by the concentration of GDP and a high growth  rate  recorded  only  in  certain  areas  of  the  country.  The  main  creators  of  growth  and employment are the regions with competitive and innovative economy, especially the largest and most dynamic urban centres located in their area. The Mazowieckie Voivodeship has the highest and growing share in generating the national GDP - in 2009 it accounted for 21.9% of Poland's GDP. Four regions with the greatest economic potential are: Masovia, Silesia, Wielkopolska and Lower Silesia - in 2009 they generated 52.6% of the national GDP. 26

The uneven pace of development and threats that arise from increasing disparities between the regions, social groups or sectors of the economy require the economic policies to address the challenges  related  to  the  elimination  of  backlog  and  to  support  the  creation  of  the  new competitive advantages.

The processes of marginalization are most concentrated territorially in peripheral rural areas, in the areas that lose their existing socio-economic functions (urban areas, former industrial areas and other degraded areas) and in border areas with a low availability of transport, particularly in eastern Poland but also in other regions of the country.

One  of  the  strategic  challenges  for  regional  policy  is  to  ensure  the  internal  cohesion  of  the country (so that none of the territories would be excluded from the development processes) and to prevent excessive spatial differences.

##### Priority directions of public intervention:

###### III.3.1.  Creating  the  institutional,  legal  and  financial  conditions  for  the  implementation  of development activities in the regions

Decentralization, strengthened with the programming process of Cohesion Policy 2007-2013, has increased the responsibility of the voivodeship governments in pursuing the regional development policy. The transfer of the responsibilities related to the management of regional programs resulted in the necessity to introduce appropriate system changes. The reformulation of the role of actors associated with conducting regional policy and redefining their relationships was  of  particular  importance.  This  issue  concerned  in  particular  the  definition  of  the  tasks  of government administration and the determination of the new role of voivodeship governments in  terms  of  their  active  participation  in  decision-making  and  implementation  processes  of  the regional policy. The relationship between the central government, voivodeship governments and other  local  entities  involved  in  the  implementation  of  the  regional  policy,  such  as  town/city authorities, social and economic partners, also required clarification.

In the light of the new challenges, as well as the development management system introduced in the country, it is advisable to review the institutional and competence links as well.

It  is  necessary  to  strengthen  the  coordination  of  government  activities  undertaken  within  the framework  of  the  strategy  implementation  at  the  regional  level.  Strategies  and  programmes should take into account the regional (territorial) aspect, particularly in the context of the impact of  their  implementation  on  the  socio-economic  situation  in  individual  regions.  It  becomes necessary  to  develop  and  formulate  the  inter-ministerial  cooperation  mechanisms  aimed  at coordinating the activities at the central level under the sectoral policies of individual ministries for the regional policy of the state, consisting i.a. in taking into account the relevant differences and  territorial  potentials  in  the  design  and  intervention  of  public  policies.  The  regional  policy should be a part of coordinating element of sectoral policies under the integrated approach. At the same time, the regional policy development measures should be integrated with the rational and sustainable spatial management.

> 26 Source: CSO.

112

[[END OF PAGE 112]]

[[START OF PAGE 113]]

The rules governing mutual relations (complementary) between individual policies will also be developed.  The  issue  of  spatial  concentration  of  interventions  and  activities  conducted  both regionally and within sectors and their mutual coherence in the implementation of the objectives of the regional policy of the state is of key importance.

Conditions  will  be  created  for  a  broad  strategic  discussion  between  different  public  and  nonpublic entities involved in activities for the strategic directions and forms of regional development and the spatial dimension of the development policy.

In order to ensure better planning and implementation of public territorially targeted intervention,  the  system  for  continuous  monitoring  and  evaluation  of  regional  development processes and the effects of regional policy will be launched. This will allow to obtain the data showing  both  the  course  of  public  intervention  and  the  development  of  the  socio-economic situation in the areas of strategic intervention of the state.

The  most  important  area,  where  it  becomes  necessary  to  take  measures  being  a  natural consequence of and a prerequisite for the decentralization of regional policy, is the decentralization  of  the  public  finance  system,  along  with  the  reconstruction  of  the  system  for territorial  distribution  of  the  funds  (the  so-called  territorialisation).  The  responsibility  of  local government  units  for  the  development  of  the  territory  concerned  requires  the  necessary financial support, thus giving them a guarantee of effective participation in the regional policy.

In order to ensure efficiency and effectiveness of actions taken in the respect of the territory at different levels of development management (national, regional and local), it becomes necessary to ensure effective methods for the central and voivodeship government to agree on common objectives  and  priority  projects  undertaken  and  funded  by  the  regional  development  policy.  It will be enabled by the territorial contract which guarantees the implementation and financing of negotiated investment projects.

The forthcoming implementation of the regional policy system and mechanisms to enhance its efficiency is to entail i.a. the introduction of instruments supporting the diffusion of development processes beyond the areas constituting the socio-economic life centres. The activities to this end will  contribute  to  improving  the  complementarity  and  functional  links  between  voivodeship centres  and  other  areas  of  voivodeships.  The  successful  diffusion  of  development  processes depends on the introduction of effective and widespread instruments enhancing the functional relationships between various types of areas, such as urban and rural areas, and establishing a clear division of responsibility for the development-related tasks within the multi-level development management system.

###### III.3.2. Strengthening of the voivodeship centres

Polish cities  and  surrounding areas are in many respects inferior to cities and regions in other countries, in particular in highly developed countries of the European Union. Compared to them, the  main  Polish  urban  centres  are  characterized  by  a  deficient  development  of  metropolitan functions, particularly in the sphere of economic management, transport functions, research and development,  culture,  and  to  some  extent  -  symbolic  functions.  These  centres  represent  a relatively  low  level  of  innovation  and  participation  in  the  knowledge-based  economy.  Other problems occurring in the cities include infrastructure gap, progressing process of uncontrolled suburbanization, population ageing and poor condition of urban space. Despite these shortcomings and weaknesses, metropolises and the largest Polish cities are the major centres of development of the country and regions.

In  order  to  strengthen  the  provincial  centres,  the  development  of  public  services  and  higherorder  and  symbolic  functions  to  enhance  the  importance  of  these  centres  on  a  national  and international  scale  are  postulated.  The  measures  undertaken  will  concern  the  development  of metropolitan functions of international and national importance, such as economic functions, i.e. the  presence  of  management  structures  of  important  economic  and  financial  corporations, scientific  functions,  which  mean  ensuring  the  highest  standards  of  operation  for  scientific  and research institutions, wide range of technology transfer centres, cultural and symbolic functions i.e. the presence of active cultural centres of the highest rank. The administrative functions are important  for  the  programming,  coordination,  initiation  and  implementation  of  development projects. Their appropriate performance can make a significant contribution to the improvement of the situation in the regions. Development and efficient use of metropolitan functions will serve as an impulse for further development of these cities and their functional areas (and at the same time the entire country).

An important part of activities aimed at stimulating the development of metropolitan functions of  the  voivodeship  centres  is  the  introduction  of  support  for  mechanisms  of  cooperation  and networking  in  the  areas  determining  the  competitive  strength  of  individual  centres  in  the country.  The  cooperation  of  regions,  cities,  businesses,  research  centres,  universities  and organizations in all its dimensions, i.e. economic, scientific, educational and cultural dimension, is an  important  factor  for  spread  of  development  processes  by  enhancing  the  economy,  the exchange of know-how, partners acquisition, science and technology, production and investment cooperation. In an environment of cooperation created by three sectors, namely the government,  enterprises  and  research  centres,  it  is  possible  to  create  innovations  that  would provide Polish regions with sustainable and competitive advantages in many areas. In order to develop the metropolitan functions, actions will be undertaken to improve the availability and quality  of  transport  between  and  within  regions,  as  well  as  of  the  municipal  transport.  Many cities face the problem of traffic jams, noise and air pollution. Therefore, actions will be taken to solve these problems.

114

[[END OF PAGE 114]]

[[START OF PAGE 115]]

An  increase  in  the  competitiveness  of  the  major  Polish  cities  is  also  supported  by  the development  of  human  resources  and  social  capital.  The  activities  of  the  state  will  focus  on improving the quality and effectiveness of education in leading academic centres, including in the area of vocational education.

The  support  will be  granted  for development  of  social  infrastructure, including cultural infrastructure, as well as for the measures aimed at protecting the cultural heritage, which is an important  factor  of  development  and  increasing  the  attractiveness  of  cities,  contributing  to building a strong competitive position of urban centres.
