###### III.3.3.  Creating  conditions  for  the  development  of  regional  and  sub-regional  centres  and increasing the potential of rural areas

The cities which are the so-called growth poles include not only the largest voivodeship centres, but also important  urban  centres having a regional, sub-regional or local  impact. 27 All voivodeships  have  a  developed  and  quite  evenly  distributed  network  of  medium-sized  cities, fulfilling important functions on the regional and sub-regional scale. This is the network through which development impulses, originating mainly in metropolitan centres, pass and spread to the remaining part of the region. Cities are fulfilling their role as centres of development playing a crucial role in economic,  social and  administrative life of individual regions.  Thanks  to specialisation, some of them directly influence the development of the country in the economic sphere, and also in terms of providing the access to public services of medium and sometimes high-order for residents of the region.

Therefore,  the  efficiency  of  the  settlement  system  which  covers  the  cities  of  various  sizes, situated outside the main  centres  and  their functional areas, will  be  enhanced.  Public intervention will be adjusted to the cities specificity at regional and sub-regional levels, and will also  take  into  account  the  relationship  between  the  growth-generating  areas  (major  cities), functional  areas  and  their  associated  peripheral  areas,  including  rural  areas.  The  work  on revitalizing  degraded  areas  and  poor  urban  areas  and  supporting  the  links  between  rural  and urban  areas  will  also  be  carried  out.  Strong  and  competitive  towns  and  medium-sized  cities should  be  centres  for  the  development  of  rural  areas,  border  areas,  as  well  as  the  areas peripherally  located  in  relation  to  major  cities,  which  act  as  intermediaries  in  spreading  the development from major cities further into the country.

Urban  development  should  be  based  on  the  use  of  territorial  specificity  and  deepening  the specialization  which  determine  the  comparative  advantages  of  the  area,  in  such  fields  as industry,  advanced  agriculture  and  services,  including    in  particular  tourism.  Support  for  the clusters  development  (transformation  of  companies'  aggregations  into  dynamic  clusters  with high levels of competition and cooperation, interaction and external impacts) will be a modern instrument for strengthening regional and local specialisations.

The  local  transport  infrastructure  is  the  main  factor  conducive  to  the  spread  of  development processes between cities and to their surrounding areas. The activities of the state in this area (in particular  of  local  government  units)  will  focus  on  building  and  modernising  the  local  road infrastructure and the intensification of transport connections, which should both promote the development of entrepreneurship and enable the access to the public services offered by urban areas. The initiatives for the construction of transport infrastructure (public transport) connecting the rural areas to urban centres will serve to improve the territorial balance of development in urban  and  rural  areas  and  will  facilitate  the  access  of  rural  population  to  the  medium-order public services and jobs in small towns and regional centres.

Investments in ICT infrastructure will be supported in parallel to the transport investments. The development of this infrastructure, in addition to transport infrastructure, is a factor contributing to economic and social cohesion, i.a. by means of the opportunities offered by the popularisation of  broadband  in  rural  and  underdeveloped  areas.  Actions  in  the  field  of  ICT  infrastructure development will  be  accompanied  by  education  and  training  activities  popularising  the  use  of modern information technologies in education, administrative services, business, culture, etc.

The planned intervention to support development processes in the regions also includes various types  of  rural  areas  and  should  be  differentiated  according  to  their  functions  and  level  of development. Cross-sectional approach to rural development results from the need to take into account their diversity and is focused on making the best use of development potentials of the most developed rural areas and increasing the absorption capacity of growth factors (by means of developing the functional relations between the cities), on the one hand. On the other hand, it refers  to  the  special  treatment  of  the  rural  areas  with  a  poorer  access  to  public  services determining  the  development  opportunities.  Rural  areas  should  be  perceived  in  a  functional sense, not only in terms of agricultural activity.

> 27 The adopted delimitation of cities is compliant with the typology of functional areas developed in the National Spatial Development Concept 2030, adopted by the Council of Minister on 13 December 2011.

116

[[END OF PAGE 116]]

[[START OF PAGE 117]]

A better use of endogenous resources of the rural areas in the development of the country and regional development will be achieved by means of functional integration of these areas with the cities,  strengthening  the  absorption  of  external  development  processes  (development  of infrastructure, including transport, energy and environmental infrastructure, support for human resources development, support for business location, mainly using local material resources and creating  green  jobs),  support  for  territorial  specialization  and  ensuring  uniform  standards  for access to basic public services and goods.

In order to support the development of rural areas dependent on agriculture it is important to carry out the activities  designed  to  increase  the  competitiveness  and  modernisation  of agriculture and fisheries. In this context, the changes in the structure of the agricultural sector will be supported.

Development  policy  should facilitate a multifunctional development  of  rural areas. The conditions  conducive  to  creation  of  non-agricultural  jobs  in  rural  areas  and  increasing  labour mobility between rural and urban areas will be improved through measures aimed at supporting the diversification of economic activities of agricultural producers, improvement of the education system  in  rural  areas  (including  its  quality  and  access  to  vocational  and  technical  education, adjusted to the needs of the local labour market), and support for people taking up teleworking.

A prerequisite for increasing the efficiency of policy with respect to rural areas is to ensure an integrated,  multisectoral  approach  to  planning  and  undertaking  actions  within  various  public policies, including in particular the regional, agricultural, social, educational, cultural, environmental and transport policies. The new approach will help eliminate structural barriers in the  country  in  the  coming  years.  The  measures  to  support  rural  development  will  aim  at increasing the level and quality of life in rural areas, starting from provision of care and preschool education,  through  improving  the  access  to  education  and  culture,  and  to  support  for  nonagricultural  business  development  and  infrastructure  development  as  well  as  by  means  of improving the access to public services.

###### III.3.4. Improving territorial cohesion

The contribution of Polish regions to achieving national development objectives varies. The main creators of growth and employment are the regions with competitive and innovative economy, and especially the largest and the most dynamic urban centres located in their area.

The uneven pace of development and threats arising from the increase in disparities between and  within  regions  ,  social  groups  or  sectors  of  the  economy  require  the  economic  policy  to address  the  challenges  related  to  the  elimination  of  backlog  and  support  for  improving  the competitiveness. Therefore, in addition to the support for growth poles, it is important to create conditions for balanced territorial development.

The development of factors capable of spreading the development processes should take place in two dimensions: by expanding the largest positive impact areas of the voivodeship centres and by strengthening the absorption capacity in areas of the voivodeship - in the sub-regional cities, poviat cities and rural areas.

The regional policy is a primary instrument for implementing  the so defined national development objectives with regard to space.

Territorial disproportions in Poland and the EU are increasing, due to the faster economic growth generated primarily by the largest urban areas.

One of the areas of  strategic  challenges  for  development  policy  and  regional  policy  is  thus  to ensure the internal cohesion of the country and avoid the excessive spatial differences.

Apart from the areas where the determinants of development are concentrated, there are areas which,  due  to  the  accumulation  of  barriers  to  development,  are  not  able  to  use  their  spatial resources to enhance competitiveness and innovation, and thus to participate to a greater extent in  the  creation  of  economic  growth. These  areas  are  defined  as problem areas, requiring restructuring  and  development  of  new  functions,  with  the  support  of  relevant  regional  policy instruments. 28

118

[[END OF PAGE 118]]

[[START OF PAGE 119]]

Marginalization  processes  in  Poland,  unlike  in  developed  countries,  are  highly  territorially concentrated  in  peripheral  rural  areas  and  former  industrial  areas,  losing  their  economic functions, as well as in border areas with low transport accessibility, particularly in the eastern Poland. This  is  the  largest  problem  area  of  the  country,  which  still  needs  special  treatment, because the cohesion of this area with the rest of the country and the EU is insufficient in each of its  aspects:  economic,  social  and  spatial. The  voivodeships  of  eastern  Poland  (Podkarpackie, Świętokrzyskie, Lubelskie, Podlaskie and Warmińsko-Mazurskie), despite a significant improvement in their socio-economic situation in recent years, are still characterised by a low (compared  to  the  whole  country  and  EU)  level  of  development,  ageing  population,  education level worse than the rest of the country, obsolete infrastructure, poor living conditions (including access to goods and services), low productivity in all sectors of the economy and low transport accessibility. The potential of this part of the country, which, if supported by appropriate public intervention, can be used to increase the contribution of eastern Poland's voivodeships to the economic growth, includes the following: environmentally friendly agriculture and high quality food model, scientific  activity  in  the  area  of  biotechnology,  development  of  specialist  services using  the  local  scientific  and  research  infrastructure,  international  logistics  in  the  east-west direction, the production of biofuels.

Regional  policy  offers  a  solution  in  this  regard  consisting  in  launching  appropriate  external interventions  to  eliminate  barriers  to  development  and  to  enable  better  use  of  endogenous potential of the given region, but also to obtain access to the necessary exogenous factors. To ensure  cohesion  at  the  national  level,  strategic  intervention  will  include  actions  aimed  at strengthening the existing network of towns/cities and facilitating urban, human resources and social capital development as well as business development and creation of infrastructural and institutional conditions for increasing investment and labour productivity.

The territory at risk of marginalization covers mainly the rural areas with the worst indicators of socioeconomic development and the lowest level of the access of residents to services and goods determining the development opportunities. They are characterized by high levels of employment in agriculture, low levels of entrepreneurship development, a large percentage of people with low education level and low professional skills, low income levels of local budgets, resulting in low level of investment and infrastructure. In addition, these areas may experience depopulation  and  defeminisation. They  are  often  too  distant  and  poorly  communicated  with urban centres.

> 28 Categories  of  functional  areas  are  characterized  in  the  National  Spatial  Development  Concept  2030 adopted by the Council of Ministers on 13 December 2011.

In  rural  areas  with  the  poorest  development  prospects,  actions  are  planned  to  reduce  the differences in the level of access to services (education, health, culture, sports, transport, business consulting, local transport infrastructure, infrastructure for investments in non-agricultural sectors of  the  economy),  which  determine  the  inclusion  of  the  inhabitants  of  these  areas  in  regional development processes.

The exact boundaries of areas of strategic intervention will be determined in the Regional Development Strategy and Regional Spatial Development Plan based on the most appropriate indicators for the region illustrating the severity of various problems at the regional level.

120

[[END OF PAGE 120]]

[[START OF PAGE 121]]

Another  group  of  areas  at  risk  of  marginalization  consists  of  urban  areas  and  urban  areas  of former industry centres, the growth rate of which significantly decreased. They are affected by impoverishment of the population and a decline in investment. The problem of concentration of poverty, social pathology, and loss of economic functions increasingly affects also some districts situated in the most dynamic cities.

Map  2:  Areas  of  strategic  intervention  for  restructuring  and  revitalization  of  cities  losing  their  socio-economic functions 30

Source: MRD.

The  challenge  in  regard  to  the  above  areas  is  the  use  of  restructuring  instruments  and  the instruments  of  recovery  for  large  urban  areas,  support  for  a  comprehensive  revitalization  and restructuring of the socio-economic areas in the smaller spatial scales. This challenge involves the introduction of a national urban policy as an important element of regional policy.

The  areas  at  risk  of  marginalization  processes  include  also  some  border  areas  particularly vulnerable to adverse events associated with the low national territorial availability, cultural and social differences and the drain of endogenous regional potentials by foreign regions. The regional policy towards these types of territories addresses measures designed to reduce barriers, increase economic cooperation, political and intercultural dialogue and to increase the competitiveness and attractiveness in both economic and social terms, enabling a full use of endogenous development potentials. The measures stimulating the economy in the areas situated on the borders with nonEU members, i.e. Russia, Belarus and Ukraine, are particularly important.

> 30 Map  shows  an  approximate  group  of  cities  at  which  the  intervention  will  be  targeted.  An  accurate determination of degraded areas and those parts of cities that require intervention will take place at the regional level. These areas will be identified in the Regional Development Strategies and Regional Spatial Development Plans.

121

[[END OF PAGE 121]]

[[START OF PAGE 122]]

One  of  the  major  drivers  of  economic  growth  and  the  spread  of  development  processes  is  a modern  transport infrastructure, understood functionally as a condition to open other possibilities  of  using  resources  and  development  opportunities. Poor  transport  accessibility  of some Polish areas, especially the cities  where social  and  economic functions are concentrated and  the  most  important  government  authorities  are  located,  is  one  of  the  major  barriers  to development.

Actions planned within the framework of regional policy in order to provide adequate transport capacity  will  improve  access  to  centres  of  innovation,  science  and  knowledge  centres,  labour markets,  education,  health  care,  etc.,  while  helping  spread  the  effects  of  innovation  to  the peripheries and counteracting marginalization processes.

An  important  objective  of  support  for  the  border  areas  is  to  develop  border  infrastructure, including that of local importance. Its absence is often the main barrier (also understood literally) for cross-border cooperation.

# IMPLEMENTATION FRAMEWORK

## Financial framework

One of the most important challenges for development policy in the medium-term horizon of the national  development strategy is to implement the planned modernization measures, while at the  same  time  reducing  the  general  government 31 sector  deficit,  and  then  to  achieve  and maintain the medium-term budgetary objective, i.e. the structural deficit at 1% of GDP. The main objective of the economic policy of the government in the medium-term is to create conditions for  fast  and  sustainable  growth,  while  at  the  same  time  ensuring  the  optimal  pace  of  public finance  consolidation  (in  accordance  with  the  successive Convergence  programmes ).  The development policy should therefore be implemented so as to maintain public debt within the limits  defined  by  the  Constitution  and  the  Stability  and  Growth  Pact.  Public  debt  and  deficit should be reduced using the means which do not hamper the development of the country.

Income  and  expenditure  of  the  general  government  by  2020  is  difficult  to  define.  It  will  be influenced by the developments in the global economy, particularly in the euro area, on the one hand, and the readiness to implement structural reforms in Poland, on the other hand. The scale of  EU  funds  that  will  be  available  under  the  financial  perspective  2014-2020  is  also  unclear. Therefore it  is  not  possible  to  precisely  determine  the  expenditure  necessary to  complete the planned development activities. The proposed financial framework is based on departure from funding  these  activities  with  the  general  government  deficit  and  focus  on  the  changes  in  the structure of public expenditure. Therefore, the primary assumption for determining the MTNDS financial  framework  is  the  increase  in  spending  on  development  activities  in  total  public expenditure  by reducing the expenditure  in the areas that generate  the smallest prodevelopment  impulse  in  favour  of  the  pro-development  areas  rather  than  by  increasing  the general government deficit.

MTNDS  2020  points  to  the  need  to  carry  out  specific  investments,  but  is  an  orientation document.  According  to  the  hierarchical system  of planning, individual projects will be implemented  on  the  basis  of  programmes,  plans  and  other  operational  documents  of  the national  strategic management system and adopted by the competent authorities at both the national  and  the  regional  level.  These  documents  will  determine  the  amount  and  method  of financing, and the exact year of implementation. They should become the basis for drafting longterm financial plans of the state, budget acts and the multiannual financial forecasts for LGUs, local  government  budgets  and  financial  plans  of  other  public  sector  entities.  The  planned investment projects should be carried out within the limit of funds planned in the state budget for the given year and should not constitute a basis for applying for additional funding from the state  budget.  Where  the  EU  funds  will  be  used  for  the  project  implementation,  they  will  be allocated  under  the  relevant  operational  programmes  or  other  programming  documents. Financial assets of private entities, including those involved in infrastructure development, will play an important role in the implementation of MTNDS 2020.

Investment plans resulting from MTNDS provide a certain framework- they define the desired investment, but leave their implementation to the relevant institutions and make it dependant on  changing  financial  and  administrative  capabilities.  The  financial  framework  is  based  on  the desired  scale  of  funding  for  implementation  of  projects  in  various  areas  of  socio-economic development in the next ten years.

> 31 Debt of the general government.

124

[[END OF PAGE 124]]

[[START OF PAGE 125]]

### Level of funding for development activities

Development expenditure for 2011-2020 was estimated based on the following macroeconomic assumptions 32: :  GDP  growth  rate  of  3.5%  a  year  on  average  until  2020,  continuation  of  fiscal consolidation  -  a  gradual  decline  in  expenditure  of  the  general  government  sector  and  a reduction  of  the  deficit  to  1%  of  GDP.  The  estimates  took  into  account  the  effects  of  public finance reforms, including an increase in disability pension contributions, introduction of taxes on certain minerals. The adopted assumptions resulting from the fiscal consolidation mean that a necessary  condition  for  meeting  the  needs  of  national  development  is  the  rationalization  of public expenditure and its reorientation towards those activities that are crucial for the socioeconomic  and  spatial  development  of  the  country,  while  reducing  the  general  government deficit. Such an approach should allow to increase the development expenditure to 17.6% of GDP in 2020 (against 16.4% in 2010).

The Efficient  and  effective  state, a  strategic  area  of  MTNDS,  presupposes  a  change  in  the structure of public expenditure, consisting in a shift from the current expenditure to development  expenditure.  The  development  expenditure 33 includes  expenditure  on:  research and development (including fundamental research), education, health, culture, economic issues (agriculture, transport, and telecommunications), environmental protection (waste management, reduction of pollution), water supply.

The strategic area of Competitive economy (Objectives II.2, II.3 and II.4) identifies research and development, technology diffusion and improving the skills of the labour force as areas that are of  particular  importance  for  increasing  innovation  in  the  economy  and  accelerating  growth. Increased  outlays  on  R&D  will  be  particularly  important  for  the  creation  of  new  technologies, innovation  and  implementation  of  research  results.  At  the  same  time,  thanks  to  increased spending  on  R&D,  it  will  be  possible  to  achieve  other  objectives  of  the  Strategy,  namely  to improve  the  condition  of  the  environment,  reduce  the  energy  consumption,  promote  egovernment services and the use of digital technologies in the economy.

Maintaining the funds on education at the current level, their re-allocation and more efficient use will allow to build human capital faster in Poland. Objective II.4 Development of human capital also focuses on increasing the labour force participation. The achievement of this objective will be possible through higher spending on institutional care for small children (development of the network  of  child  care  institutions  for  children  up  to  age  of  3  and  kindergartens),  and  higher education  (to  provide  qualified  personnel  for  the  economy).  The  labour  force  participation  of people aged 45-65 may also be improved by the proposed reform of the social security system and labour market. This will contribute to the reduction of social marginalisation (Objective III.1 Social Inclusion).

The construction of modern transport connections will increase the accessibility of the country (thus  contributing  to  greater  territorial  cohesion,  III.3  Strengthening  the  diffusion  mechanisms and spatial integration in order to develop and make a full use of the regional potentials), reduce

· category of environmental protection: waste management (80%) and reduction of pollutants,

· category: health,

· category of housing and utilities: water supply (50%)),

· category of recreation, culture and religion: cultural services,

125

[[END OF PAGE 125]]

[[START OF PAGE 126]]

the number of road accidents (Objective I.3.3. Increasing the citizen security) and transport costs. In  the  long  run  it  will  be  possible  to  close  the  infrastructure  gap  between  EU15  and  Poland (Objective II.7. Increasing the efficiency of management in the transport sector).

The challenges in public health, resulting from demographic processes, should also be taken into account. Population ageing and the need to prevent shrinkage of the labour force requires not only  the  increase  (starting  from  2013),  but  also  the  reorientation  of  expenditure  in  this  area. Improving the accessibility of health services and better management of public health contribute to the objectives: III.2. Ensuring access and specific standards of public services, I.3.3. Increasing the citizen security, III.3.4. Improving territorial cohesion. This will be achieved in particular by increasing  the  expenditure  on  research  and  development,  medical  equipment  and  prevention (Public health department in the COFOG classification).

In order to implement the development projects while maintaining macroeconomic stability, public expenditure on these activities in 2020 should reach 17.6% of GDP. This means that it could be possible to allocate about PLN 3 billion of public funds on the development activities in 2011-2020.

### Development expenditure structure

Most of the development expenditure in the period 2011-2020 will be allocated for three areas: education, health care and economic issues (including transport, agriculture and telecommunications, including digitization). Those areas of intervention will receive more than 88% (PLN 2 621.8 billion) of the total development expenditure, which accounts for about 15.4% of GDP.

It  should  be  noted  that  despite  the  steady  growth  of  expenditure  for education, its  share  in development  expenditure  will  gradually  decrease  from  33.0%  in  2011  to  30.5%  in  2020.  The share  of  expenditure  on health  care in  development  expenditure  will  increase  from  30.2%  in 2011  to  33.9%  in  2020.  In  the  case  of  expenditure  on  education,  its  share  of  GDP  will  be maintained at the same level until 2020, but there will be a reorientation in its structure.

In the  initial  period,  the  share  of  expenditure  on economic  affairs in  total  development expenditure will not change and will amount to 26.0% in 2015, and next, after a gradual decline, it  will  total  22.9%  in  2020. The  major  costs  in  this  category  are  transport  costs,  amounting  to about  96%  of  expenditure  in this group, which  represents approximately  24%  of  total development  expenditure  in  the  period  2011-2020. The  share  of  transport  expenditure  will increase until 2014, reaching a level of 25% of development expenditure (which will constitute 4.3% of GDP), and then will decrease to 22.0% in 2020 (3.9% of GDP). The structure of transport expenditure will change (especially after 2015), with the share of expenditure on rail transport increasing. This will be related the increase of EU funds for such projects, on the one hand, and to the implementation of railway projects in Poland and the high standards of multimodal transport in big cities and their functional areas, on the other hand.

It is estimated that approximately PLN 228 billion will be spent on this area, of which about PLN 120 billion will come from public funds. #### Key investment activities in the period until 2020

- Broadband Internet

- Network of motorways, expressways and bypasses

- Modernisation of railway lines, replacement of rolling stock, modernization of railway stations

- Modernization and expansion of airports

- Strengthening maritime transport links

- Nuclear power

- Transmission networks

- Storage of gas, oil and liquid fuels

- Waste management, sewage treatment infrastructure

The  projects  resulting  from  MTNDS  will  be  further  defined  during  the  creation  of  long-term operational  plans  and  programmes  with  a  medium-term  perspective.  Only  a  part  of  the expenditure that must be made by 2013/2015 (depending on the sector) has been specified in detail in the applicable documents. It should also be noted that in the case of large infrastructure projects such as roads, a significant part of the costs may be the expenditure on the purchase of land (in the case of investment in new routes), whose value may change over time.

The distribution of funds for development in terms of nine integrated strategies is presented in Table 4. The distribution suggests that some development activities financed from public funds will  be  implemented  under  several  integrated  strategies. At  the  programming  stage  of  the development activities  it  is  difficult  to  determine  the  precise  scope  of  intervention  in  specific areas  under  their  assigned  strategies. This  will  be  only  possible  at  the  operational  stage  upon formulation of  programmes and division  of  activities  between  individual  entities. The  National Strategy for Regional Development is a special case, since it does not assign individual funds for its  objectives  and  priorities. They  are  implemented  using  mainly  sectoral  instruments,  and therefore under other integrated strategies.

### Sources of development activities' funding

In  order  to  achieve  the  expenditure for development defined in MTNDS, additional sources of financing of the proposed projects must be found. They may include:

-EU budget;

-foreign direct investment;

-reforms of the labour market and the social security system, increasing the  supply  of  labour,  productive  employment  and  thus  the  number  of persons paying the contributions and taxes;

-changes in the tax system;

-increased revenues from environmental taxes (including the ETS system of  emission  charges)  and  the  introduction  of  charges  for  using  the transport infrastructure;

-additional public revenues or savings in other expenditure than spending on development, generated by constant monitoring of the funding needs of the reforms.

It  is  assumed that under the EU financial perspective for 2007-2013 ,  Poland will obtain nearly PLN 284 billion in the years 2011-2013, including almost PLN 216 billion for implementation of the  National  Cohesion  Strategy  2011-2013  (in  line  with  the  n  +2  spending  rule  until  the  end 2015). Actions related to the restructuring of agriculture and rural development under the Rural Development Programme 2007-2013 may receive the financing of PLN 35.5 billion in the years 2011-2013 (in line with the n +2 spending rule until the end 2015). Significant funding from the EU  budget  will  be  transferred  to  Poland  in  form  of  direct  payments  under  the  Common Agricultural Policy (PLN 31 billion in the years 2011-2013).

> 39 The expenses under the 'defense' function according to COFOG classification will cover only expenditure on  R&D.  While  the  expenditure  included  in  the  NSS  financial  framework  also  covers  other  expenses provided for the implementation of intervention concerning the defense needs of the State, the annual financing  of  which,  in  accordance  with  applicable  law,  should  amount  to  at  least  1.95%  of  GDP  for  the previous year (Act of 25 May 2001 on reconstruction, modernization and financing of the Polish Armed Forces (Journal of Laws of 2009 No 67, item 570, No 157, item 1241 of 2011 No 81, item 439).

132

[[END OF PAGE 132]]

[[START OF PAGE 133]]

In the 2014-2020 perspective the EU funding is expected to remain at a level similar to the one under the 2007-2013 perspective, namely within the limits of PLN 400-440 billion. Final decisions concerning  the  amount  of  the  EU  funds  will  depend  on  the  development  of  socio-economic situation in the euro area and the results of the negotiation on the package of regulations related to the EU financial perspective 2014-2020. The EU funds are therefore an important source of financing for development activities; however, they require the contribution from national public funds in line with the principle of co-financing and pre-financing.

Private economic operators will play an important role in the financing of the widely understood development of the country thanks to their own investments . Public-private partnership, which has not been widely used thus far, will be an essential tool for integrating private initiatives with the country's development activities. The mechanisms and conditions for the functioning of the instrument will have to be simplified. The National Bank of Poland estimates that the FDI inflow to Poland increased to approximately EUR 12.7 billion in 2011. This growth results from increased flows of capital on an international scale, the relatively high investment attractiveness of Poland and an increase in reinvested earnings. The FDI inflow is expected in industries where foreign companies look for opportunities to cut costs, and therefore in the area of widely understood services, primarily in the creation of business service centres and shared service centres (SSC). Such investment requires much less capital, while creating a relatively large number of jobs.

Additional funds may be found through a reallocation of expenditure from current spending to development  expenditure.  It  is  mainly  related  to  the  reduction  of  social  transfers  (due  to structural  reforms).  Introducing  an  equal  retirement  age  for  men  and  women  and  a  gradual increase  of  this  age  allowing  to  simultaneously  decrease  the  expenditure  for  retirement pensions, increase revenues from social security contributions and from personal income tax. At the same time, the labour force participation of women aged 25-44, men aged 25-54 and young people (15-24 years) will need to increase. A gradual integration of special pension schemes and rationalization of pension expenditure provided for in MTNDS will result in synergies for public finances, consisting in reducing the pension liabilities of the state and increasing the revenues from contributions.

Due to demographic changes, the growth of expenditure on education is expected.

Moreover, in the long run, i.e. from 2020, maintenance of the constructed road and motorway network will be facilitated by adopting a system of charges for the use of transport infrastructure.

It  is  estimated that the introduction of structural reforms and tax changes will allow to obtain 0.7% of GDP  in additional government  revenue an average in the period 2013-2020, accumulatively by 1.2% of GDP in 2020. In particular, the structural reforms of the labour market should  generate  positive  external  effects  in  the  form  of  increased  labour  resources  and  an increased employment rate, up to 64% for those aged 15+, 69% for women and 76% for men aged  15-64  years.  It  may  also  result  in  a  higher  rate  of  economic  growth  and  income  of  the population and in the reduction of social exclusion. This would allow to achieve the objectives of the Europe 2020 strategy in terms of employment and social cohesion.

133

[[END OF PAGE 133]]

[[START OF PAGE 134]]

A gradual introduction of fees for CO 2 emissions starting from 2013 would result in a significant increase in budget revenues (up to 0.6% of GDP in 2020). At the same time, potential sources of additional  revenue will  be  constantly monitored,  with  particular  emphasis on  indirect  taxes  as those that are the least distorting for the allocation of labour resources and capital. The proposed changes in the tax system would also contribute to increasing the investment and savings, which is consistent with objective II.1.2 Efficient state.

### Summary

Financing all development priorities set for 2020 will require substantial public (national and EU) funds.  The  analyses  show  that  the  implementation  of  the  development  activities  planned  in MTNDS will require the spending of about PLN 3 trillion of public funds in the years 2011-2020, which constitutes about 17.3% of GDP. It is assumed that 23-25% of the indicated development expenditure in the analysed period will be financed by the EU funds. Private funds will constitute an  additional  source  of  funding  for  development  activities,  particularly  in  the  area  of  R&D, transport, or the main  source  of financing in the area of energy, port, airport and telecommunications  infrastructure  (matching  funds  from  the  EU  and  the  associated  national public funds allocated for co-financing).

Implementation of an ambitious vision of Poland in 2020 in the context of an ageing population also  entails  the  need  to  increase  state  revenues  and  reduce  the  deficit  arising  from  social transfers.

Although  Poland  is  still  backward,  especially  in  terms  of  basic  infrastructure,  the  estimated amount of funds for development is sufficient to finance the priorities identified in MTNDS 2020 and 9 integrated strategies. Such expenditure also appears to correspond to the capacity of the Polish economy and guarantees the country's macroeconomic stability.

An  important  task  of  the  state  is  to  constantly  increase  the  national  contribution  in  total expenditure for development so that by the end of the decade it would possible to replace the financing of development measures from the EU funds, which are gradually decreasing, with the national funds.

The structural reforms and measures reducing the general government deficit that are provided for  in  MTNDS  should  allow  to  achieve  and  maintain  the  medium-term  budgetary  objective (structural  deficit  of  1%  of  GDP)  and  the  accomplishment  of  development  objectives  in  the perspective  of  10  years,  thus  creating  a  favourable  ground  for  modernization  activities  in  the long-term. The failure to carry out the reforms would result in Poland remaining a country with low competitiveness and prosperity, and also with growing fiscal problems.

[[END OF PAGE 134]]

## Implementation system

### Entities and coordination

Programming and implementation of individual policies and strategies under the development policy in a broad sense will be based on the principles of the so-called multilateral coordination and multi-level management by means of an active participation of actors from various levels of public administration and other entities of the social and economic life.

The  Council  of  Ministers  is  responsible  for  the  development  policy  implementation  at  the national  level,  while  local  government  units  bear  this  responsibility  at  the  regional  and  local levels.  The  assumption  is  that  the  MTNDS,  as  a  key  strategic  document  of  the  development policy,  should  integrate  all  entities  that  can  participate  and  contribute  to  the  development process, including public institutions at central and regional level, as well as economic and social circles,  around the strategic objectives of the policy. This will require efficient coordination, at the programming, planning and implementation stages, in order to obtain synergies necessary for the effective achievement  of the  MTNDS  objectives. This principle will govern the implementation mechanisms in all development strategies and programmes, including rules on the project identification and funding.

Determination of the strategic objectives of the state and the major programming decisions rest with the Council of Ministers. The Prime Minister directly supervises the strategic management. The adopted development management system increases the personal involvement of the Prime Minister at the programming stage of strategic government documents. This applies particularly to  the  long-term national development strategy, the preparation and consultation of which is, pursuant to the Act of 6 December 2006 on the principles of development policy, coordinated by the Prime Minister or his delegated representative.

The Coordinating Committee for Development Policy (CC) is an advisory and consulting body of the  Prime  Minister  in  the  area  of  development  policy  whose  task  is  to  ensure  an  effective coordination  of  programming  and  implementation  of  development  policy,  as  well  as  strategic monitoring  and  evaluation  of  its  implementing  instruments.  The  CC  is  chaired  by  the  Prime Minister or his delegated representative which ensures his direct supervision of the implementation of the national development policy.

Ministers  and  heads  of  central  offices  provide  the  Committee  with  the  key  programming documents and materials relating to their implementation and evaluation, as well as any other studies  concerning  the  programming,  implementation  and  evaluation  of  development  policy (including recommendations for its amendments).

The minister responsible for regional development plays an important role in the development and coordination of development policy. The minister is responsible for development programming in the medium-term (including the preparation of the draft medium-term national development  strategy  and  its  updates,  the  national  strategy  for  regional  development,  transregional strategies), definition of methodological and professional standards and organizational procedures of the programming process as well as the enforcement of their observance by the entities  involved  in  this  process  with  regard  to  all  government  policies  constituting  the development policy. The minister in charge of regional development also prepares the National Spatial Development Concept which includes the long-term perspective.

The  main tool allowing the minister responsible for regional policy to coordinate the programming of the national development policy in the horizontal aspect is the assessment of compliance of the government strategies and development programmes with the medium-term national development strategy (i.e. the National Development Strategy 2020). The opinion of the minister responsible for regional development is required for the adoption of the document by the Council of Ministers.

135

[[END OF PAGE 135]]

[[START OF PAGE 136]]

The coordinators in the individual ministries are responsible for the preparation and implementation of integrated strategies. Based on integrated strategies, they determine which programmes or other instruments must be adopted for appropriate and effective implementation of these strategies.

A  special  role  in  coordinating  the  development  policy  consisting  in  ensuring  effective  and efficient financing for the development policy belongs to the minister in charge of budget and public  finances  who  is  responsible  for  i.a.  preparation  of  the  Multi-Year  State  Financial  Plan (MYSFP i.e. a plan of income and expenditure, revenues and spending of the budget for next four financial years, which is updated annually).

The MYSFP includes medium-term objectives of national development strategy and objectives of socio-economic policy of the Council of Ministers. Multi-Year State Financial Plan includes i.a. a forecast  of  budget  expenditure,  including  the  expenditure  on  national  development  activities, based  on  the  priorities  and  main  directions  of  intervention  indicated  in  the  medium-term development strategy. The MYSFP is the basis for preparation of the Budget Acts for subsequent years of its application and is, along with the performance budget, the basic instrument to ensure consistency of the budget planning and strategic programming.

The  voivodeship  government  plays  an  essential  role  in  the  programming,  management  and coordination  of  development  measures  at  the  regional  level  .  Its  most  important  include programming  of  development  activities  in  the  region  (voivodeship  development  strategy), building  cooperation  networks  at  the  regional  level,  coordinating  the  development  activities implemented  in  the  region  and  supervision  of  the  regional  policy  implementation  using appropriate  monitoring  and  evaluation  mechanisms.  Local  governments  which  are  involved  in the development policy implementation at the regional and local level at both the programming and  implementation  stages  of  development  activities,  constitute  an  important  element  of  the regional cooperation network .

Other  entities  involved  in  the  implementation  of  the  development  policy  objectives  on  a partnership basis include the representatives of socio-economic partners, associations and NGOs, academic circles and private entities. Their participation at all stages of the processes related to programming  and  implementation  of  the  development  activities  ensures  the  efficiency  and effectiveness of these processes by building broad public support and trust between the public entities and their partners with the aim of achieving the development objectives.

### Implementation instruments - recommended strategies and programmes

The  strategic  objectives  identified  in  MTNDS  are  implemented  in  the  form  of  integrated development  strategies  that  go  beyond  traditional  sectoral  framework  and  comprehensively cover the main areas of the country's development. MTNDS defines the strategic tasks of the state.  While  ensuring  consistency  between  actions  at  the  national  and  regional  level,  the objectives of the strategy, pursuant to the Act on voivodeship government, should be included in the development strategies of the voivodeships.

The instrument for coordination of development activities at the national and regional level is a territorial contract. The territorial contract allows to establish common objectives in relation to a specific  territory  and  coordinate  development  activities  by  both  the  central  and  the  local government. Such contract includes  activities  implemented  at  the  national  level  (by  individual ministries, to the extent indicated as a condition for the implementation of other activities at the regional  level)  and  at  the  regional  level  (activities  implemented  by  various  units  of  local government  -  voivodeship,  poviat,  gmina).  As  a  result,  the  contract  defines  the  directions  of intervention  of  the  competent  ministers  and  various  local  governments  (voivodeship,  poviat, gmina).  At  the  national  level,  the  government  is  represented  by  the  minister  responsible  for regional  development.  The  local  government  (voivodeship,  poviat  or  gmina  government)  is represented by the board or boards of voivodeships (in case of a contract covering an area larger than the area of the voivodeship).

136

[[END OF PAGE 136]]

[[START OF PAGE 137]]

The  National  Reform  Programme  for  the  implementation  of  the  Europe  2020  strategy  (NRP), which is the basic element of the management system in the European Union Member States, is an important tool for implementation of the development objectives. This document focuses on major  reforms  for  smart,  sustainable  and  inclusive  growth,  resulting  from  the  Europe  2020 objectives  and  on  the  barriers  to  growth  for  Poland.  The  NRP  incorporates  and  clarifies  those elements  of  the  medium-term  strategy  and  9  integrated  strategies,  which  contribute  to  the achievement of the Europe 2020 objectives and implementation of its flagship initiatives.

The  implementation  tools  for  integrated  strategies  include  the  operational  and  development programmes,  including multiannual programmes.  The  process  of organizing the existing development  programs,  involving  their  verification,  possibly  combining  them  into  broader thematic  blocks  and  identification  of  the  lacking  ones,  will  be  conducted  based  on  the  needs resulting from the adopted integrated strategies.

The measures that need to be undertaken by the ministers in charge of individual sections of government  administration  within  their  competences  are  identified  based  on  regulations concerning the management control of the public finances within the framework of annual action plans. They should include the tasks performed under the specific sections of the government administration  that  implement  development  objectives  set  out  in  the  above  mentioned documents.

### Monitoring and reporting

Under the current system, the implementation of the adopted strategic objectives under the medium-term national development strategy, was monitored mainly based on the report on the implementation of the medium-term national development strategy drawn up annually by the minister responsible for regional development, using the contributions from individual ministries. Pursuant to the Act on development policy, the Prime Minister shall forward such a report to the Sejm and the Senate by 31 July each year.

The experience from the functioning of the MTNDS implementation system so far points to the necessity of its modification by linking the strategic planning level with the operational level. A new tool, i.e. the MTNDS Action Plan, will be used for this purpose.

The  minister  in  charge  of  regional  development  will  still  be  responsible  for  coordinating  and monitoring the implementation of MTNDS. The competent ministers will be responsible for the implementation of the various elements of the Strategy.

#### MTNDS Action Plan - introduction of the principle of linking strategic and operational planning

MTNDS  Action  Plan  will  constitute  a  document  for  the  operationalization  of  a  medium-term strategy and the main tool for ongoing monitoring of progress in its implementation.

The plan will specify the following, as for the objectives (priorities) resulting from MTNDS:

- head2right List of key projects planned for this period, broken down into:

- square4 legislative changes,

- square4 institutional changes,

- square4 interventions/ investment programmes.

- head2right Ministries responsible individual projects;

- head2right Performance indicators of individual projects.

137

[[END OF PAGE 137]]

[[START OF PAGE 138]]

The plan will be prepared by the minister responsible for regional development.

The implementation of the Plan will be regularly monitored on an annual basis. Reports on the implementation of the Plan, together with recommendations for actions necessary to ensure its timely  and  effective  implementation,  will  be  examined    by  the  Coordinating  Committee  for Development Policy.

The  annual  reviews  of  the  MTNDS  Action  Plan,  adopted  by  the  Council  of  Ministers,  will constitute  a  tool  for  monitoring  the  medium-term  strategy.  At  the  same  time,  the  annual information on the MTNDS implementation will be submitted to the Sejm and the Senate, in line with the current practice and statutory requirements.

The  basic  point  of  reference  for  monitoring  the  MTNDS  Action  Plan,  and  thereby  the  MTNDS itself,  will  be  the  key  indicators  included  in  this  document,  which  should  be  elaborated  and complemented in the integrated strategies and individual programmes.

The  strategic  management  system  is  to  create  a  nationwide,  comprehensive  system  of  key indicators  for  designing  the  systems  for  monitoring  the  implementation  of  strategies  and development programmes, as well as the process of development monitoring at the national and regional level. The key indicators will be included in the publicly accessible electronic database of statistical data used for monitoring at the national level and at the level of regional units. This set of structural indicators will enable the institutions compiling strategic documents to quickly and easily obtain the information necessary to monitor the strategic documents.

### Evaluation and risk analysis

Mechanisms  and  standards  for  monitoring  and  evaluation,  developed  for  the  needs  of  the European cohesion policy in Poland, are increasingly being used to evaluate other public policies, including those financed from national sources.

MTNDS, in  accordance  with  the  requirements  of  the  Act  on  development  policy  principles,  is subject to ex ante evaluation (i.e. assessment of the effectiveness and efficiency of the strategic document before its adoption).

An ex ante 40 evaluation was carried out for the MTNDS draft and included:

-Assessment  of  the  socio-economic  diagnosis  and  assessment  of  the  accuracy  of  the selection of objectives of the strategy in the context of the diagnosis;

-Quality assessment of the assumptions for the implementation system, in particular:

⇒ Effectiveness and efficiency of the support instruments adopted in the implementation system,

⇒ Verification  of  adequacy  and  compliance  of  the  proposed  indicators  with  the objectives,

-Evaluation of the appropriateness of the identified state policy objectives, objectives of intervention, strategic tasks of the state and the estimated efficacy of the instruments for their implementation;

-Evaluation  of  the  estimated  strategy  impact,  defined  as  the  expected  impact  of  the strategy on the socio-economic development of the country;

-Evaluation  of  the  inclusion  of  the  findings  from  the  'Poland  2030.  Development Challenges'  and  the  draft  'Long-term  National  Development  Strategy',  'Europe  2020

> 40 Final Report. Ex ante evaluation of National Development Strategy 2020., Zachodniopomorska Advisory Group LLc, Szczecin, October 2011

138

[[END OF PAGE 138]]

[[START OF PAGE 139]]

Strategy', 'Renewed  EU  Sustainable Development  Strategy' in  MTNDS  and  the consistency between these documents and MTNDS.

The  evaluation  of  the  effects  of  the  medium-term  strategy,  which  is  part  of  the  ex-ante evaluation, was performed using the economic impact assessment, complemented by the Delphi survey.

Two extreme variants of the conditions for the implementation of the Strategy were adopted to assess  the  effects  of  the  Strategy.  The  first  variant,  described  as  pessimistic,  assumed  that development activities are conducted in the situation where many adverse external factors are present (such as the deepening global financial crisis) have a negative impact on the situation in the country. The second variant, an optimistic one, did not foresee the circumstances that would prevent the implementation of the strategy.

The presented evaluation of the effects of actions taken under pessimistic variant leads to the conclusion that the economic position of Poland in comparison with other countries should not deteriorate and may even slightly improve. This is due to the fact that in the event of a crisis other countries will be in a similar situation as Poland and due to a relatively good current socioeconomic  condition  of  the  country.  If  the  optimistic  variant  materializes,  the  competitive economy and effectively functioning public institutions will ensure macroeconomic stability and further dynamic development of Poland.

Another possible scenario is that the implementation of strategic tasks in the area of efficient and effective  state will  allow  to  fundamentally  improve  the  quality  of  the  functioning  of  state institutions. It will be possible as a result of i.a.  the entrepreneurship development, socialization of  the  decision  making  processes,  increase  in  the  decision-making  competence,  better  use  of resources (including aid), better spatial order and improvement of public safety.

According  to  estimates,  the  implementation  of  the  tasks  in  the  strategic  area  of Competitive economy will create solid foundations for strengthening the macroeconomic policy as the basis for  the  functioning  of  the  economy,  more  effective  activities  of  the  state,  reduction  of  the investment  risk  and  encouragement  for  investment,  savings  and  generating  of  investment capital.  Information  and  communications  technologies  will  be  implemented  on  a  large  scale which  will  allow  to  improve  the  management  and  information  flow.  Moreover,  the  economic efficiency  and  the  condition  of  the  natural  environment will  be  improved.  Energy  security  will guarantee  the  uninterrupted  functioning  of  the  economy  as  the  diversification  of  the  supply should reduce the costs of energy and fuel supply.

According  to  the  evaluators,  the  implementation  of  tasks  in  the  strategic  area  of Social  and territorial cohesion will allow to increase the efficiency of the expenditure on social services and elimination of pathologies and to promote the activity of the excluded, as well as to improve the social  security.  Moreover,  it  should  boost  the  interregional  cooperation  and  generate  and support sustainable economic development.

The  evaluation  report  included  a  conclusion  that  the  planned  interventions  should  generate lasting  effects  in  the  area  of  development,  provided  that  the  authorities  are  determined  to implement such measures. The majority of objectives set forth in the strategy will most likely be accomplished, although to various extent.

In  addition  to  the  estimation  of  the  effects  of  the  strategy,  the  evaluation  report  contained conclusions  and  recommendations  on  the  structure  and  content  of  the  document.  They  were used to add a separate diagnostic part to the document which covers 15 areas coinciding with the main areas of intervention of the National Development Strategy 2020. The comments on the indicators  and  data  sources  were  taken  into  account.  The  structure  of  the  strategic  part presenting the financial framework has changed substantially compared to the draft submitted for evaluation.

139

[[END OF PAGE 139]]

[[START OF PAGE 140]]

The evaluation based on the analysis of the document and the opinions of experts concluded that the strategic areas, objectives and priorities of intervention had been defined accurately. It also confirms their compliance with the requirements of the Act on the principles of development policy and with key national and EU strategic documents. The monitoring system was described as  consistent  with  the  assumptions  of  the  whole  document  implementation  system  and  the proposed support instruments were rated as having a potential for efficiency and effectiveness.

The evaluation report also included proposals for certain adjustments that have been used.

*

MTNDS  implementation  will  be  accompanied  by  comprehensive  projects  for  evaluation  of activities conducted by public administration units in different sectors of the economy, as well as the wider public policies. It is a continuous process, serving as a tool to assess the effectiveness of  public  policies  and  actions  under  those  policies.  This  will  be  supported  by  co-operation, particularly in the evaluation of policies and actions for achieving the MTNDS objectives.

A  report  on  the  socio-economic,  regional  and  spatial  development,  including  the  analysis  of development  directions,  evaluation  of  the  effectiveness  of  the  implementation  of  national development objectives stated in the individual policies and programmes and recommendations for national development policy, including the development of regions, sectors and areas, as well as spatial development, which is drawn up by the minister responsible for regional development every three years, is an important element supporting the evaluation of conducted development activities. The report assesses the level and pace of development in these areas against the EU background.  The  conclusions  of  the  report  can  serve  as  the  basis  for  updating  the  existing strategies and programs.

The  introduced  system  of  strategic  documents  based  on  LTNDS,  MTNDS,  nine  integrated strategies and their implementing programmes requires a comprehensive evaluation and analysis in  order  to  avoid  excessive  expansion  of  the  evaluation  system  in  each  of  these  documents, duplication of analyses and studies, and thus the dispersion of budget funds.

[[START OF PAGE 141]]

# STRATEGIC TASKS OF THE STATE IN THE 2020 PERSPECTIVE

## Tasks of systemic nature

- Consolidation of public finances.

- Modernization of the structure of public spending, including increasing the share of development expenditure (reallocation of public expenditure, gradual replacement of expenditure from the EU funds with national funding).

- The gradual increase and equalisation of the retirement age of women and men over time, starting from 2013.

- Increasing the efficiency of social benefits (illness, maternity, invalidity, accidents at work, unemployment, old age)

- Changes in competences of public administration at central and local government level and new management methods.

- Changes in the management of public policies (including territorialisation of public policies) and principles of financing the resulting tasks.

- Improving the justice system resulting in shorter average duration of judicial proceedings (change in the management of courts, simplification of court procedures) and changes in the organizational and procedural system of the prosecution system.

- Determining the standards of public services (valuation of public services) and introduction of the public services management.

- Supporting the development of entrepreneurship (elimination of legal and administrative barriers, improvement of the access to capital, strengthening entrepreneurship, institutional, organizational and financial strengthening of business environment).

- Promoting export and investments (financial instruments, Polish investments abroad, inflow of innovative FDI, and coherent system of promoting the Polish economy).

- Introducing the innovation support system (preferences for innovation - the diversity of instruments, linking science and business, increasing investment in R & D, use and targeting of FDI)

- Identifying and supporting new technologies that can provide competitive advantages of the economy (such as clean technologies, medicine, industrial design, agrobiology)

- Modernising the system of vocational qualifications and ensuring re-training for employees of restructured companies.

- Modernization of agri-food sector and diversification of employment in rural areas, use of internal resources enabling a unique specialization of regions.

- Improving the quality of food and improving food security by supporting integrated, organic, traditional and regional food production methods using local raw materials.

- Introduction and ensuring spatial order at the national and local level.

- Introducing an integrated system of state security, rescue and civil protection (including the improvement of the crisis management system).

141

[[END OF PAGE 141]]

[[START OF PAGE 142]]

- Increasing the efficiency of activities in the area of environmental protection, including the creation of a system for adaptation to climate change and for protection of the air against pollution, flood risk management.

- Introducing the natural resources management system (including strategic mineral resources, groundwater resources).

## Investment tasks - hard investments

- Road infrastructure - high quality road connections between voivodeship capitals and Warsaw, construction of bypasses, construction and modernization of local connections (access to labour markets).

- Railway infrastructure - building a network of high standard connections between the major economic and social centres, modernization of rail connections providing access to the labour markets.

- Development of energy infrastructure projects (transmission lines modernization, transmission infrastructure development, development of selected product pipelines, Polish nuclear power programme implementation, increasing the share of RES).

- Providing access to broadband.

- Measures to minimize the risk of flooding

- Actions in the field of rational waste management

- Improving the health care infrastructure and the teaching facilities of medical schools and research institutes.

## Investment tasks - soft investments

- Implementation of comprehensive solutions for a family policy (flexible forms of employment, different forms of child care, housing, etc.).

- Implementation of solutions arising from the adverse demographic trends and an increase in retirement age on the labour market and in health care.

- Changes in the education system and higher education conducive to improving the quality of teaching and building a knowledge based society.

- Actions in the field of public health (precisely targeted prevention, information and popularization campaigns) covering lifestyle diseases

- Strengthening the social capital at the local level by raising awareness of the benefits of cooperation and shaping pro-social and pro-entrepreneurial attitudes.
