# INDICATORS DEFINITIONS

## KEY INDICATORS

### Ad 1) GDP growth rate (2010 = 100, constant prices)

The indicator shows the rate of growth of GDP in the base year 2010. (Source: CSO).

### Ad 2) GDP per capita in PPS (EU27 = 100)

The value of gross domestic product per capita presented as a ratio of GDP per capita for Poland against the average level of GDP per capita in 27 European Union countries. GDP calculated by purchasing power parity  (PPP)  and  expressed  in  the  contractual  currency  unit  PPS  (Purchasing  Power  Standard)  (Source: Calculations based on national data are performed by Eurostat).

### Ad 3) Gross disposable income of households in total (2010 = 100)

The  indicator  shows  the  percentage  increase  in  real  income  relative  to  the  base  year  2010  (Source CSO).shows the rate of growth of GDP in the base year 2010. (Source: CSO).

### Ad 4) Public balance (%)

The difference between revenues and expenditure of the general government sector in structural terms, i.e.  after  correction  by  the  impact  of  business  cycle,  temporary  and  one-off  activities.  The  data  are presented in relation to GDP and compiled in accordance with the principles of the European System of National Accounts (ESA95). ESA95 is an international methodology and accounting standard for a detailed description  of  the  economy  (i.e.  regions,  countries  or  group  of  countries),  its  components  and  relations with other economies (Source: MF).

### Ad 5) Human development index (HDI) / / Ranking

A  distance  indicator,  whose  components  include  life  expectancy  at  birth,  mean  years  of  schooling  (for adults), expected years of schooling, net national income per capita (according to the methodology of PPP, in USD) (source: UNDP).

### Ad 6) Global Competitiveness Index / / Ranking

An indicator of the capacity of individual countries for long-term economic growth, developed based on 12 interrelated  pillars  (institutions,  infrastructure,  macroeconomy,  higher  education  and  training,  goods market efficiency, health and primary education, labour market efficiency, financial markets development, technological readiness, market size, business sophistication, innovation) (source: WEF - World Economic Forum).

### Ad 7) Employment rate for persons aged 20-64 (%)

The share of the employed aged 20-64 in the total population of the same age group. The data presented are based on the Labour Force Survey (LFS), as an average per year. The study concerns the population living in private households, excluding residents of institutional households (for example, workers' hostels, student hostels, nursing homes).

The working population = total number of people who performed any work for at least one hour in the week when the survey was carried out, for which they received remuneration and the people who helped

147

[[END OF PAGE 147]]

[[START OF PAGE 148]]

(without pay) in conducting a family farm or a non-agricultural family business. It also includes people who have not worked, but had a job. (Source: CSO)

### Ad 8) Average investment rate (%)

The ratio of gross fixed capital formation to GDP (source: CSO)

## SPECIFIC INDICATORS

### I. Effective and efficient state

#### 1.1 Transition from administration to management

##### Ad 1, Ad 2) Government effectiveness (according to the World Bank); Regulatory quality (according to the methodology of the World Bank)

The World Bank adopts six indicators of good governance: Voice and Accountability, Political Stability and Absence of Violence, Government Effectiveness, Regulatory Quality, Rule of Law, Control of Corruption. The scale of indicators: from -2.5 to +2.5.

##### Ad 3) Position in the ranking of global competitiveness in terms of regulatory burden

See item 6 under the Key Indicators. The regulatory burden index is a part of the "institutions" pillar - the first of the 12 pillars of the Global Competitiveness ranking. (Source: WEF).

##### Ad  4)  Use  of  the  Internet  for  interaction  between  public  authorities  and  citizens  /  /  including  for returning completed forms (%)

The  percentage  of  individuals  aged  16-74  using  the  Internet  to  interact  with  public  administrations  for private purposes within the 12 months preceding the survey (source: Eurostat)

#### 1.2 Ensuring funds for development

##### Ad 1) The share of public expenditure on development-oriented goals in GDP (%)

The pro-development expenditure includes (COFOG classification):

- basic research and R&D in all categories,

- category  of  economic  affairs:  part  of  agriculture,  forestry,  fishing  and  hunting,  transport, telecommunications,

- category of environmental protection: waste management, pollution reduction

- category of recreation, culture and religion: cultural services.

- category of housing and utilities: water supply,

The indicator shows the share of expenditure in GDP (%) (Source: IBS calculations based on CSO data)

#### 1.3 Strengthening the conditions conducive for citizens' interests and activities

##### Ad 1) Adults level of interpersonal trust (%)

148

[[END OF PAGE 148]]

[[START OF PAGE 149]]

The percentage of people aged 18 and over stating that other people can be trusted (Source: CBOS).

##### Ad 2) Average duration of debt collection through the courts (in days)

One of the components of the factors affecting the ease of doing business index (in days) specified in the Doing Business report. The report includes indicators presenting the quantitative assessment of regulations on  starting  a  business,  obtaining  construction  permits,  employing  workers,  registering  property,  getting credit,  protecting  investors,  paying  taxes,  trading  across  borders,  enforcing  contracts,  getting  electricity and the closure of business.  (Source: Doing Business Report, World Bank).

##### Ad 3) Trust in public institutions (%)

The  percentage  of  people  that  answered  'I  rather  do'  or  'I  definitely  do'  to  the  question:  'Do  you,  in general, trust or not trust the listed institutions?' (Source: CBOS, report: Public trust).

##### Ad 4) Percentage of the population declaring activity in social organisations (%)

The percentage of people who - in the past 12 months - have spent their free time in voluntary and unpaid work  in  a  non-governmental  organization  (association  or  foundation),  institution,  church  or  other organization  (Source:  CBOS,  Social  activity  of  Poles  -  the  level  of  commitment  and  motivation,  Warsaw, May 2011).

##### Ad 5) The share of area covered by the current spatial development plan in the total country geodesic area (%)

The area of the country covered by the current spatial development plan in the total country geodesic area (Source: own calculations based on CSO data).

##### Ad 6) The percentage of people who feel safe in the place they live (%)

The percentage of people who believe that their place of living is safe and quiet (Source: CBOS, Sense of security, the threat of crime and attitudes towards the death penalty, Warsaw, May 2011).

### II. Competitive economy

#### 2.1 Macroeconomic stability strengthening

##### Ad 1) General government debt (ESA '95) (%)

Consolidated gross debt of general government. The data are presented in relation to GDP and compiled in accordance with the principles of the European System of National Accounts - ESA95 (source: CSO).

##### Ad 2) Share of exports in GDP (%)

Exports of goods and services in relation to GDP in current prices (source: CSO).

#### 2.2. Increasing the economy productivity

##### Ad 1) Gross value added (GVA) per employee in PPS (EU27 = 100)

149

[[END OF PAGE 149]]

[[START OF PAGE 150]]

The value of products (goods and services) produced by national market and non-market entities, net of intermediate  consumption  in  connection  with  its  production,  expressed  in  PPS  (Purchasing  Power Standard), per one employee, compared to the EU27 average (Source: calculations based on CSO Eurostat data).

##### Ad 2) Structure of employed persons by sector of the economy (sector I / II sector / sector III) (%)

It specifies the number of employees in individual sector of the economy in the total number of the employed. The indicators were developed based on the results LFS sample survey.

Sector I - agriculture, forestry, hunting and fishing,

Sector II - industry and construction,

Sector III - services.

(Source: CSO)

##### Ad 3) Share of fields of high and medium-high technology in value of sold production of products (%)

Share of fields of high and medium-high technology in value of sold production of products  - in manufacturing enterprises employing over 49 people (source: CSO).

#### 2.3 Increasing innovation in the economy

##### Ad 1) European Innovation Ranking

An instrument of the European Commission for measuring and comparing EU Member States in terms of innovation and identifying strengths and weaknesses of the research and innovation sector in the countries based on 25 indicators. The rating distinguishes between  modest innovators (less than 50% of the EU27 average), moderate innovators (member with an index in the range between 50% below the EU27 average and 10% below the EU27 indicator), a group catching up with the leaders (innovation followers: members with an index in the range between 10% below the EU27 average and 20% above the EU27 average), and leaders of innovation (innovation leaders: 20% or more above the EU27 average) (Source: Union innovation Scoreboard: http://www.proinno-europe.eu/inno-metrics/page/innovation-union-scoreboard2010).

##### Ad 2) Expenditure on R & D (GERD) (%)

Expenditure on R & D (including public) in relation to GDP ( Gross Domestic Expenditure on Research and Development, GERD )  -  total research and development expenditure in the given country and in a specific unit of time (source: CSO).

##### Ad 3) Business Enterprise Expenditure on R&D ( BERD) (%)

Expenditure of the enterprises sector on R & D in relation to GDP. This includes funds that finance research and development, whose equity investors are enterprises (source: Eurostat).

##### Ad 4) Share of students at technical and natural science faculties in the total number of students (%)

It  concerns faculties classified as "science" and "technology, industry, construction"; excluding foreigners (source: CSO).

150

[[END OF PAGE 150]]

[[START OF PAGE 151]]

#### 2.4 Human capital development

##### Ad 1) Employment rate for persons aged 15-24 / / 55-64 (%)

For people aged 15-24 years - the share of the employed aged 15-24 in the total population of the same age group. The data presented are based on Labour Force Survey (LFS), as an average per year. For people aged 15-24 years - the share of the employed aged 15-24 in the total population of the same age group. The data presented are based on Labour Force Survey (LFS), as an average per year (source: CSO).

##### Ad 2) NEET

The percentage of people aged 25-29 years that are neither in employment nor in education or training (within 4 weeks preceding the survey) in the total population of the same age group (source: Eurostat).

##### Ad 3) Employment rate of women whose youngest child is aged up to 5 years (%)

Share  of  working  women  aged  15-64  whose  youngest  child's  age  did  not  exceed  5  years  in  the  total number of women in the given age group (Source: LFS, Eurostat).

##### Ad 4) Percentage of children under the age of 3 years in formal child care (%)

The share of children under the age of 3 years that are enrolled in child care institutions for children aged up to 3 in the total number of children in that age group (in %).

##### Ad  5)  Results  of  the  PISA  test -  percentage  of  students  below Level  2 in  three  areas:  reading  (C), mathematics (M) and science (P)

The results of competence tests of students aged 15 years - the percentage of students below Level 2 in three areas - reading, mathematics and science.

PISA stands for Programme for International Student Assessment -  an  international study coordinated by the OECD. Its purpose is to provide comparable data about the skills of students who completed 15 years of age in order to improve the quality of teaching and organization of education systems. Seven levels are used in student assessment - the best students achieve level 5 or 6, while students having only very basic skills and knowledge achieve level 1a or 1b (source: PISA, OECD).

##### Ad  5a) Results  of  the PISA  test -  percentage  of  students  over Level  4 in  three  areas:  reading  (C) , mathematics (M) and science (P )

The results of competence tests of students aged 15 years - the percentage of students at 5 and 6 levels in three areas - science (P), mathematics (M) and Reading(C).

PISA stands for Programme for International Student Assessment - an international study coordinated by the OECD. Its purpose is to provide comparable data about the skills of students who completed 15 years of age in order to improve the quality of teaching and organization of education systems. Seven levels are used in student assessment - the best students achieve level 5 or 6, while students having only very basic skills and knowledge achieve level 1a or 1b (source: PISA, OECD).

##### Ad 6) Early Childhood Education ratio (%) total / / urban / / rural
The percentage of children covered by preschool education from 4 years of age to the age of compulsory schooling.  The  preschool  education  institutions  offer  education-oriented  child  care.  They  may  include schools  or  institutions  managed  by  other  entities  than  those  responsible  for  education.  They  have  to employ staff with specialist qualifications (source: CSO).

151

[[END OF PAGE 151]]

[[START OF PAGE 152]]

##### Ad 7) Share of 30-34 year olds with a tertiary or equivalent degree (%) total / / urban / / rural

The share of persons aged 30-34 with a tertiary or equivalent degree in the total population of the same age group. The data presented are based on Labour Force Survey (LFS), average per year. Tertiary degree is equivalent  to  5 th and  6 th level  of  the  International  Standard  Classification  of  Education  (International Standard Classification of Education, in short ISCED) (source: CSO).

##### Ad 8) Adults in education and training (in %)

The share of persons aged 25-64 years in education and training in the total population of the same age group (within four weeks before the survey), data are presented based on the Labour Force Survey (LFS), average per year (source: CSO).

#### 2.5. Ensuring the use of digital technologies in the economy

##### Ad 1) Percentage of individuals using the Internet regularly (%) total / / urban / / rural

Percentage of people aged 16-74 using the Internet regularly, for example at least once a week (source: CSO).

##### Ad 2)  Households having access to the broadband Internet in the total number of households  (%)

Broadband connections - the type of Internet connection characterized by a high speed information flow, measured in kb/s (kilobits per second) or Mbit s (megabits per second). Due to rapid technological progress in the field of telecommunications, determination of the throughput limit (digital bandwidth), starting from which the connection is considered broadband, is likely to become obsolete soon after the adoption of the definition.  Therefore,  the  Community  research  on  ICT  defines  broadband  based  on  the  type  of  Internet connection. According to this definition, broadband access is enabled by the DSL technologies (ADSL, SDSL, etc.),  cable  network  (cable  modem),  3G  mobile  phones  (UMTS,  EDGE,  etc.)  and  other,  such  as  satellite links,  fixed  wireless  connections  (radio  network).  Broadband  allows  to  transmit  high-quality  images, movies, watching TV or playing games online, telephoning over the Internet with the possibility to watch the caller and to use a variety of advanced Internet services (source: CSO).

##### Ad 3) Digital literacy level (percentage of respondents who declared they have carried out 5 or 6 of the specific Internet related activities) (%)

The percentage of respondents who declared that they have carried out 5 or 6 of the specific Internetrelated activities: use of search engines, sending e-mails with attachments, using chat rooms, forums and discussion  groups,  making  calls  over  the  Internet,  sharing  files  via  peer-to-peer  and  web  development (source: Eurostat).

#### 2.6 Energy security and the environment

##### Ad 1) Aggregate energy efficiency index (ODEX) (2000 = 100)

152

[[END OF PAGE 152]]

[[START OF PAGE 153]]

ODEX is used to measure the increase in energy efficiency in the manufacturing, transport and households sector; it does not show the current level of energy intensity, but the improvement against the base year. It is  calculated  as  a  weighted  average  of  the  relative  decrease  (increase)  of  unit  energy  consumption  in various sectors or areas of use in subsequent years. A decrease in value of the indicator means an increase in energy efficiency (for example, if the energy efficiency ratio in 2009 was 76.0%, this means that energy efficiency has improved by 24% compared to energy technologies and practices used in 2000) (source: CSO / Odyssee database).

##### Ad 2) FBI - Farmland Bird Index (2000 = 100)

The index of the population of a selected group of common breeding birds, characteristic for agricultural landscape.  It  has  been  created  by  averaging  out  the  information  about  the  population  indexes  of  22 species (base year 2000 = 100) (Source: CIEP / CSO).

##### Ad 3) Energy intensity of the economy (kgoe/1000 €)

The relation of gross national energy consumption to gross domestic product at constant prices from the base year (2000). This indicator measures the amount of energy needed to produce one unit of GDP and is given in kilograms of oil equivalent per 1,000 euro. A decrease in energy intensity means that less energy is needed to produce the same unit of GDP and it is related to increased energy efficiency. This indicator does not reflect the actual gap between the energy efficiency of the Polish  and the EU economy due to differences in purchasing power, which means for example that the price levels of market or non-market goods  and  services  vary  (purchasing  power  of  euro  in  Poland  is  higher  than  the  EU  average).  The differences in the energy intensity of Poland and the EU at the physical level (e.g. energy consumption per production of a tonne of product) are much smaller (source: Eurostat).

##### Ad 4) Share of renewable energy in gross final energy consumption (%)

The share of renewable energy is calculated as the value of gross final energy consumption from renewable sources divided by the gross final energy consumption from all sources (%) (Source: CSO).

##### Ad 5) Change in greenhouse gas emissions compared to 1990 (%)

The change in aggregate emissions of six greenhouse gases (carbon dioxide, methane, nitrous oxide, hydrofluoro-hydrocarbons, perfluoro-hydrocarbon, sulphur hexafluoride), weighted by global warming potentials with the base of 1990 = 100 (source: KOBiZE / CSO).

##### Ad 6)  Reduction  of  biodegradable  municipal  waste  going  to  landfill  in  the  total  amount  by  weight  of BMW produced in 1995 (%)

Pursuant to the Act of 13 September 1996 on maintaining cleanliness and order in gminas, biodegradable municipal waste is waste generated by households, excluding the vehicles out of service, and waste that does not contain hazardous waste from other waste generators, which due to its nature or composition is similar  to  household  waste,  degradable  by  aerobic  or  anaerobic  microorganisms.  Landfills  are  facilities intended for waste storage. The method for calculating this indicator will be specified in the Regulation of the  Minister  of  Environment on  the  levels  of  reduction  of  the  weight  of  biodegradable  municipal  waste going  to  landfill  and  the  method  of  calculating  the  level  of  waste  weight  reduction (Source:  Ministry  of Environment).

##### Ad 7) Water quality indicator (%)

The  share  of  surface  water  according  to  the  division  of  surface  water  into  ecological  quality  classes pursuant to the Regulation of the Minister of Environment of 20 August 2008 on the classification of the condition of surface water bodies.

153

[[END OF PAGE 153]]

[[START OF PAGE 154]]

Class I - very good ecological status, Class II - good ecological status, class III - moderate ecological status, Class IV - poor ecological status, Class V - bad ecological status. (Source: CSO)

#### 2.7. Increasing the efficiency of transport

##### Ad 1) Inter-branch Transport Accessibility Indicator - ITAI

The  index  is  a  synthetic  qualitative  measure  of  the  potential  time-availability  of the  most  important national settlement units and transport nodes (internal and border) taking into account all the basic modes of  transport  (proportionally  to  their  share  in  transport  volume)  and  weighted  with  the  level  of  socioeconomic  development.  The  measure  can  be  calculated  separately  for  the  transport  of  goods  and passengers,  and  divided  into  16  voivodeships  and  four  types  of  transport  (road,  rail,  air  and  inland waterways). The indicator ranges from 0 to 1 (Source: The Academy of Sciences for MRR).

##### Ad2) Motorways (A) and expressways (D) ( km)

( Source: CSO on the basis of GDNRM data)

##### Ad 3) Length of railway lines allowing for movement of passenger trains at speeds above 160km / h (in km)

(Source: MI)

##### Ad 4) Volume of goods in intermodal transport in the total load transported by rail (%)

(Based on Glossary for Transport Statistics- Eurostat, EKGON2, ITF): Intermodal transport is the movement of  goods  (in  one  and  the  same  unit  of  intermodal  transport)  by  successive  modes  of  transport  without handling of the goods themselves when changing modes. Intermodal transport unit may be - for example a container, swap body, a road vehicle (source: CSO).

[[START OF PAGE 155]]

### III. Social and territorial cohesion

#### 3.1. Social integration

##### Ad 1) Risk of poverty or social exclusion (aggregate of three sub-indicators) (%)

The percentage of people who are at risk of poverty and/or deeper material deprivation and/ or live in households  with  very  low  labour  intensity.  The  persons  are  counted  only  once,  even  if  they  are  under several indicators (source: Eurostat).

##### Ad 2) Early leavers from education and training (%)

The  share  of  persons  aged  18-24  with  at  most  lower  secondary  education  who  are  not  in  education  or training  in  the  total  population  of  the  same  age  group.  The  data  presented  are  based  on  Labour  Force Survey (LFS), average per year (source: CSO).

##### Ad 3) Employment rate of people with disabilities aged 16-64 (%)

Share of workers with disabilities aged 16-64 in the total population of people with disabilities of the same age group. The data presented are based on Labour Force Survey (LFS), average per year (source: CSO).

#### 3.2. Providing access and certain standards of public services

At this stage, there is no synthetic indicator of public services - the access to services will be measured by public opinion polls once in two years. In the meantime, a synthetic indicator will be developed.

#### 3.3 Strengthening the diffusion mechanisms and spatial integration in order to develop and make a full use of the regional potentials

##### Ad 1) Share of Eastern Poland voivodeships in GDP (%)

The  total  share  of  Eastern  Poland  voivodeships,  which  includes  the  following  voivodeships:  Lubelskie, Podkarpackie,  Podlaskie,  Świętokrzyskie,  Warmińsko-Mazurskie,  in  generating  the  national  GDP  (source: own calculations based on CSO data).

##### Ad 2) Population living within  the 60-minute road isochrones from voivodeship cities (%)

The percentage of the population whose time of getting to the centre of the voivodeship capital is less than 60 minutes (Source: Institute of Geography and Spatial Organization, Academy of Sciences).

##### Ad 3) Non-farm income in rural households (%)

The total share of "income from hired work" and "income from self-employment" categories in the category of "disposable income (rural areas)" (source: CSO).
